# YeffoPrint — Architecture

Companion to `PROJECT_SPEC.md`. This file explains *how* the system is structured, not *what* it should do. Keep it current as implementation decisions are made — future Claude Code sessions should be able to onboard from this file alone.

## 1. Two-Codebase Split

```
yeffoprint/          # block theme — presentation only
yeffoprint-core/      # plugin — business logic, data, pricing, orders
```

**Rule of thumb:** if it would still need to exist under a different theme, it belongs in `yeffoprint-core`. If it's purely how things look/lay out, it belongs in `yeffoprint`.

### `yeffoprint` (theme) owns
- `theme.json` design tokens (color, spacing, radius, typography)
- Block templates and template parts (FSE)
- Reusable block patterns (hero, featured designs, materials grid, etc.)
- Header/footer/announcement bar presentation
- WooCommerce template overrides for presentation only (product cards, cart drawer markup, checkout layout)
- Configurator **UI** (rendering, styling, preview surfaces) — consumes state/data from plugin-provided APIs, does not own pricing or schema logic
- Responsive/accessibility CSS and JS behavior for presentation components

### `yeffoprint-core` (plugin) owns
- Custom post types / data records: Templates, Materials, Sizes, Pricing Rules, Custom Orders, Proofs
- Template field-schema engine (generic, not compound/strength-specific)
- Pricing engine (base price, surcharges, bulk discount tiers, server-side authoritative calculation)
- Batch/variant data model and validation
- Cart/order metadata structure (how customization state is serialized and reconstructed)
- Custom order + proof workflow and status model
- REST/AJAX endpoints the configurator calls (fetch template schema, live price calc, submit custom order, etc.)
- Admin UI under a top-level **YeffoPrint** menu (Templates, Materials, Sizes, Pricing Rules, Custom Orders, Proofs, Site Settings)
- Seed/demo data tooling (dev-only, never auto-overwrites production content)
- Future: saved designs, reorder, rewards, sticker infrastructure

**Never:** business logic (pricing math, schema validation, order state transitions) inside the theme. Never core WordPress/WooCommerce file modification.

## 2. Core Data Model

```
Template
 ├─ id, name, active/inactive, featured, popularity/sort
 ├─ artwork, gallery imagery, vial mockup imagery
 ├─ tags: searchable / color / style / material-compatibility
 ├─ field_schema[]            # generic — see below
 └─ compatible_sizes[], compatible_materials[]

Field (within a Template's field_schema)
 ├─ id, label, type, default, required
 ├─ max_chars, position, alignment
 ├─ font_size_max, font_size_min, formatting_rules
 └─ admin_description, preview_behavior

Size (admin-managed record)
 ├─ id, name, active/inactive
 ├─ print_dimensions
 └─ price_adjustment

Material (admin-managed record)
 ├─ id, name, description, swatch/image, active/inactive
 ├─ price_adjustment
 ├─ compatible_products
 └─ sort_order

PricingRule
 ├─ base_unit_price
 ├─ size_surcharges[], material_surcharges[]
 ├─ bulk_discount_tiers[]   # threshold, type(percent|fixed_unit_price)
 └─ custom_design_fee

Batch (a single line item's shared config)
 ├─ template_id + version, size_id, material_id
 ├─ total_quantity
 ├─ pricing_snapshot            # rule version + computed breakdown
 └─ variants[]

Variant (one personalized label within a Batch)
 ├─ id, quantity
 ├─ field_values{}              # keyed by Field.id, per the template's schema
 └─ preview_state

CustomOrder (Fully Custom Design workflow — separate from Batch/Variant)
 ├─ size, material, quantity
 ├─ compound(s)/strength(s), brand name, style notes, instructions
 ├─ inspiration_uploads[]
 ├─ design_fee (fixed, $25 at launch)
 ├─ status  # Design in progress → Proof ready → Awaiting approval → Approved → Printing → Shipped
 └─ proof_history[]             # future portal: uploads, approvals, timestamps
```

**Design principle:** the schema is generic over field types — nothing in the data model or pricing engine should assume "compound" or "strength" specifically. Compound/strength are just two Fields defined on a given Template's schema.

## 3. Pricing Flow

1. Client (configurator JS) computes a **provisional** price locally from cached pricing-rule data for instant feedback.
2. On every state change that affects price, and always before Add to Cart / checkout, the client calls a plugin REST endpoint that recalculates from the authoritative `PricingRule` data server-side.
3. The server-computed value is what's stored on the cart item and order — client-submitted totals are never trusted.
4. Bulk discount tier is evaluated against the **batch's combined quantity** across all variants, not per-variant.

## 4. Preview Architecture

- Single source of truth: the active Batch/Variant state (JS state object, mirrored to a hidden form/cart payload).
- Label View and Vial View are two renderers subscribing to the same state — never separate data paths. Prefer SVG/structured HTML+CSS for Label View so text stays crisp and font-scaling can be computed precisely (measure against `font_size_min`/`max` before falling back to the overflow warning).

## 5. Cart & Order Metadata

Cart line items store structured metadata (not a single serialized blob), sufficient to:
- Re-render the exact configurator state for "Edit customization"
- Reconstruct the order for a future "Reorder" action
- Preserve which `PricingRule` version was active at time of purchase (so historical orders remain accurate even after prices change later)

## 6. Proof / Custom Order States

Modeled as a dedicated status field on `CustomOrder`, independent of WooCommerce's native order status, since the production/proof lifecycle (design → proof → approval → printing) doesn't map cleanly onto WooCommerce's processing/completed states. WooCommerce order status still drives payment/fulfillment; `CustomOrder.status` drives the production workflow and is surfaced to the customer and admin separately.

## 7. Key WordPress/WooCommerce Integration Points

- WooCommerce: product/catalog infra, cart, checkout, customer accounts, orders, WooPayments, shipping — used natively wherever it models the need adequately.
- Product-level customization (fields, batches, variants, pricing) is layered on top via `yeffoprint-core`, not via WooCommerce Product Variations (avoids the template × material × size variation explosion the spec explicitly rules out).
- REST/AJAX endpoints exposed by the plugin for: template schema fetch, live price calc, cart add w/ structured metadata, custom order submission + uploads.

## 8. Extensibility Points (built now, UI later)

- `CustomOrder` proof fields already shaped for a future customer-facing proof portal.
- Batch/Variant model already sufficient for a future "Reorder" (rehydrate into configurator) and "Saved Designs" (persist a Batch to a user without purchasing).
- Pricing/upload/option infrastructure intentionally generic so Custom Stickers (Phase 2) can reuse it rather than needing a parallel system.

## 9. Open Architectural Decisions

*(Log decisions here as they're made during implementation, with brief rationale — keep entries short.)*

- **Data records as CPTs (Phase 1):** Template, Material, Size, Pricing Rule, Custom Order, and Proof are implemented as custom post types (`yp_template`, `yp_material`, `yp_size`, `yp_pricing_rule`, `yp_custom_order`, `yp_proof`) with structured meta rather than custom DB tables. Rationale: native wp-admin list-table/CRUD UI for free, works with REST out of the box, no migration tooling needed at this stage. Revisit only if query patterns demand custom tables later.
- **Plugin bootstrap pattern:** single root plugin file (`yeffoprint-core.php`) that requires a `YeffoPrint_Core` singleton in `includes/class-yeffoprint-core.php`, which in turn loads one class per post type under `includes/post-types/`. Keeps registration declarative and easy for future phases (pricing engine, REST endpoints, admin screens) to hook into the same singleton without touching the bootstrap file.
- **Theme scaffolding (Phase 1):** minimal valid FSE block theme (`style.css` header + `theme.json` + empty `templates/index.html` + `parts/header.html`/`footer.html`) with design tokens (color palette, radius scale, typography, spacing) from PROJECT_SPEC §5 encoded directly in `theme.json`. No patterns or WooCommerce template overrides yet — those start in Phase 2/3.
- **Global UI tokens (Phase 2):** added shadow (`shadow-elevation.low/medium/high`), glassmorphism (`glass.background/border/blur`), and `focus-ring` tokens under `theme.json` `settings.custom`, plus a `spacingSizes` scale mirroring the existing `spacing-scale` custom tokens so the block editor's own spacing UI offers the same values. Button element styling (radius, padding, hover) set via `styles.elements.button` rather than a class, so it applies to every core Button block automatically.
- **Component CSS lives in one file (Phase 2):** `assets/css/global.css` holds all cross-page component styles (header, drawer primitive, cards, forms, footer, a11y utilities) enqueued via `wp_enqueue_scripts`. Section/page-specific styling will move into block patterns as they're built (Phase 3+); this file stays scoped to reusable chrome so it doesn't grow into a dumping ground.
- **Drawer primitive, not a cart-specific component (Phase 2):** built one generic accessible drawer (`.yp-drawer`, open/close/focus-trap/ESC in `assets/js/site.js`) used now for the mobile search overlay and stubbed for the cart. The Phase 7 cart drawer will populate `#yp-cart-drawer`'s body with real line-item markup (and swap in WooCommerce's cart-fragments data) rather than introducing a second drawer mechanism.
- **Mobile nav uses core Navigation block's built-in overlay** (`overlayMenu: "mobile"`) instead of hand-rolled hamburger JS — avoids extra JS for something WordPress core already handles accessibly, in line with the "minimal third-party dependencies" priority.
- **Account icon uses the WooCommerce `customer-account` block** (`icon_only` display) rather than custom account-menu markup, since Accounts (PROJECT_SPEC §16) isn't built until Phase 9 — the block already links to the native My Account page in the interim with no custom code required.
- **Shop Labels is the `yp_template` archive (Phase 3):** rather than a hand-built page template with a manual `WP_Query`, the Template CPT now registers `has_archive => 'shop-labels'` with a matching rewrite slug, so `/shop-labels/` *is* `templates/archive-yp_template.html`. Filters (Style/Color/Material) and sort both work by extending that one native archive query — see the next two entries — instead of introducing a parallel query layer.
- **Filters use native taxonomy query vars, sort uses `pre_get_posts` (Phase 3):** `yp_style`/`yp_color`/`yp_material_tag` are registered with matching `query_var`s, so `?yp_style=bold` filters the archive with zero custom code — WordPress folds it into the main query automatically. Sort (`?sort=featured|newest|popularity`) is resolved in `YeffoPrint_Template_Query::apply_shop_labels_sort()` (plugin, since it's query/business logic) via `pre_get_posts`. Both are plain GET requests with a working non-JS fallback, matching PROJECT_SPEC §6's "richer JS is justified [only in] the configurator."
- **Predictive search extends core search rather than replacing it (Phase 3):** on every Template save, `YeffoPrint_Template_Search` flattens title + style/color/material term names into a `_yp_search_index` post meta value, then splices an `OR (yp_search_index.meta_value LIKE ...)` into core's own `posts_search` SQL fragment (scoped to `yp_template` searches only). The same extended search backs both the classic `s=` query and the REST `?search=` param the header's predictive dropdown (`assets/js/search.js`) calls — one search implementation, two entry points.
- **Card presentation is a theme-owned dynamic block, not a Query Loop of core blocks (Phase 3):** `yeffoprint/template-card` (script-less, `render.php` only, per the WP 6.4 "render" block.json field) is what actually needs per-post plugin data — the vial-mockup hover-swap image and badge can't be expressed as static core block markup inside a Query Loop's Post Template. The plugin exposes that data through one template tag, `yeffoprint_core_get_template_card_data()` (`includes/api/template-api.php`), so the block never reads `_yp_*` meta keys or plugin class constants directly — it stays a presentation consumer of a plugin API, per the Architecture §1 split. `yeffoprint/gallery-toolbar` (the sort/filter bar) is the same pattern, server-rendered because it needs live `$_GET` state and term counts that static markup can't provide.
- **Starting price is a plugin template tag, not a literal string (Phase 3):** `yeffoprint_core_starting_price_label()` (`includes/pricing/class-pricing-placeholder.php`) wraps the `$0.35` base price constant so the theme card never hard-codes a price string, even though the real PricingRule-driven calculation doesn't exist until Phase 6. That file is deleted once Phase 6 lands.
- **Template meta (featured/popularity/badge/vial mockup) has no admin UI yet (Phase 3):** registered via `register_post_meta` with `show_in_rest` so it's settable through the REST API, WP-CLI, or the block editor's Custom Fields panel, but no meta box was built — Phase 4 ("Template Management") owns the admin CRUD experience and will replace this with proper controls. `wp yeffoprint seed` (dev-only WP-CLI command, idempotent by slug) seeds demo Templates so the gallery/filters/sort/search are actually exercisable before Phase 4 lands.
- **field_schema is a single JSON meta value, not per-key `register_post_meta` fields (Phase 4):** each Template's `field_schema` (array of field definitions — id/label/type/default/required/max_chars/position/alignment/font sizing/formatting rule/preview behavior/admin description) is stored as one JSON-encoded string in `_yp_field_schema`, validated by `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::sanitize()` — not exposed via `register_post_meta`'s REST array schema. Reasoning: a REST-typed nested array-of-objects schema buys nothing yet, since nothing public reads it until Phase 5's schema-fetch endpoint, which can shape the JSON however the configurator actually needs it. The admin editor reads/writes it through the classic nonce-verified meta box POST flow, same as `compatible_sizes`/`compatible_materials` (also plain `get/update_post_meta`, same rationale).
- **Field-schema repeater reorders via Move Up/Down buttons, not drag-and-drop (Phase 4):** `assets/admin/field-schema.js` keeps the whole repeater keyboard-operable (PROJECT_SPEC §6 WCAG 2.2 AA target) rather than requiring pointer-based HTML5 drag-and-drop, which needs significant extra ARIA work to be accessible. Same vanilla-JS, no-build-step approach as the theme's JS — state lives in a plain in-memory array, every mutation re-renders and re-syncs one hidden JSON input that submits with the normal post form.
- **V1 field types are `text` and `textarea` only (Phase 4):** `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::TYPES` is a single constant map nothing else depends on the shape of — extending it later (e.g. a `select` type) touches one array, not the sanitize logic, the repeater JS, or the save flow.
- **Position is two percentage inputs (x/y, 0–100), not a visual click-to-place picker (Phase 4):** matches PROJECT_SPEC §10 ("no freeform/drag-drop placement in V1") — admin sets a fixed position per field, customers never move it. A visual picker on top of the actual artwork is Phase 5 territory (it needs the Label View preview surface to exist first), not a Phase 4 admin-CRUD concern.
- **Material/Size lean on native WordPress fields instead of custom meta (Phase 4):** active/inactive is `post_status` (publish/draft) rather than a redundant boolean meta flag; sort_order is native `menu_order` (via `page-attributes` support, which also gives a built-in drag-orderable admin list for free); Material's description is `post_content` (via `editor` support); the swatch image is the existing featured-image (`thumbnail` support, already registered in Phase 1). The only new meta is `price_adjustment` (both) and Size's `print_width_mm`/`print_height_mm`. `compatible_products` from the Architecture §2 Material model isn't duplicated on Material — `Template.compatible_materials` (set from the Template editor) is the single source of truth for that relationship, read from the Template side only, so the two can't drift out of sync.
- **The "template schema fetch" endpoint is public and read-only (Phase 5):** `GET /yeffoprint-core/v1/templates/{id}/configurator` (`class-template-schema-controller.php`) needs no auth (`permission_callback` is `__return_true`) because everything it returns is already public storefront data — a published Template plus its published, compatible Sizes/Materials. It shapes `field_schema`/sizes/materials/pricing into exactly what the configurator needs, rather than the theme assembling that from three separate `wp/v2` REST calls. `base_unit_price` is still the Phase 3 placeholder constant — Phase 6 is what makes this endpoint's pricing authoritative.
- **Quantity presets (25/50/100/250/500/1000) live in the plugin, not the theme (Phase 5):** `yeffoprint_core_quantity_presets()` (`includes/configurator/quantity-presets.php`) is a fixed business rule from PROJECT_SPEC §10, not an admin-configurable record like Sizes/Materials — but it's still returned through the schema-fetch endpoint rather than hard-coded in `configurator.js`, so there's one source of truth if it ever needs to change.
- **Label View and Vial View render the exact same field DOM (Phase 5):** per Architecture §4 ("never a separate data source"), `configurator.js` builds one set of absolutely-positioned field elements from `field_schema` and only swaps the background image + toggles a CSS class between views — Vial View isn't a second render path. Because there's no per-template "where does the label sit on this bottle" data yet, Vial View approximates it with a flat `scale(0.55)` on the same field positions rather than a calibrated placement region. Revisit if/when Vial View needs to look convincing rather than illustrative — likely a new admin-configurable placement field on Template, not a configurator rewrite.
- **Font auto-scaling measures against a shared box ratio, not a per-field box size (Phase 5):** the Field data model has `font_size_min/max` and `position` but no explicit box width/height, so `fitText()` in `configurator.js` measures every field against a box that's a fixed percentage of the stage's own rendered size (86% width; 32% height, multiline fields only) rather than a per-field dimension. Shrinks the field's font from `font_size_max` down to `font_size_min` in 1px steps until it fits (or doesn't — then it gets the `is-overflowing` outline and the shared "Text is too long for this design" banner, per PROJECT_SPEC §10). If per-field box sizing turns out to matter, it's an additive schema field, not a rework of the fitting algorithm.
- **Provisional pricing only, still no server validation (Phase 5):** the configurator's running total (`unitPrice() × totalQuantity()`) uses `base_unit_price` plus whatever Size/Material `price_adjustment` the customer picked — real values already in the REST payload — but has no bulk-discount tiers and is never sent anywhere the server would trust it. "Server always recalculates/validates authoritative price" (PROJECT_SPEC §12) is Phase 6.
- **"Add to Cart" is a visible stub, not a disabled button (Phase 5):** since WooCommerce cart integration is Phase 7, both Add to Cart buttons (desktop summary panel + mobile sticky bar) are live and clickable but show an inline "Cart isn't connected yet" message rather than being permanently `disabled` — keeps the buttons testable/stylable now and is a one-line swap for the real cart-add call later.
- **`wp yeffoprint seed` now also creates the launch Sizes/Materials and wires each demo Template's compatible_sizes/compatible_materials/field_schema (Phase 5):** the Phase 3 version only seeded Templates, which left the configurator with nothing to render. Same idempotent-by-slug approach, extended to `yp_size`/`yp_material`.
- **PricingRule doesn't duplicate `size_surcharges[]`/`material_surcharges[]` (Phase 6):** even though docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §2 lists them there, that data already exists as `price_adjustment` on the Size/Material records themselves (Phase 4), admin-managed on the record they describe. `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule` only owns what doesn't belong anywhere else: `base_unit_price`, `bulk_discount_tiers[]`, and `custom_design_fee`. One source of truth per fact, same principle as the `compatible_materials` vs. `compatible_products` call in Phase 4.
- **Exactly one "active" PricingRule, not a versioned/selectable set (Phase 6):** `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::get_active_rule_id()` uses the most recently modified published `yp_pricing_rule` post, auto-creating a default (matching the old placeholder's $0.35 base / $25 design fee) on first use so the site always prices correctly. A nondev just edits the one entry under Pricing Rules; nothing stops creating a second published one, but there's no reason to and the UI doesn't invite it. A `_yp_rule_version` meta increments on every save specifically so Phase 7/8 order metadata can snapshot which version priced an order (Architecture §5), without building full CPT-revision-based versioning now.
- **A `fixed_unit_price` bulk-discount tier sets the resulting per-unit price, not a flat amount off (Phase 6):** reconciles the tier-type name with PROJECT_SPEC §12's literal formula `(base + adjustments − discounts) × quantity` — `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()` derives `discount_per_unit = (base+adjustments) − value` for that tier type, so the formula still holds structurally for either tier type (`percent` computes the discount as a share of the pre-discount price instead).
- **`/pricing/calculate` is template-agnostic (Phase 6):** takes `size_id`/`material_id`/`quantity`, not a template ID — size and material adjustments live on their own records and are shared by every variant in a batch (one template/size/material per batch, PROJECT_SPEC §10), so the calculation never needed template context. Public and read-only (no auth) since nothing it returns is sensitive and it has no side effects; Phase 8's Custom Order flow can call it unchanged rather than needing a parallel calculator.
- **The configurator shows an instant client estimate, then swaps in the authoritative breakdown (Phase 6):** `renderSummary()` in `configurator.js` renders the same provisional math as Phase 5 immediately (no network round-trip), then a 300ms-debounced call to `/pricing/calculate` replaces it with the real, discount-aware breakdown — implementing Architecture §3's two-stage pricing flow exactly. A monotonic request counter discards stale responses if the customer changes size/material/quantity again before the first call returns.
- **One WooCommerce simple product per Template, hidden from the shop (Phase 7):** resolves Architecture §7's "layered on top via yeffoprint-core, not via WooCommerce Product Variations" — one product per *Template*, not per template×size×material combination, avoiding the variation explosion PROJECT_SPEC §20 rules out. `YeffoPrint_Linked_Product` keeps it in sync on every Template save: `catalog_visibility` is `hidden` (customers only ever reach it through the configurator's Add to Cart, never WooCommerce's own shop/search), `manage_stock` is off with `stock_status` always `instock` (made-to-order, PROJECT_SPEC §15 — never "only X left"), and price is always `0` since the real price is computed live, never read from the product. Unpublishing a Template drafts its linked product; it's never hard-deleted, since past orders still reference it by ID.
- **One batch = one WooCommerce line item, quantity = the combined quantity across its variants (Phase 7):** matches PROJECT_SPEC §10 ("a batch shares one template/size/material") and Architecture §3.4 (discount tier evaluated against combined quantity) exactly — a batch never splits across multiple cart/order line items. The variant-level detail (each variant's own quantity and field values) lives inside that one line item's `yp_variants` data, not as separate lines.
- **Cart price is always live-recalculated, order price is a frozen snapshot (Phase 7):** `woocommerce_before_calculate_totals` re-derives every cart item's price from `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()` using its *current* stored size/material adjustments and combined quantity — never a price cached at add-to-cart time — so an item sitting in a cart always reflects current pricing (PROJECT_SPEC §12: "server always recalculates"). At checkout, `woocommerce_checkout_create_order_line_item` freezes that calculation, plus Template/Size/Material snapshots, onto the order line item — satisfying Architecture §5's "preserve which PricingRule version was active at time of purchase" without needing a formal Template version-number system (there isn't one yet; a JSON snapshot of the Template's title + field_schema at order time serves the same purpose more directly).
- **Order item data is several named meta keys, not one blob — except `_yp_variants`, deliberately (Phase 7):** `_yp_template_snapshot`, `_yp_size_snapshot`, `_yp_material_snapshot`, `_yp_pricing_snapshot`, and `_yp_batch_quantity` are separate meta rows, matching PROJECT_SPEC §14's "never one opaque serialized blob." `_yp_variants` is still one JSON array, because a batch's variant list is inherently one repeating structured unit — the same reasoning already applied to `field_schema` in Phase 4. All five are hidden from the default order-meta display via `woocommerce_hidden_order_itemmeta`; separate human-readable rows (Size, Material, Labels in this batch) are added alongside them for the admin order screen and customer emails.
- **`woocommerce_get_item_data` is the only item-data mechanism needed — it already reaches the Cart/Checkout blocks (Phase 7, corrected post-launch):** originally assumed to be classic-template-only and documented as a "known gap" requiring a future Store API `ExtendSchema` integration. That assumption was wrong: WooCommerce core's own `CartItemSchema::get_item_data()` (`StoreApi/Schemas/V1/CartItemSchema.php`) calls `apply_filters( 'woocommerce_get_item_data', [], $cart_item )` directly and feeds the result into the Store API's `item_data` field that the Cart/Checkout blocks render — the same hook `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::display_item_data()` already uses. Its only real restriction is that each value must be a scalar (non-scalar entries, i.e. anything with markup, are silently dropped), which our implementation already satisfies since it only ever emits plain-text key/value pairs. No integration work was needed; `assets/css/woocommerce.css`'s generic Blocks-class selectors already cover styling either page style renders. (A live cart inspected during debugging showed `item_data: []`, which looked like this gap — it turned out to be an unrelated cause: that cart line was a manually-added test product that never went through the configurator/`/cart/add` flow, so it had no batch data for `display_item_data()` to describe in the first place, classic or Blocks.)
- **The cart drawer is custom-rendered PHP (server-side), not the WooCommerce Mini Cart block (Phase 7):** PROJECT_SPEC §14 specifies exact drawer contents (template, thumbnail, size, material, quantity, price, Edit customization + Checkout/View Cart — nothing else, deliberately no inline remove/quantity controls) that would need deeper Store API/Blocks slot-fill work to reproduce precisely. Reuses the Phase 2 `.yp-drawer` primitive and `#yp-cart-drawer` shell instead: `GET /cart/drawer` and the `drawer_html` returned by `POST /cart/add` are server-rendered HTML fragments injected into that shell's body, keeping one drawer mechanism (not two) and exact control over markup — consistent with the classic-hooks choice above rather than mixing both WooCommerce integration paradigms.
- **"Edit customization" is remove-then-re-add, not in-place mutation (Phase 7):** a cart item's size/material can change on edit, so there's no clean way to mutate an existing WC cart item in place once its defining attributes change anyway. `configurator.js` reads `?edit=<cart_item_key>` from the URL, fetches that item's stored batch via `GET /cart/item/{key}`, and rehydrates the configurator from it; submitting sends `edit_key` back to `POST /cart/add`, which removes the old cart item before adding the new one — the standard, robust WooCommerce pattern for this, and avoids a second "update" endpoint.
- **A defensive `woocommerce_add_to_cart_validation` net, bypassed by a static flag (Phase 7):** `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::allow_next_add()` is a static bool set immediately before and cleared immediately after the cart controller's one legitimate `WC()->cart->add_to_cart()` call — anyone reaching WooCommerce's native add-to-cart directly for one of these hidden linked products without going through `/cart/add` gets rejected with a clear notice. A static flag (not a `$_REQUEST` marker) because the filter fires on *every* `add_to_cart()` call including our own internal one, and REST JSON bodies don't populate `$_REQUEST`.
- **Shipping/taxes/WooPayments are WooCommerce admin configuration, not code (Phase 7):** PROJECT_SPEC §15's flat rates (US: USPS $6 / UPS $15; International: $25) are ordinary Shipping Zones — `wp yeffoprint setup-shipping` (dev-only WP-CLI command, idempotent by zone/method-existence check, same pattern as `wp yeffoprint seed`) configures them so this doesn't have to be clicked through by hand on every fresh setup, but there's no custom shipping logic anywhere. Taxes stay at WooCommerce's default (disabled) — nothing needed there beyond not interfering with it. WooPayments is a separate extension a site owner installs and connects a merchant account to; no custom payment code was written, and none is needed — checkout works with whatever gateways WooCommerce has configured.
- **CustomOrder payment status and production status are two different fields, reusing the Material/Size `post_status` pattern (Phase 8):** a CustomOrder starts `post_status = draft` at submission (unpaid) and flips to `publish` only once `woocommerce_payment_complete` fires for its linked order — at which point `_yp_status` (the six-state PROJECT_SPEC §13 pipeline: Design in progress → Proof ready → Awaiting approval → Approved → Printing → Shipped) is set to its first value. This is Architecture §6's "WooCommerce order status drives payment/fulfillment; CustomOrder.status drives the production workflow" made concrete: `post_status` *is* the payment gate, `_yp_status` only ever means something once that gate has passed.
- **The custom design fee reuses the cart/pricing infrastructure from Phase 7 rather than a parallel system (Phase 8):** one lazily-created, catalog-hidden `Custom Design Fee` product (`YeffoPrint_Custom_Design_Fee_Product`, a simpler single-product version of `YeffoPrint_Linked_Product`) is added to the cart with a `yp_custom_order_id` cart-item key; `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::apply_price()` and `require_batch_data()` (Phase 7) were extended, not duplicated, to recognize it — price is read live from `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::get_custom_design_fee()` at calculate-totals time, same "always recalculates" rule as label batches, and the same defensive add-to-cart-validation net applies to it.
- **No customer name/email field on the Custom Design form (Phase 8):** PROJECT_SPEC §13's field list doesn't include one, and WooCommerce checkout already collects billing details to process the $25 payment — asking twice would be redundant. `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Payment` copies `get_billing_email()`/`get_formatted_billing_full_name()` from the linked order onto the CustomOrder record once payment completes, the same moment it publishes the record and starts the status pipeline.
- **SVG uploads are allowed but sanitized and validated per-request, not enabled site-wide (Phase 8):** WordPress doesn't allow SVG uploads by default — reasonably, since an SVG can carry `<script>`, event-handler attributes, or external references. PROJECT_SPEC §13/§18 wants SVG as an inspiration-file type, so `YeffoPrint_Secure_Upload` allows it scoped to *this one upload flow only*, via `wp_handle_upload()`'s per-call `mimes` override rather than the global `upload_mimes` filter (which would open SVG uploads to every uploader on the site, well beyond what this feature needs). Every accepted SVG is parsed with `DOMDocument`, stripped of `<script>`/`<foreignObject>`/event-handler attributes/`javascript:`+`data:` URIs, and rejected outright if it contains a `<!DOCTYPE` (closes off XXE/entity-expansion attacks entirely rather than relying on libxml defaults) or doesn't parse as valid XML — we can't sanitize what we can't inspect, so an unparseable file is rejected, not stored as-is.
- **Proof is its own CPT, one post per proof, not an array on CustomOrder (Phase 8):** matches Architecture §2's `proof_history[]` in spirit while giving each proof its own post_date (the timestamp), its own file, and — when the customer-facing proof portal is eventually built (an explicit V1 non-goal, PROJECT_SPEC §19; Architecture §8 anticipates it) — its own approval/comment state, without reshaping this record. `YeffoPrint_Proof_Meta::get_for_custom_order()` queries by `_yp_custom_order_id` rather than the CustomOrder holding a list of IDs, so adding a proof never means re-saving the CustomOrder post. Uploading a proof auto-advances its CustomOrder from "Design in progress" to "Proof ready" (forward-only, never overwriting a status staff already moved past) — the one piece of workflow automation V1 needs; everything past that (customer view/approve/request-changes) stays manual/off-platform until that future portal exists.
- **The Custom Design page is created by a setup command, not auto-provisioned (Phase 8):** `wp yeffoprint setup-pages` creates a page at `/custom-design/` assigned to the theme's `custom-design-form.html` custom template, idempotent by slug — same reasoning as `setup-shipping`: this is site structure a real store needs once, not demo content, so it's dev-triggered only and never overwrites an existing page.
- **"Restore batch into configurator, then edit" is in scope; "one-click reorder" isn't — these are different things (Phase 9):** PROJECT_SPEC §16 literally describes the first ("Architecture must support future Reorder (restore batch into configurator, then edit before purchase)"); §19's non-goal is the second, a hypothetical instant re-cart that skips the configurator entirely — a pattern this project was never going to use anyway, since every order needs a size/material/quantity/customization pass through the same validated path everything else goes through. So Phase 9 builds the first for real: a "Reorder this design" link per order line item (`class-reorder.php`, via `woocommerce_order_item_meta_end`) sends the customer to that Template's configurator with `?reorder=<order_id>:<item_id>`; a new endpoint (`GET /orders/{order_id}/items/{item_id}`, ownership-checked — order data isn't public the way a cart session is) returns the same `{template_id, size_id, material_id, variants}` shape `GET /cart/item/{key}` already returns for "Edit customization," so `configurator.js` rehydrates from either source through one shared code path (`loadExternalBatch()`/`hydrateFromBatch()`) — reordering was already almost free once Phase 7's edit-rehydration existed generically enough to reuse instead of being cart-item-specific.
- **WooCommerce's native "Order Again" button is hidden, not left to fail (Phase 9):** it re-adds every line item via a bare `add_to_cart()` call, which every product this store sells rejects outright unless it carries batch/custom-order data (`require_batch_data()`, Phase 7/8) — since practically every order here contains at least one such item, the native button would silently do nothing useful. `class-reorder.php` removes it via the `woocommerce_my_account_my_orders_actions` filter (checking each order's items for `_yp_template_snapshot`/`_yp_custom_order_id`) in favor of the per-item link above, matching PROJECT_SPEC §18's "non-silent error handling" — a control that can't work correctly shouldn't be shown at all.
- **Saved Designs and Rewards are real navigation tabs with placeholder content, not features (Phase 9):** both are explicit V1 non-goals (PROJECT_SPEC §19), but the *account navigation structure* — Orders, Saved Designs, Rewards, Proofs, Addresses, in that order — is itself a PROJECT_SPEC §16 requirement independent of whether each tab's feature is built yet. `class-account-endpoints.php` rebuilds `woocommerce_account_menu_items` to match that order (preserving any other WC-provided items like Downloads) and registers the two extra endpoints with plain "coming soon" content — no data model, no save/restore logic, nothing to rework later. The Batch/Variant JSON shape a real Saved Design would eventually store already exists generically (it's the same shape cart items and order snapshots already use) — no new "architecture" was needed for that part specifically, only the nav placeholder.
- **Proofs is the one new customer-facing tab with real content (Phase 9):** it queries `yp_custom_order` posts by the new `_yp_customer_id` meta (set from `$order->get_customer_id()` at payment time, Phase 8's `class-custom-order-payment.php`) rather than cross-referencing every order against the current user on each page load, and lists each request's status and any uploaded Proof files as plain download links — view-only, no approve/reject controls, which stays the deliberate line against the "online proof approval UI" non-goal.
- **My Account dashboard is a real template override, not an action-hook addition (Phase 9):** `wp-content/themes/yeffoprint/woocommerce/myaccount/dashboard.php` fully replaces WooCommerce's default "Hello X (not X? Log out)" welcome text with branded copy and quick-link cards — an early draft just hooked the `woocommerce_account_dashboard` action instead, which only *adds* content after WC's own hardcoded greeting paragraph rather than replacing it, so it couldn't actually satisfy PROJECT_SPEC §16's "not default WooCommerce My Account visuals." The override still fires `woocommerce_before/after_account_dashboard` and `woocommerce_account_dashboard` so any other extension's dashboard content still appears.
- **A rewrite-flush-on-activation hook was added retroactively (Phase 9):** the Template CPT's `/shop-labels/` archive slug (Phase 3) never had one, meaning it likely wouldn't resolve on a fresh install until something else triggered a permalink flush (e.g. visiting Settings → Permalinks). The new My Account endpoints have the exact same requirement, so this was the right moment to fix both: `register_activation_hook()` in `yeffoprint-core.php` flags a flush, and `YeffoPrint_Core` performs it on the next `init` at priority 20 — after every CPT/endpoint has registered its rewrite rules at the default priority 10, in the same request.
- **Rewrite flush also self-heals on a version bump, not just plugin activation (live-site bugfix):** deployed via manual file upload rather than the WP admin plugin installer, so `register_activation_hook` above never fired at all — `/shop-labels/` and the My Account endpoints 404'd until someone happened to visit Settings → Permalinks. `YeffoPrint_Core`'s init hook now also flushes whenever the stored `yeffoprint_core_rewrite_version` option doesn't match `YEFFOPRINT_CORE_VERSION`, which covers every real-world deploy path (FTP/file-manager upload, git sync, staging→production push) that skips WP's activation hook entirely — not just a proper install/update through wp-admin.
- **Query Loop grid className belongs on Post Template, not the Query wrapper (live-site bugfix):** `patterns/featured-designs.php` and `patterns/popular-designs.php` both had `"className":"yp-card-grid"` set on the `core/query` block, which renders it onto `<div class="wp-block-query">` — that div's only child is the `<ul class="wp-block-post-template">`, so `.yp-card-grid`'s `display:grid` had exactly one grid cell to lay out and the cards stacked full-width instead of in a 4-column grid. `templates/archive-yp_template.html` already had this right (className on `core/post-template`, which is what actually renders as the `<ul>` wrapping each card); both patterns now match it.
- **WooCommerce's Block-Hooks auto-injected Mini Cart is explicitly stripped (live-site bugfix):** on a block theme, WooCommerce uses the `hooked_block_types` filter to auto-insert its own `woocommerce/mini-cart` block into the header — `parts/header.html` never added one, but it rendered live anyway, as a second cart UI alongside the theme's own icon/drawer (Phase 2/7) with its own independently-updating count and no wiring to `assets/js/site.js`'s drawer state. `functions.php` now filters `woocommerce/mini-cart` out of `hooked_block_types` so the theme's own cart drawer is the only one that renders, consistent with the Phase 7 decision above to build a custom server-rendered drawer instead of adopting the Mini Cart block.
- **The "classic-cart/Blocks-cart gap" from Phase 7 was a false alarm, not a real bug (corrected post-launch):** the live cart's empty `item_data: []` that originally looked like confirmation of the Phase 7 risk was actually just a manually-added test product with no batch data — see the corrected Phase 7 entry above. WooCommerce core's Store API already relays `woocommerce_get_item_data` (plain-text values only) into the Blocks Cart/Checkout, so `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::display_item_data()` needs no changes and no `ExtendSchema` integration was built. Verified against WooCommerce core's `CartItemSchema::get_item_data()` source, not just assumed.
- **A `gradient-brand` custom token replaces flat near-black as the site's primary "brand moment" color (post-launch design pass):** the palette already had CMYK-inspired accent colors (cyan/magenta/yellow, apt for a printing brand) but they were only ever used as three small decorative dots in the hero — the rest of the homepage was white/warm-white/charcoal/near-black, reading as flat rather than as a brand with a distinct visual identity. `theme.json`'s new `--wp--custom--gradient-brand` (`linear-gradient(135deg, magenta → cyan)`) is the single source of truth for that color moment, now applied to: the primary button style (`is-style-accent`, used for every real CTA — Browse Designs, Add to Cart, Create a Custom Label), the header logotype (gradient text, with an `@supports` fallback to solid near-black for browsers without `background-clip: text`), and the Featured/badge pill. Section eyebrows switched from charcoal to magenta, the "How It Works" step markers now cycle through cyan/magenta/yellow (echoing the hero's three-dot motif instead of introducing a new pattern), the hero and the dark "Fully Custom" section both got soft radial-gradient color washes instead of flat backgrounds, and card/badge hover states use a colored glow shadow instead of a neutral one. Product photography and the neutral warm-white/charcoal base are left alone — the goal was a bolder brand accent layered on top of a still-quiet backdrop, not a full repaint.
- **`magenta-deep`/`cyan-deep` and `gradient-brand-deep` exist specifically for text/foreground use, separate from the brighter decorative palette (Phase 10, and self-caught in the color-scheme pass above):** raw `#EC008C`/`#00AEEF` read well as large decorative fills (glows, washes, dots) but fail WCAG AA (4.5:1) as small text or as a background behind white text — magenta-on-white measures ~4.24:1, white-on-cyan ~2.53:1. A Phase 10 audit caught several pre-existing instances of this (configurator error/status text, character-limit indicator, upload error text, cart-count badge) and, in the same pass, caught that the color-scheme redesign above had just introduced more of it (the gradient CTA button and header logo both put white/foreground text directly on the bright gradient's cyan end). Fixed by adding `magenta-deep` (#C2007A, ~5.9:1 vs white) and `cyan-deep` (#0078A4, ~5.0:1 vs white) to the palette and a `gradient-brand-deep` token built from them, then routing every text-bearing use (eyebrows, error/status text, the CTA button fill, the logo's gradient-clipped text, badges, the cart-count pill, the magenta "How It Works" step marker) through the deep variants — the brighter originals stay reserved for non-text decorative use (hero/dark-section washes, glow shadows, the hero's three accent dots), where contrast rules don't apply.
- **Shop/homepage label images are 15:7 (45mm x 21mm), scoped to `.yp-template-card__media` only, not the shared `.yp-card__media` (Phase 10):** the generic card-media class (`global.css`) still defaults to a 4:5 portrait ratio because other card types reuse it for non-label content (e.g. `customer-work.php`'s placeholder inspiration tiles) — forcing every `.yp-card` into a skinny rectangle would have been wrong for those. The label-specific override lives in `patterns.css`'s `.yp-template-card__media` rule instead, which wins the cascade (patterns.css is enqueued after global.css) without touching the shared default. 15:7 is the label's real 45mm:21mm ratio in lowest terms, not a rounded approximation, so uploaded artwork cropped to that ratio lines up exactly.
- **A shared `YeffoPrint_Rest_Security::guest_or_nonced_write()` permission callback closes a CSRF gap on `/cart/add`, `/custom-orders`, and `/custom-orders/uploads` (Phase 10 security audit):** all three were `permission_callback => '__return_true'` with no nonce check at all, meaning a third-party page could POST to them riding a logged-in customer's cookies (force-adding cart items, or submitting a Custom Order and its $25 fee on their behalf). Guests have no authenticated session worth protecting, so they still pass through unchecked (`is_user_logged_in()` short-circuits to `true`) — preserving PROJECT_SPEC §20's "no required account to purchase" — but a logged-in request now must carry a valid `X-WP-Nonce`, which `yeffoprintConfigurator`/`yeffoprintCustomOrder`'s localized `nonce` (already `wp_create_nonce('wp_rest')`) now gets sent on every write call, not just the reorder endpoint it originally existed for.
- **SVG upload detection sniffs content, not just the filename extension (Phase 10 security audit):** `YeffoPrint_Secure_Upload::looks_like_svg()` previously trusted `pathinfo($file['name'], PATHINFO_EXTENSION)` alone, so a script-bearing SVG renamed to end in `.png`/`.jpg` would skip `sanitize_svg_file()` entirely and be stored via the ordinary (non-SVG) path. It now also sniffs the first 4KB of the uploaded file for an `<svg` tag; if content looks like SVG but the declared extension isn't `.svg`, the upload is rejected outright (a mismatch is treated as suspicious, not silently corrected) rather than accepted under the wrong type.
- **The `/custom-orders/uploads` endpoint is rate-limited per IP (Phase 10 security audit):** unauthenticated, multi-file, 10MB-per-file uploads with no request-level throttling is disk-fill/storage-cost abuse waiting to happen. A transient-keyed (by IP, 10-minute window, 20 requests) counter in `class-custom-order-controller.php` caps it, independent of `YeffoPrint_Secure_Upload`'s existing per-request file-count/size limits — it fails open (no limit applied) only if the request has no discoverable IP at all, rather than blocking a legitimate customer.
- **`/custom-stickers/` nav links (header + footer, PROJECT_SPEC §7) 404'd on a fresh install — fixed with a "coming soon" page, same pattern as My Account's Saved Designs/Rewards placeholders (Phase 10 audit):** Custom Stickers is a real Phase 2 product line (Architecture §8 already anticipated it), not vaporware, but no page ever existed at that slug. `wp yeffoprint setup-pages` now creates it too (plain default template, honest "coming soon" copy + a link back to the label gallery) — same idempotent, dev-triggered-only convention as the existing Custom Design page, just refactored into a small reusable `create_page()` helper instead of one-off inline code.
- **The configurator's selection state and live price updates are now exposed to assistive tech (Phase 10 a11y audit):** Size/Material pickers are custom button groups that visually toggled `.is-selected` with no ARIA equivalent — they're now `role="radiogroup"`/`role="radio"` with `aria-checked` kept in sync. The Label View/Vial View toggle had `role="tablist"/"tab"`/`aria-selected` but no `aria-controls`/`role="tabpanel"` link to the stage and no arrow-key navigation (an incomplete ARIA tabs pattern); both are now wired up with roving `tabindex` and Left/Right/Home/End handling per the ARIA APG tabs pattern. The live price/breakdown summary (`[data-yp-summary]`) had no `aria-live` region, so changing size/material/quantity never announced the new price — it's now `aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"`.
- **An orphaned `<label for>` on the Custom Design form's Quantity field pointed at an id that didn't exist (Phase 10 a11y audit):** the static label targeted `yp-co-quantity`, but the JS-rendered input's actual id is `yp-co-quantity-input` — a copy/paste mismatch from Phase 8 that made the label non-functional (clicking it did nothing, and it wasn't announced as associated with the control). Fixed the `for` attribute and removed a since-redundant `screen-reader-text` label the JS was also rendering for the same input.
- **The sticky configurator preview no longer assumes a fixed header height (Phase 10 audit):** `top: 96px` was a guess that doesn't track the header's actual height, which changes with scroll-compaction (Phase 2) and nav wrapping at different viewport widths/zoom levels — on some combinations the sticky preview sat partially under the header. `site.js`'s existing scroll handler now also publishes the header's real, live height as `--yp-header-height` (via `ResizeObserver`, with a `window.resize` fallback), which `configurator.css` reads with a `96px` fallback for the pre-JS/no-JS case.
- **Typing in a customization field no longer rebuilds the entire stage on every keystroke (Phase 10 performance audit):** `renderStage()` previously ran on every `input` event too, tearing down and recreating every field's DOM node and re-running the font-fit reflow loop for all of them, not just the field being typed into. A new `updateStageField(fieldId)` updates and refits only that one field; `renderStage()` (full rebuild) is now reserved for view toggle/variant switch/initial load, where every field's position can change at once. `fitText()`'s shrink search was also switched from a 1px-at-a-time linear walk to a binary search over the font-size range (same monotonic-shrink assumption, far fewer forced-layout reads on a wide min/max range).
- **The newsletter signup form now says so instead of silently reloading (Phase 10 cleanup):** no email service is connected yet, and the form had no submit handler at all — submitting just reloaded the page with no feedback either way, which PROJECT_SPEC §18 calls out directly ("non-silent error handling"). `site.js` now intercepts submit and shows an honest "aren't open yet" status message, the same stub-not-silent-failure pattern already used for the Phase 5 Add to Cart button before Phase 7 wired it up for real.
- **`hooked_block_types`'s strict type hints caused a live site-wide critical error — confirmed, not just theorized (live-site bugfix):** the closure added in an earlier bugfix pass declared `array`/`string` type hints on the parameters WordPress core passes in (`$hooked_block_types`, `$relative_position`, `$anchor_block_type`), which fatals with a PHP `TypeError` the instant core calls it with a value that doesn't match exactly — plausible for `$anchor_block_type` on a root-level hook with no specific anchor. Removed the type hints (keeping only an `is_array()` guard on the one value the filter actually uses) rather than trying to match core's exact type contract, since this filter doesn't need to enforce a contract on values it doesn't read. User confirmed this fixed the reported "critical error on the site" after deploying the Phase 10 commit.
- **`.yp-card-grid` collapses gracefully with fewer cards than a full row, instead of leaving empty column tracks (live-site bugfix):** a screenshot from the live site (only one Featured Design published) showed exactly the bug this predicts — one card on the left, three full-width blank gaps next to it, since `repeat(4, 1fr)` always creates 4 explicit tracks regardless of how many children exist. Switched to `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr))`, which collapses unused tracks instead of rendering them empty, and dropped the now-redundant fixed 2-column/1-column breakpoints (auto-fit already adapts at every width). To stop a lone card in an otherwise-empty row from stretching to fill the whole row width, `.yp-template-card` also got a `max-width: 320px` cap — the grid cell can still stretch, but the card content inside it doesn't have to.
- **Hero and dark-section color washes were visually too faint to read as intentional (live-site bugfix):** the live screenshot showed them barely visible — a symptom of both low opacity and the hero variant's `inset: -20%` sizing the blob positions against an enlarged box instead of the visible section, diluting where the color actually landed. Hero opacities raised (~0.18–0.24 → ~0.28–0.4) and switched to `inset: 0` to match the dark section's already-more-visible approach; the dark section's own opacities got a smaller bump too (~0.32–0.35 → ~0.4–0.45).
- **Homepage polish round 2, from live-site feedback:** four fixes. (1) `.yp-eyebrow` was `display: inline-block`, which silently breaks WordPress core's `has-text-align-center` utility class — centering an inline-block's own text does nothing once the box has already shrunk to fit that text, so "Materials"/"Trending"/"Inspiration"/"Fully Custom" (all using that class) rendered left-anchored instead of centered under their heading, reading as "oddly placed." Switched to `display: block` so the centering utility works as WordPress intends; bumped 0.75rem → 0.8125rem since "tiny" was also flagged. (2) How It Works' three cards could render at very slightly different heights depending on browser stretch behavior inside a `layout:{"type":"constrained"}` group — made this deterministic with explicit `align-items: stretch` on the grid and `display:flex; flex-direction:column` on each card, rather than relying on implicit grid stretch through a WordPress layout wrapper. (3) A `.yp-section--tint` modifier (a faint brand-gradient wash, not flat gray) now alternates onto the Materials and Customer Inspiration sections, breaking up an otherwise all-white/warm-white page. (4) The Customer Inspiration tiles were flat light-gray empty boxes (`.yp-card__media`'s default) with zero visual content — read as broken/missing images rather than deliberate placeholders. `.yp-customer-tile` gives them a soft brand-gradient wash and a small photo-icon glyph instead, honestly signaling "real photos coming soon" the same way the newsletter-form fix (Phase 10) was honest about not being live yet. FAQ was also dropped from the homepage (`front-page.html`) per direct request — the pattern itself (`patterns/faq.php`) wasn't deleted, only unhooked from the front page, in case it's wanted on a dedicated page later.
- **The header logo slot already exists — `parts/header.html` already has `core/site-logo` next to `core/site-title` (clarified, not a bug):** the "no logo anywhere" report turned out to be a content gap, not missing code — no logo image has been set via wp-admin (Site Editor/Customizer → Site Identity), so only the site-title text renders; `core/site-logo` renders nothing on the front end when unset, by WordPress's own design. Once a real logo is uploaded there, it'll appear automatically. Worth revisiting if the logo image and the "YeffoPrint" site-title text end up looking redundant side by side once that happens — not fixed preemptively since the actual rendered result can't be judged before a real logo exists.
- **The header is now dark, not light-glass — the real logo is a white/colored wordmark that needs a dark background to read at all:** `.yp-header` switched from the translucent-white glass token to `rgba(20,20,20,0.88)`; nav links, icon buttons, and the site-title text fallback all switched from near-black/charcoal to warm-white/translucent-white to stay legible. The gradient text-clip fallback (for whenever no logo image is set yet) switched from `gradient-brand-deep` back to the brighter `gradient-brand` — the "-deep" variant exists specifically for text-on-light-background contrast, which no longer applies once the background itself is dark. The mobile hamburger overlay (core Navigation block) also got explicit dark styling, since its unstyled default is a plain white panel that would otherwise clash with the header it opens from.
- **Homepage trimmed from 9 sections to 5, and given bolder, more consistent card/section treatments (direct request: "less info… more focused on my printing and my branding"):** dropped Reviews (explicitly placeholder/unattributed — `patterns/reviews.php`'s own docblock says "replace with real, attributed customer reviews before launch"), Rewards Promo (advertises a "Coming Soon" feature that doesn't exist yet), Popular Designs (redundant with Featured Designs — same card-grid showcase, and Popular is already a sort option on the Shop Labels gallery page), and Customer Inspiration (currently all-empty placeholder tiles, no real photos yet). None of the four pattern files were deleted, only unhooked from `front-page.html` — trivial to bring back once there's real content for them (real reviews, a real rewards program, real customer photos), which was the same reasoning as the "customer-tile" honest-placeholder decision above. Remaining 5 (Hero, Featured Designs, How It Works, Fully Custom CTA, Materials) is a tighter, product-and-brand-focused page. Also added `.yp-section--tint` to Featured Designs (alternating wash→tint→white→dark→tint down the page) and a colored top-accent bar to `.yp-card` (brand gradient) and `.yp-step` (its own cyan/magenta/yellow per card) for a bolder, more consistently branded card treatment site-wide — this was the "keep body sections light but noticeably bolder" direction chosen over a full dark theme or alternating dark/light sections.
- **Customer customization text (compound/strength/brand/whatever a Template's field_schema defines) was never actually shown anywhere — a real bug, not a display nit:** `_yp_variants` (the JSON holding each label's typed field values) has been hidden order-item meta since Phase 7, and the only human-readable rows added alongside it were Size/Material/a bare variant *count* — never the field values themselves. So neither staff on the order screen nor the customer on their cart/checkout review or order email could see what was actually typed in, only that *something* was — a real gap for a print business where "what does this label actually say" is the one thing that matters most. Fixed with one shared formatter, `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::format_variant_summary()` (e.g. `"Compound: NAD+ — Strength: 500mg"`, using each field's own label and skipping blanks), called from two places: `class-order-item-meta.php`'s snapshot (adds a `Customization` row, or one `Label N (qty M)` row per variant for a multi-label batch) and `class-cart-pricing.php`'s `display_item_data()` (same rows on the cart/checkout review, so a customer can catch a typo before paying, not just after). The compact cart *drawer* deliberately still doesn't show this level of detail — PROJECT_SPEC §14 scopes it to template/size/material/quantity/price/Edit-customization by design; the cart page and order are where the full review belongs.
- **Theme assets are now versioned by their own filemtime, not the never-bumped `style.css` Version header — a real, session-spanning bug, not a one-off:** every `wp_enqueue_style`/`wp_enqueue_script` call in `functions.php` used `wp_get_theme()->get('Version')` (`0.1.0` since Phase 1, never once bumped) as the cache-busting `?ver=` query string. That means every CSS/JS-only deploy this entire build — the color-scheme pass, the dark header, the Phase 10 a11y/performance work including the exact configurator.js change that prompted this fix, all of it — was enqueued under an *identical* URL to the version before it, so any browser, CDN, or host-level cache that had ever cached one of these files had no reason to ever fetch the new one. Combined with the caching layer already confirmed present on this site (it caused the earlier "Add to Cart doesn't work" report), this is a very plausible explanation for "I don't see the fix" symptoms generally, not just the live-preview one. Replaced with `yeffoprint_asset_version( $relative_path )`, which returns `filemtime()` of the actual file — automatically busts cache on every deploy that touches that specific file, permanently, without depending on anyone remembering to bump a version number by hand (which never happened once). Scoped to the theme's `functions.php` enqueue calls; the plugin's admin-only assets (`yeffoprint-core/includes/admin/*`) have the same never-bumped-version pattern but weren't touched here — lower risk (wp-admin is rarely behind the same caching layers) and out of scope for this specific report.
- **The configurator's preview stage now matches each view's actual image shape instead of forcing both into one fixed square (live-site bugfix):** `.yp-configurator__stage` was a single `aspect-ratio: 1` box for both Label View and Vial View, with `object-fit: contain` — since real label artwork is 15:7 (rectangular, same ratio as the shop gallery cards) not square, it got shrunk to fit inside the square box, leaving visible gray letterboxing on the sides (the box's own background color, showing through wherever the image doesn't reach). Label View is now `aspect-ratio: 15/7` by default, with `[data-view="vial"]` overriding back to `1` — each view's box now matches what's actually meant to be uploaded to it, no code needed to detect the uploaded image's own dimensions since `field.position` percentages and `fitText()`'s box-fit math already read the stage's live rendered size rather than a hardcoded value (unaffected by this change). Also documented the expected upload sizes at the point of upload: a hint under the Template's featured-image box (`admin_post_thumbnail_html` filter, scoped to `yp_template`) for the 15:7 label artwork, and an updated description under the Vial mockup image field noting it's square for Vial View but also doubles as the gallery card's rectangular hover-swap image — a square mockup will letterbox in that second, unrelated slot, which is expected and only affects the hover-swap, not Vial View itself.
- **`.yp-card-grid` switched from `auto-fit, minmax(…,1fr)` to fixed-width `auto-fill` columns — the previous fix for a sparse row (one card, four columns) introduced a different misalignment bug for a *partially* full row:** with `1fr` tracks, each card's own grid cell stretched to share the row's full width, so with e.g. 2 cards in a nominally-4-column row, card 2 sat at the start of its own (very wide) second cell — far to the right of card 1, not next to it, since the `max-width: 320px` cap on `.yp-template-card` only capped the *card*, not the cell it was left-aligned within. `repeat(auto-fill, 270px)` uses fixed-width tracks instead: every card is the same width and sits immediately next to the last one regardless of count, and unused row space is simply blank at the end rather than distributed into cells — still fully responsive (auto-fill fits as many 270px columns as the viewport allows, same as before). The per-card `max-width` cap is gone too; it's redundant now that the column itself is a fixed size.
- **Header background made bolder per direct feedback ("just gray... can we be more creative, something with CMYK color, something that pops"):** the flat `rgba(20,20,20,0.88)` picked up two layered radial-gradient glows (cyan top-left, magenta bottom-right, same technique as the hero and dark CTA sections) instead of a flat color, plus a 2px CMYK gradient line along the bottom edge (`gradient-brand`, the same token used on card accent bars) replacing the plain `rgba(255,255,255,0.08)` border. A "Deployer for Git" banner visible in the screenshot that prompted this initially looked like a mid-deploy artifact explaining a mismatched light-header/light-text render — the user clarified it was an unrelated window behind the screenshot, not the site, so that render was real and not a deploy-timing artifact. The likely actual explanation (flagged to the user, not yet confirmed): WordPress stores template-part edits made through Appearance → Editor in the database, permanently overriding the theme file from then on — if `parts/header.html` was ever opened and saved there, the DB copy, not this repo's file, is what renders, and no amount of git-committed CSS/HTML changes would be visible until that override is cleared.
- **Nav links restyled as visible pill/buttons, cache-busting switched from filemtime to a content hash (live-site feedback: "can't see the text... make them look like buttons," cards still misaligned after the auto-fill fix):** `.yp-header__nav` links now get a translucent-white pill background (`rgba(255,255,255,0.1)`, brightening on hover) instead of relying solely on text-color contrast against the dark header — directly requested ("style the links better, make them look like buttons"). For the card grid, no further structural bug was found on inspection (the `auto-fill, 270px` fix from the previous entry is correct CSS); `justify-content: start` and `align-items: start` were added to `.yp-card-grid` explicitly as a defensive measure removing any ambiguity, and `yeffoprint_asset_version()` (`functions.php`) switched from `filemtime()` to `md5_file()` (truncated to 12 chars) — some git-based deploy tools don't reliably bump a file's modified-time on checkout, which would silently reintroduce the exact stale-cache problem the filemtime fix was meant to close, on a per-deploy-mechanism basis that's invisible from this codebase. A content hash has no such dependency: it changes if and only if the file's actual bytes did, regardless of how the deploy got them onto the server.
- **`align-items: start` in the commit above was a self-caught regression, reverted immediately:** it removed the grid's default `stretch`, which is what keeps every card the same outer height across a row when body text length differs between two cards — `start` would size each card to its own content instead, misaligning bottom edges, the opposite of the intent. `justify-content: start` (unrelated — controls leftover horizontal space, not row height) was kept.
- **Two Template-editor features landed together, both closing gaps flagged as deferred back in Phase 4/5 (direct request: positioning is "really hard to decide," and no way to set text color when a label's own background is dark):** (1) A `text_color` field (`YeffoPrint_Field_Schema`, default `#000000`, sanitized via `sanitize_hex_color()`) flows through the existing `field_schema` pipeline with no separate REST work needed — `class-template-schema-controller.php` already passes `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::get()`'s output through wholesale. `configurator.js` applies it as `el.style.color` when rendering a stage field. (2) A drag-to-position preview in the "Customization Fields" meta box (`assets/admin/field-schema.js`, `#yp-field-position-preview`) shows the Template's actual featured image with one draggable marker per field, labeled by the field's own label — dragging a marker updates that field's `position.x`/`position.y` (and vice versa: editing the raw percentage inputs live-updates the marker), so "where will this land" is answered by looking at the real artwork instead of guessing two blind percentages. The numeric inputs stay for exact adjustment; nothing about the stored data shape changed, so this is presentation-only on top of the existing `position: {x, y}` schema. Matches Architecture §9's Phase 4 note that a visual picker was intentionally deferred "until the Label View preview surface exists" — it does now (Phase 5's configurator), so this was always meant to land as a follow-up, not a new decision. The plugin's admin-only assets (`admin.css`, `field-schema.js`, `pricing-tiers.js`, `vial-mockup-picker.js`) also picked up the same content-hash cache-busting (`yeffoprint_core_asset_version()`, `yeffoprint-core.php`) as the theme got two entries above — flagged then as lower-risk and out of scope, but landing new admin JS right now made it the right moment to close that gap too, so the new picker doesn't risk the exact stale-cache problem that prompted the theme-side fix.

## V2 (V1's PROJECT_SPEC §19 non-goals, built one at a time)

Direct request: "onto v2 — saved designs UI, one-click reorder UI, online proof approval UI, rewards dashboard, referral system." Five separate features; building them all in one pass would break the phased-validation discipline the whole V1 build followed and be too much to review together. Sequenced as: Saved Designs and one-click Reorder first (both reuse existing rehydration/snapshot infrastructure directly, lowest ambiguity), then Proof Approval (moderate — customer-facing state changes plus staff notification), then Rewards and Referral last, since those need real business-rule decisions (points-earning formula, redemption mechanics, referral payout amounts) that can't be invented unilaterally — those get asked about when their turn comes, not guessed now.

- **Saved Designs is real (first V2 feature):** Architecture §8 anticipated this exact shape ("Batch/Variant model already sufficient for a future... 'Saved Designs' (persist a Batch to a user without purchasing)") — a new `yp_saved_design` CPT (customer-owned via native `post_author`, not a custom `_yp_customer_id` meta field like CustomOrder needs, since a saved design is always created by the logged-in customer who owns it, never before that's known) stores `{template_id, size_id, material_id, variants}`, the identical shape a cart item and order line item snapshot already use. `YeffoPrint_Saved_Design_Controller` exposes create/list/fetch/delete over REST, all requiring login (nothing to attach an anonymous save to) with item-level ownership checks on fetch/delete. Fetching one saved design returns the exact same `{template_id, size_id, material_id, variants}` shape `GET /cart/item/{key}` and `GET /orders/{id}/items/{id}` already return, so `configurator.js`'s existing `hydrateFromBatch()`/`loadExternalBatch()` needed only a third source URL (`?saved=<id>`) added alongside `?edit=`/`?reorder=`, not new rehydration logic.
- **Saved designs are explicitly allowed to be incomplete drafts, unlike a cart-bound batch:** the "required field must be filled" check inside variant sanitization was previously baked into `class-cart-controller.php`'s private `sanitize_variants()`. Extracted to `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::sanitize_variants( $raw, $field_schema, $enforce_required = true )` — a shared method now used by both cart-add (`$enforce_required` stays `true`, unchanged behavior) and saved-design create (`false`) — so the exact same quantity/max_chars rules apply everywhere without duplicating them, while only cart/checkout enforces completeness. Rejecting an unfinished save would defeat the point of saving something to finish later.
- **"Save this design" is a plain button in the configurator, not conditionally hidden for guests:** `templates/*.html` isn't executed as PHP (an established fact from earlier in this project — see the custom-design-form.html stray-`<?php>`-tag bugfix), so it can't conditionally omit the button server-side based on login state. It's always rendered; `functions.php` localizes `isLoggedIn`/`accountUrl` alongside the existing nonce, and `configurator.js` relabels it to "Log in to save this design" (redirecting to My Account on click) rather than showing a button that would silently fail or need a confusing error message for a guest.
- **The "Remove" action on a saved design is a plain POST-and-redirect form, not REST/JS:** matches the account tab's existing all-server-rendered pattern (Proofs has no JS either). Handled on `template_redirect` — before any theme output starts — specifically so a real `wp_safe_redirect()` back to a clean URL is possible after deleting, avoiding a resubmit-on-refresh POST sitting in the customer's browser history the way processing it inline during the tab's own render (after output has already started) would have.
- **My Account nav switched from wrapped horizontal pills to a vertical stack (live-site bugfix, pre-existing since Phase 9, not a regression from recent work):** `.woocommerce-MyAccount-navigation ul` was `display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap`, packing pill-style links left-to-right and wrapping by available width — fine at the original ~5-item count, but with Saved Designs now real and the nav at 9 items (Dashboard/Orders/Saved Designs/Rewards/Proofs/Downloads/Addresses/Account details/Log out), a live screenshot showed it wrapping into uneven, oddly-indented clusters rather than a predictable list. Switched to `flex-direction: column` — a vertical stack has no wrapping ambiguity regardless of how many endpoints this ever grows to, and is the more conventional pattern for an account sidebar anyway.
- **Found the real card-misalignment bug, via a DevTools-confirmed screenshot (two prior fix rounds addressed real but different issues — grid track stretching, then a self-caught align-items regression — without ever being the actual cause here):** `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, 270px)` was confirmed correctly applied (computed styles matched exactly), so the grid itself was never the problem. The screenshot showed card 2's image area visibly *taller* than card 1's, pushing its whole card down out of alignment — `.yp-template-card__image--primary` was `position: static` (correct back when the card's height was *determined by* the image, pre-dating `.yp-template-card__media`'s `aspect-ratio: 15/7` fix), and a static image relying on `height: 100%` doesn't reliably resolve against an ancestor whose height comes from `aspect-ratio` rather than an explicit value — it falls back to the image's own intrinsic ratio instead. So each card's height ended up depending on whatever ratio its specific uploaded image happened to actually have, not the fixed 15:7 box — explaining why one card looked right (its source image happened to be close to 15:7 already) and the other didn't (its source image wasn't). Fixed by making the primary image `position: absolute` too (inheriting from the base `.yp-template-card__image` rule, matching the hover image), so both are forced into the identical box via `inset: 0` + `object-fit: cover` regardless of each source image's own dimensions — the correct fix given the aspect-ratio box now exists, not a workaround.
- **Custom Design reorder + placeholder image (V2, second item — direct request bundled "AI-generated placeholder image" and "save this custom template to reorder"):** the "upload an example / describe what you want / compound-strength / colors" part was already the Phase 8 Custom Design flow — nothing new needed there. Two things genuinely were: (1) reordering a past Custom Order, and (2) a placeholder image where a real one doesn't exist. User chose a plain placeholder over integrating a real AI image-generation API for now (that needs a provider/API key decision that's the user's to make, not something to invent) — swapping in real AI generation later is a small, contained change once a provider is chosen. Reorder for a CustomOrder can't restore into a configurator the way Template Reorder does (Architecture §2: a CustomOrder is a one-off request, no premade Template exists) — instead, `GET /custom-orders/{id}` (ownership-checked, login-required — this is always private customer data, no guest path like Template Reorder's cart/order lookup has) returns the past request's own details, and `custom-order-form.js` pre-fills a fresh form from them, including carrying forward previously-uploaded reference files as already-uploaded (same attachment ID, no re-upload needed) unless removed. `class-reorder.php`'s per-order-item link (previously only handled `_yp_template_snapshot` items, silently doing nothing for a Custom Order line item) now also renders "Reorder this custom design" for `_yp_custom_order_id` items, linking to `/custom-design/?reorder=<id>`; the My Account Proofs tab's CustomOrder cards get the same link directly (more discoverable than digging into order details) plus a generic vial-icon placeholder thumbnail, matching the same "coming soon," not-broken-image placeholder language already used for Customer Inspiration tiles.
- **Card-grid saga, round 4 — a real latent specificity conflict, found by reading the full cascade side by side rather than one file at a time:** `.yp-card__media` (global.css, generic default `aspect-ratio: 4/5`) and `.yp-template-card__media` (patterns.css, `aspect-ratio: 15/7`) are both single-class selectors applied to the *same element* (`render.php` outputs `class="yp-card__media yp-template-card__media"` together) — equal specificity, so which one actually won was determined by stylesheet load order, not guaranteed by anything explicit in the rule itself. Fixed by combining both classes into one selector (`.yp-card__media.yp-template-card__media`), which is unambiguously more specific and can't lose to a load-order accident regardless of future enqueue changes. Not yet confirmed against live evidence as of this entry — the user reported the previous round's fix (image `position:absolute`) didn't resolve it even after confirming a live, cache-clear-verified deploy, so this is treated as an additional real bug found on inspection, not a confirmed root cause; asked for a fresh screenshot + computed-height check again rather than assuming this closes it.
- **Custom Design orders now charge for the print run itself, not just the $25 design fee (direct request: "customers also order the labels that go with their custom design at the same time... charged for whatever quantity... adjust pricing per label depending on the size/material"):** previously `submit()` only ever added one cart item — the flat fee, priced from `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::get_custom_design_fee()` — and the form's own copy said so explicitly ("Your printed order is priced and placed separately once your proof is approved"), which was accurate for what the code did but not what the business actually wants: the customer pays for both up front. A second hidden anchor product, `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Labels_Product` ("Custom Order Labels"), mirrors `YeffoPrint_Custom_Design_Fee_Product`'s pattern (exactly one instance ever, catalog-hidden, price always read live from the cart item never the product record) but pipes through the *same* per-unit formula a Template batch uses (`YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()`, base + size/material adjustments, bulk discount tiers) — size/material selection on the Custom Design form already existed and already had `price_adjustment` meta on those records, so no new pricing data was needed, only a second cart item to carry it.
