Distinguishing the two Custom-Order-linked cart items (both carry `CUSTOM_ORDER_ID` so `woocommerce_payment_complete` can find and publish the record from either) is `TOTAL_QTY`: present only on the labels item, so `apply_price()`, `display_item_data()`, and `class-order-item-meta.php::snapshot()` all branch on "`CUSTOM_ORDER_ID` + empty `TOTAL_QTY`" (fee, flat price, shows Brand) vs. "`CUSTOM_ORDER_ID` + present `TOTAL_QTY`" (labels, per-unit price, shows Size/Material/Quantity, no Batch/variant rows since a Custom Order has no field_schema behind it). `class-reorder.php`'s per-item "Reorder this custom design" link now only renders on the item *without* a `_yp_batch_quantity` meta key (the fee item) — both items would otherwise carry `_yp_custom_order_id` and print the identical link twice per order. The form's pricing summary was rewritten to show the fee, the labels subtotal (with unit price), and a bolded total-due-today, live-updated via the existing template-agnostic `GET /pricing/calculate` endpoint (Phase 8 already built it "deliberately template-agnostic... so Custom Order flow can reuse this unchanged") on every size/material/quantity change — a provisional preview only, same caveat as the Template configurator's live pricing, since `apply_price()` is what's actually authoritative at checkout.
- **Proof Approval is real (fourth V2 feature) — public, guest-friendly, and closes the "Proof Foundation" loop the account-endpoints comment had flagged as still missing:** direct request: land a customer-facing approve/request-changes flow, and make sure it works for a customer with no account, since "not everyone has an account that orders." A CustomOrder gets a long random `_yp_access_token` (`wp_generate_password(40, false)`, generated once at submission — not lazily, since a guest can't log back in later to fetch one) that's the sole guest-access credential for a new public page, `/proof-approval/` (`templates/proof-approval.html`, same `<!-- wp:html -->` + JS-talks-to-REST pattern as `/custom-design/` — block templates in this theme aren't executed as PHP, established earlier in this project, so a page needing server-side logic here means a REST-backed JS app, not conditional template markup). `class-proof-approval-controller.php`'s `check_access()` accepts either a `token` request param matching the stored one (`hash_equals()`, timing-safe) or, for a logged-in customer/staff, the same nonce check every other authenticated write endpoint in this plugin already uses — a guest has no session worth CSRF-protecting, a logged-in customer does. Uploading a proof (`class-proof-editor.php`) now advances the CustomOrder straight to a relabeled **"Awaiting Proof Approval"** status (was "Awaiting approval" — matches the user's own phrase for it, and it's user-facing text now, not just an internal marker) instead of the intermediate "Proof ready," since with a real customer-facing step to send them to, "ready" and "time to notify" are the same moment; approving sets `approved`, and "Request changes" deliberately reuses the existing `design_in_progress` state (PROJECT_SPEC §13's six states, no new state invented) rather than adding a seventh, since staff have design work to do again regardless of which state got them there — the customer's free-text feedback is captured in a new `_yp_change_request_notes` meta, surfaced on the CustomOrder admin screen. Both response actions are guarded to only fire from `awaiting_approval` (else a 409, "already responded to") so a customer can't double-approve or a stale tab can't silently overwrite a newer response. Reaching the customer for a guest order specifically (the whole reason this needed to be link-based) also got a `wp_mail()` notification — best-effort only, quietly does nothing without a valid stored email rather than erroring, since the admin's Proofs meta box shows the exact same link in a copyable field for staff to (re)send manually regardless (a bounced/mistyped email is a real possibility this project has no way to detect). The My Account Proofs tab also gets a "Review & approve this proof" link when a CustomOrder is in that status, reusing the exact same public page and permission logic rather than building a second parallel flow for logged-in customers.
- **A paid test Custom Order never published, staying invisible in both the admin list and the Proofs screen's dropdown — root cause found via live testing (order confirmed "Processing" in WooCommerce, record still stuck as an unpublished draft):** the only thing that ever moves a CustomOrder from `draft` to `publish` (class-custom-order-payment.php) was a single hook on `woocommerce_payment_complete` — a method gateways are expected to call themselves after actually processing a charge, not something WooCommerce fires automatically on every "order is now paid" transition. `WC_Gateway_COD::process_payment()` (core WooCommerce), the easiest way to test checkout without a real card, moves the order straight to "processing"/"on-hold" via `update_status()` and never calls `payment_complete()` at all — so a COD test order (or a manual admin status change) reaches "Processing" while this plugin never hears about it. Fixed by also hooking `woocommerce_order_status_processing` and `woocommerce_order_status_completed`, which fire on that exact transition regardless of gateway or manual change; the existing `'draft' !== $custom_order->post_status` guard already made the handler idempotent, so a normal gateway order that fires both `payment_complete` *and* a status-transition event still only gets linked once. Along the way, fixed a second, related bug the labels-pricing cart item introduced: `DESIGN_FEE` meta was being set from `(float) $item->get_total()` of *whichever* order item happened to trigger the handler — correct back when only the flat fee item carried `_yp_custom_order_id`, but now the labels item carries it too, and `$order->get_items()` isn't guaranteed to iterate the fee item first, so a labels-item-first order would have recorded its label-print total as the "design fee." Now looked up explicitly by the one trait that tells the two items apart (`_yp_batch_quantity`, present only on the labels item). A CustomOrder already stuck as a draft from before this fix needs one manual nudge to pick up the retroactive fix — an admin toggling its WooCommerce order's status away and back to Processing (or Completed) fires the newly-added hook and publishes it immediately; no code/data migration needed beyond that.
- **A submitted change-request was real data (correctly saved and already displayed on the CustomOrder screen) but easy to miss, plus a genuinely missing feature — an in-admin "needs attention" notification (direct request: "can I get a notification somewhere in the admin panel when there's a new custom order or a proof change request"):** the note itself was already showing as a plain table row near the bottom of the Request Details box (six rows down, same as Style/Colors or Instructions) — technically present but with no visual weight distinguishing "the customer is waiting on you" from "background info," so it read as invisible during testing. Promoted to a `notice notice-warning` callout at the *top* of that box (reusing wp-admin's own notice styling rather than inventing new CSS), shown only while the note is still live for the current cycle (`design_in_progress` — the same status a fresh request-changes always lands back on; once staff upload a new proof and the status moves on, this callout stops appearing even though the note itself isn't erased, since it'd be stale). The Custom Orders list table gets the same "(changes requested)" flag next to the Status column for the same reason — visible without opening each order individually. The actual missing piece was the notification: a Comments/Orders-style bubble count (`class-admin-menu.php`, WordPress's own established pattern for "N things need you" — reused, not reinvented) on both the "Custom Orders" submenu and the top-level "YeffoPrint" menu, counting every CustomOrder in `design_in_progress` — deliberately one shared count rather than separate "new" vs. "changes requested" numbers, since both mean the exact same next action (upload a proof) and the count self-clears the same way either case does.
- **Bulk discount tiers now apply across a customer's whole order, not just one line item (direct request: "they can mix and match to meet that minimum to get a discount") — the admin editor for tiers already existed (threshold, "percent off"/"fixed resulting unit price"), but `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()`'s own docblock had explicitly scoped the discount to "the batch's combined quantity... total across all variants," i.e. one Template's own batch, never across *different* designs/sizes/materials in the same order:** `calculate()` gained an optional `$tier_quantity` param, separate from `$quantity` — `$quantity` still means only this line's own units (what its own `total` in the return value is computed from), `$tier_quantity` is what gets compared against the configured thresholds; omitting it keeps the old single-item behavior for any caller that doesn't have cart context. `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::combined_label_quantity()` (new) sums `TOTAL_QTY` across every label-bearing cart item — a Template batch or a Custom Order's own labels item, either one, since both share that key regardless of which design/size/material each is — and both `apply_price()` (the authoritative cart/checkout charge) and the checkout-time order-item snapshot (`class-order-item-meta.php`, unchanged — it already called `calculate_for_cart_item()` with no override, which now defaults to a fresh `combined_label_quantity()` read) pick it up automatically. The pre-add-to-cart live preview (`/pricing/calculate`) got the same treatment — it now adds whatever's already in the customer's `WC()->cart` (`wc_load_cart()` added so a fresh guest session actually has one to read) on top of the quantity being previewed, so the estimate matches what apply_price() will actually charge once it's added; `exclude_cart_item_key` (wired from configurator.js's existing `?edit=` flow) exists for the one case that would otherwise double-count — editing a batch already in the cart, where the *old* quantity is still sitting there until the edit is saved.
- **Bulk discount tiers now only ever discount the base per-label price, never a material/size upcharge (direct follow-up: "the bulk discount just adjusts the base price of the label. If the customer uses more expensive material or a bigger label, those charges need to be added on top."):** `calculate()` previously computed the discount from `$unit_price_before_discount` (base + material + size combined) — a percent-off tier shaved a share off the upcharges too, and a `fixed_unit_price` tier could force the *entire* per-label price (upcharges included) down to one flat number regardless of which material/size was picked, which is exactly what this request rules out. The discount is now computed from, and subtracted from, `$base` alone; material/size adjustments are added back on top of the *discounted* base afterward, always at full price, regardless of which tier applies or how large it is. Renamed the tier-type labels to match the new, narrower meaning (`Percent off base price`, `Fixed resulting base price` — the stored `fixed_unit_price` array key is unchanged, so existing saved tiers keep working under the new math with no migration).
- **Homepage redesign pass (direct feedback: "the misaligned cards are still not fixed even on the latest commit... too much blank space... more colorful, more modern... the logo in the header is tiny"):**
  - **Header logo:** `wp:site-logo`'s `width` attribute was `40` (px) with `.yp-header__logo img{max-height:32px}` on top — a real, simple bug, not a design opinion; bumped to `width:180` / `max-height:48px` (38px on mobile).
  - **Card-grid saga, round 5 — escalated to `!important` plus a defensive `<li>` reset, and flagged as likely no longer a CSS bug at all:** four previous rounds each found and fixed a real, distinct, verified-correct CSS bug (sparse grid tracks, `1fr` stretch, an `align-items` regression caught and reverted same-session, a `position:static` image, an aspect-ratio specificity conflict) — and the live site still shows the same symptom after the latest of those shipped. Re-read every rule touching `.yp-card-grid`/`.yp-template-card*` fresh, start to finish, and found nothing further to fix in the logic itself. Added `!important` to the grid's own `display`/`grid-template-columns`/`justify-content` (closing off "beaten by an unknown competing rule" as a possibility, the same class of bug the header-nav fix turned out to be) and a defensive `.yp-card-grid > li { margin:0; width:auto }` reset (core/post-template's actual grid *item* is the `<li>`, one level above `.yp-template-card` — never directly inspected before). If this still doesn't resolve on the next deploy, the CSS itself should no longer be the first suspect: the two live explanations neither confirmed nor ruled out are (a) a full-page cache serving stale HTML independent of correctly-deployed source (this store has a confirmed caching layer — it caused the earlier "Add to Cart doesn't work" report), and (b) the Site Editor storing a customized copy of a template/pattern in the database that permanently shadows the theme file the moment it's ever opened and saved in Appearance → Editor (raised as a live possibility once already, never confirmed either way) — both need checking directly against the live site/host, which isn't visible from this repo.
  - **Materials section had zero actual color — every finish rendered as the identical flat light-gray box, independent of the "more colorful" ask, this was close to a bug:** `patterns/materials.php` now carries a slug + one-line blurb per material; `patterns.css` gives each its own representative gradient (a soft glossy-white sheen via a diagonal highlight overlay, a flat matte-white, an animated-gradient holographic rainbow, a dashed-border "clear" treatment, a brushed-metal gradient) instead of one shared swatch style.
  - **How It Works got a small inline SVG icon per step** (grid/gallery, sliders, printer — matching each step's existing cyan/magenta/yellow color coding) **instead of a bare numeral**, and the Hero got a compact three-item trust-stat row (48-hour turnaround / no minimum order / studio-grade print quality, each with a CMYK-colored dot) directly under the buttons — both add real visual weight and color to sections that were previously closer to bare text, addressing "sloppy... too much blank space" more directly than a padding change alone would.
  - **Section rhythm tightened**: `.yp-section`'s `padding-block` (shared token, also used by the footer) dropped from the `--spacing-xl` token (4rem) to a section-local `3rem`; the Hero's own padding tightened similarly — paired with the added content density above rather than relied on alone, since shrinking whitespace without adding anything to fill it would read as cramped, not "cleaner."
  - **"View all designs →" promoted from a plain text link to a small white pill button** (`.yp-view-all-link`) matching the elevated-card visual language used elsewhere on the page, for the same "sloppy" complaint.
  - Deliberately did **not** rebuild the theme on Elementor or any page builder — explicit ask ("I'd rather not") — everything above stays within the existing block-theme/pattern-file architecture.
- **A different, genuine height-mismatch bug in How It Works (direct report, after the .yp-card-grid fix was confirmed resolved everywhere else: "the first card is still slightly taller than the rest") — WordPress's own default blockGap spacing, not the same class of bug as the Template card saga at all:** `.yp-steps` is a Group block with `layout:"default"` (flow), which makes WordPress auto-inject vertical spacing as `margin-block-start` on every child *except the first* — correct for a normal stacked layout, but `.yp-steps` is actually a 3-column CSS grid (our own `gap` already spaces the columns), so that injected margin was landing on cards 2 and 3 only. With `align-items: stretch`, each card's box stretches to fill the shared row height *minus its own margin* — card 1 (nothing to subtract) ends up visibly taller than 2 and 3 (which lose height to that unwanted top margin) even though nothing else differs between them. Fixed by zeroing `margin-block-start`/`-end` on `.yp-steps`'s direct children — safe since spacing is already fully handled by the grid's own `gap`, so that margin was never doing anything but causing this.
- **Six-item bug/feature list, all landed together:**
  - **Material images are now admin-uploadable, with a hover swap (direct request: "upload what the material actually looks like... on hover, switch to a second picture that shows what the material looks like on the vial"):** the swatch photo is Material's existing native featured image (already there, just never actually used as a photo — the homepage section rendered CSS gradients instead); a new "Hover / on-vial image" field (`YeffoPrint_Commerce_Record_Meta::HOVER_IMAGE`, a plain attachment-ID meta field, reusing the same generic wp.media picker script — `vial-mockup-picker.js` — Template's Vial mockup field and the Proof editor already use) is the second photo. `patterns/materials.php` was rewritten to query real published `yp_material` records instead of a hardcoded array, with the same hover-swap opacity-crossfade CSS pattern already established for Template gallery cards. A Material without an uploaded photo yet falls back to the gradient swatch treatment from the previous pass (keyed by the record's own slug — matches the demo-seeded Glossy White/Matte White/Holographic/Clear/Metallic records), not a plain gray box, so "no photo yet" doesn't look broken.
  - **A Template's own description (the native post editor content, already a supported field, never actually surfaced anywhere) now shows under the configurator's preview image** (direct request) — `class-template-schema-controller.php`'s schema response gained a `description` field (rendered through `the_content` before stripping tags, since `post_content` is raw block markup, not display-ready text), and `configurator.js` populates a new `<p data-yp-description>` under the stage.
  - **Mobile hamburger overlay was white-on-white — the previous fix's CSS selectors were real but apparently never matching anything live, not a repeat of a bug already fixed:** rather than guess at another selector, switched to the Navigation block's own dedicated `overlayBackgroundColor`/`overlayTextColor` attributes (`parts/header.html`) — WordPress's actual, native mechanism for styling the mobile overlay specifically (it's legitimately allowed to differ from the inline nav's own colors), applied by core directly to whatever DOM structure it renders the overlay as. The most likely explanation for the previous CSS not matching: the open overlay isn't actually a descendant of `.yp-header__nav` in the rendered DOM (a portaled/modal-style panel) — the old class-scoped rules are kept as a defensive fallback, not removed, in case some part of the overlay the color attributes don't reach still needs them.
  - **Vial View still showed gray bars after the container itself was already correctly squared off in an earlier pass — the box wasn't the problem, `object-fit: contain` was:** `contain` never crops but always letterboxes any mismatch between the box's aspect ratio and the uploaded photo's own (real vial/bottle product photography is essentially never exactly square); switched to `object-fit: cover` for the vial background specifically (Label View keeps `contain`, since that artwork carries text that must never be cropped off — a product photo cropping slightly is normal, cropped-off label text is not). If a specific photo crops awkwardly under `cover`, the fix is re-cropping that source image closer to square, not the CSS — no single `object-fit` value is correct for every possible uploaded aspect ratio without knowing it in advance.
  - **New `color` field type (direct request: "I need the ability to add a custom field with a color picker... I know I can do text boxes, but I need the color picker option"):** added to `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::TYPES` — the admin repeater's Type dropdown is already fully data-driven from that constant, so no admin JS changes were needed there. The field's value is sanitized as a hex color everywhere a value is read or written (the schema's own `default`, and `sanitize_variants()`'s customer-submitted value) rather than as free text. On the storefront, the customer's control is a native `<input type="color">` instead of a text box; on the configurator stage, the value renders as a small circular swatch positioned like any other field (a hex string as literal text would look wrong there) — `fitText()`'s font-size-fitting logic is skipped entirely for this type since a swatch has a fixed size, nothing to shrink-to-fit.
  - **Text alignment never actually did anything visually beyond wrapping — the real, previously-undiagnosed cause of "I have the text set to left justified, but it still looks centered, it's definitely not starting where I place the location":** every field's box was positioned via `transform: translate(-50%, -50%)` unconditionally, meaning the box's *center* — not the alignment-appropriate edge — was always what anchored to `position.x/y`, so `text-align: left` only changed how text wrapped inside a box that never stopped being centered on the drag-picker's point. `configurator.js` now computes the anchor per field's alignment (`anchorTransformFor()`): left anchors the box's left edge at x (`translateX(0%)`), right anchors its right edge (`translateX(-100%)`), center is unchanged. This also had to absorb the Vial View scale-down (previously a separate CSS rule) into the same inline `transform`, since setting `transform` inline from JS overrides a class-level CSS rule outright rather than combining with it.
- **Reusable Field Presets, and the existing per-field tooltip capability made actually discoverable (direct request: "creating them one by one every time is a lot" + "I'd like the ability to add a little tooltip or descriptive text"):** the tooltip half already existed — every field already had an `admin_description` that rendered under its input on the storefront form — but it was labeled "Admin description" in the editor (reads as an internal-only note, not "this becomes customer-facing help text") and rendered as a permanently-visible paragraph, not anything resembling a tooltip. Relabeled to "Tooltip / help text (shown to the customer next to this field)" and rendered on the storefront as an actual toggleable tooltip (a small `?` button next to the label, `aria-expanded`-tracked, revealing the text on click) instead of always-on text. The reuse half was genuinely missing: a new `yp_field_preset` CPT stores a field_schema in the exact same shape/meta key a Template's own uses (a preset *is* a field_schema, just not attached to any one Template) — admins build a reusable set of field definitions once (label, type, max length, alignment, font sizing, formatting rule, tooltip text) on its own "Field Presets" screen (reusing the Template editor's entire field-schema.js repeater UI as-is, since the shape is identical), then insert it into any Template's own field list from a new "Insert from preset" dropdown+button there. Deliberately excludes position from what's reused: a field's placement is inherently tied to the specific artwork it's being dragged onto, so an inserted field still needs the existing drag-to-position picker per-Template, same as a field added by hand — only the field *definitions* are what's actually redundant to rebuild each time. Duplicate field ids between an inserted preset and a Template's existing fields aren't de-duplicated client-side on insert; `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::sanitize()` already does that unconditionally on save (suffixing `-2`, `-3`, …), so there was no reason to duplicate that logic in JS.
- **Venmo/Zelle checkout with automatic payment matching (direct request: "automatically recognize when I receive a Venmo payment, match the amount, and update the order status"):** the "notify the site" half fundamentally needs a bridge outside this codebase — nothing here can read the admin's personal inbox — so this is deliberately split into a site side that's fully built and a documented, external one-time setup (`docs/venmo-zelle-setup.md`: a Gmail filter + a small Google Apps Script the admin owns, polling on a timer and POSTing to a webhook when it finds a new payment email; Zapier/Power Automate would work identically against the same endpoint for a non-Gmail inbox).
  - **Two new checkout gateways** (`YeffoPrint_Venmo_Gateway`, `YeffoPrint_Zelle_Gateway`), sharing a base class (`YeffoPrint_Manual_Payment_Gateway`) since they're identical except id/copy — same shape as WooCommerce's own core BACS/Cheque gateways: `process_payment()` just sets the order to **on-hold** and reduces stock, no real payment processing happens in PHP at all. Each gateway's own WooCommerce settings screen shows its ready-to-paste webhook URL (token included) directly, so there's no separate settings screen to build.
  - **Real class-loading gotcha, caught before it shipped**: unlike every other class in this plugin, `YeffoPrint_Manual_Payment_Gateway extends \WC_Payment_Gateway` — a class *declaration*, resolved the instant the file is `require_once`'d, not a lazy reference inside a method body the way this plugin references WooCommerce everywhere else. Requiring it eagerly during this plugin's own bootstrap (same as every other file) risks a fatal error if WooCommerce's class hasn't loaded yet, depending on plugin load order — `Requires Plugins: woocommerce` (yeffoprint-core.php's own header) narrows but doesn't fully rule this out. Fixed by loading the three gateway files only from inside the `woocommerce_payment_gateways` filter callback itself — a filter WooCommerce only ever calls from deep inside its own fully-booted payment-gateway registry, so it can never fire before `WC_Payment_Gateway` exists.
  - **A dedicated bearer-token webhook** (`POST /yeffoprint-core/v1/payments/notify`, `class-payment-webhook-controller.php`) — there's no WordPress session on the caller side (an external automation, not a logged-in browser), so a long random secret in the URL (`YeffoPrint_Payment_Webhook_Secret`, same generate-once-store-in-an-option pattern as the Custom Order proof-approval token) is the entire access control, checked with `hash_equals()`.
  - **Matching is deliberately conservative, prioritizing "never guess wrong" over "always auto-resolve":** an order number found in the payment note (validated against that exact order's own gateway/status/amount) resolves with high confidence; otherwise, exactly one on-hold order at that gateway+amount resolves; zero or more than one candidate resolves *nothing* automatically and emails the admin instead (with direct edit links for any candidates) — marking the wrong order paid is a materially worse outcome (shipping unpaid product, a real payment left unmatched) than asking a human to break a same-amount tie by hand. A match calls `$order->payment_complete()`, not a direct status write — the same call already used elsewhere in this codebase, which is what correctly fires the existing Custom Order payment-linking hooks too (class-custom-order-payment.php) when the matched order happens to be a Custom Design order, so both order types resolve through the identical path with no special-casing needed here.
- **Venmo/Zelle enabled and saved correctly but never showed at checkout — root cause found via live diagnosis (view-source confirmed present in the initial server-rendered HTML, but no `update_order_review` AJAX request ever fired, and view-source contained "wc-block-checkout"): this site's Checkout page uses the WooCommerce Checkout *block* (Store API), not the classic `[woocommerce_checkout]` shortcode this theme's own `woocommerce.css` was written against (`#order_review`, `.col2-set` — classic-only selectors, now confirmed dead CSS on the live checkout page, a separate, not-yet-addressed follow-up if the checkout's styling ever needs attention).** A classic `WC_Payment_Gateway` registration (`woocommerce_payment_gateways` filter, already correct — confirmed via wp-admin settings working, the gateway's HTML present on initial load) has no bearing on the block-based Checkout at all; it needs a *separate* registration with WooCommerce Blocks' own client-side payment method registry. Core WooCommerce gateways (BACS, Cheque, COD) ship this second registration built into WooCommerce itself, which is exactly why "other gateways show fine" while a brand-new custom one didn't — this was a real gap in what shipped originally, not a site configuration issue. Added `YeffoPrint_Manual_Payment_Blocks_Support` (extends `Automattic\WooCommerce\Blocks\Payments\Integrations\AbstractPaymentMethodType`, hooked via `woocommerce_blocks_payment_method_type_registration`) plus a small vanilla-JS file per gateway (`assets/blocks/{venmo,zelle}-payment-method.js`, using `window.wc.wcBlocksRegistry.registerPaymentMethod` — no build step, matching every other JS file in this project) that the block's own frontend actually reads to list the option. Same lazy-`require_once`-inside-the-hook-callback pattern as the classic gateway registration, and for the identical reason: `AbstractPaymentMethodType` is also a class-declaration-time dependency (`extends`), not a lazy method-body reference, so the file can only safely load from inside a hook WooCommerce Blocks itself only ever calls once its own classes exist.
- **Cart/Checkout block styling (direct follow-up to the Venmo/Zelle investigation confirming this site's Cart/Checkout pages use the WooCommerce Blocks, not the classic shortcodes this file was originally written against):** added decorative styling — color, radius, shadow, spacing, matching every other card/section treatment already established elsewhere on the site — for the documented, stable WooCommerce Blocks theming class API (`wc-block-components-checkout-step`, `-radio-control__option`, `-totals-item`/`-totals-footer-item`, `-panel`, `-notice-banner`, `wc-block-cart-items`, etc.), deliberately layered *on top of* WC Blocks' own structural/layout CSS rather than rebuilding the grid/column layout from scratch — that base CSS already works correctly and isn't visible to verify a rebuild against from here, so only decoration was touched, not structure. The "selected" radio-option state (shipping method and payment method both use the same RadioControl component, so this covers Venmo/Zelle's own selected-state styling too) uses `[aria-checked="true"]` rather than a guessed BEM modifier class name, since the component is accessibility-built on a `role="radio"` pattern that's far more certain to be correct than an exact modifier suffix would be. Flagged plainly to the user that this is a best-effort pass against WooCommerce's documented API rather than something verified against the live render — a screenshot is the next step if anything doesn't land pixel-perfect, same honest caveat as every other "can't see the live site" situation in this project.
- **Checkout/shipping payment method labels overlapping their radio circles ("USPS Ground Advantage" reading as "PS Ground Advantage," "Venmo" as "nmo"), plus the rest of the Cart/Checkout block styling not visibly landing — both from a live screenshot, both real bugs in the pass that just shipped:** the label overlap was a genuine, findable-from-the-screenshot bug: `.wc-block-components-radio-control__option`'s rule used the `padding` *shorthand*, which resets all four sides — that silently collapsed WooCommerce Blocks' own larger `padding-left` (reserved for the absolutely-positioned radio circle sitting in front of the label) down to a flat `1rem`, not enough room to clear the circle. Fixed by only setting the sides this section actually needs to change (`padding-block` + `padding-right`), leaving `padding-left` exactly as WC Blocks itself sets it. The "nothing else styled" complaint got the same `!important` treatment as every other place on this site where a plain single-class rule has lost to WooCommerce/WordPress's own competing CSS (header nav, mobile overlay, icon buttons, the color-picker field) — same pattern, applied here for the first time to this whole section rather than case-by-case as each one gets reported.
- **Header nav links were invisible against the dark header — a real, previously-undiagnosed root cause, not a repeat of an already-fixed bug:** direct report ("the color of the links is dark and the header is dark so you cannot see the navigation menu") after an earlier pass had already given `.yp-header__nav a.wp-block-navigation-item__content` an explicit `color: var(--warm-white)` pill treatment — that rule was correct but losing. `theme.json`'s global `styles.elements.link` sets every plain link's text color to `near-black` (`#141414`) — effectively identical to this header's own background (`rgba(20, 20, 20, .92)`) — and WordPress compiles that into global CSS with enough specificity/print-order to beat a plain `class + element` selector here, so the nav (and the search/account/cart `.yp-icon-button` icons, same failure mode: also plain `<a>`/`<button>` elements with a one-class color rule) were rendering near-black-on-near-black. Fixed with a scoped `!important` on exactly these two selectors (nav links + `.yp-icon-button`, including their hover/mobile-overlay variants) — overriding one global rule for these specific header elements, not disabling `theme.json`'s link color site-wide, where it's an intentional, correct default for body copy.

## Custom Stickers (PROJECT_SPEC §19 non-goal, now in scope)

Direct answers on the three open questions before building: pricing is preset size tiers (not a pure $/sq-in formula, not a manual-quote flow), the order flow requires a staff-uploaded proof before production (reusing the CustomOrder/Proof machinery Custom Design already validated), and "Custom Shape" means contour-cut to the artwork's own outline — no separate cut-path upload. Built in stages like every other phase here; this entry covers stage one (data model + admin), landed before any customer-facing form exists yet.

- **`yp_custom_order` gets a `type` field rather than a parallel CPT — the single biggest reuse decision:** `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Meta::ORDER_TYPE` (`'label'`, the default every pre-existing record implicitly has since the meta row doesn't exist yet, or `'sticker'`) means Custom Stickers inherits payment gating (`post_status` draft→publish on paid), the six-state proof pipeline, guest access tokens, `YeffoPrint_Secure_Upload`'s validated uploads, the Proofs My Account tab, and Reorder — all of it, unmodified — exactly the "generic infrastructure... so Custom Stickers can reuse it rather than needing a parallel system" §8 called for. `SIZE_ID`/`MATERIAL_ID`/`QUANTITY` are reused as literal meta keys for both flows too (a size/material/quantity is that regardless of product; `SIZE_ID` points at a `yp_size` post for a label order and a `yp_sticker_size` post for a sticker one — every reader already has to branch on `ORDER_TYPE` anyway). New sticker-only meta: `STICKER_TYPE` (sheet/die-cut), `SHAPE`, `CUSTOM_WIDTH_IN`/`CUSTOM_HEIGHT_IN` (only meaningful when `SIZE_ID` points at the custom tier), and `ARTWORK_UPLOADS` — deliberately a separate field from `INSPIRATION_UPLOADS`, since a sticker order's file is the actual print-ready artwork, not a label order's "here's what I'm going for" reference image.
- **A new `yp_sticker_size` CPT, not a reuse of `yp_size` — the one place reuse was rejected, on purpose:** `yp_size.price_adjustment` is a small delta on top of a shared $0.35/label base; a sticker size tier's price *is* the base for that sticker, an order-of-magnitude different meaning. Reusing the same field/record type would have blurred two distinct pricing models together the exact way §9's Phase 6 entry warns against duplicating a fact across records. One tier can be flagged `is_custom` (`YeffoPrint_Sticker_Size_Meta::IS_CUSTOM`) — the admin editor enforces at most one by clearing the flag on every other record on save, so `get_custom_tier_id()` never has to pick among several. This is how "preset tiers" (the chosen pricing model) still prices a genuinely custom dimension live, at checkout, before the proof step: the customer types exact inches on that one tier, and price is computed from an admin-set $/sq-in rate instead of a stored flat price — reconciling "preset tiers" with "sizes can be very custom" rather than treating them as a contradiction.
- **`YeffoPrint_Sticker_Pricing` is a separate formula from `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()`, not a reuse — same reasoning as the CPT split above:** the label formula's "base" is one global per-label constant with material/size as small adjustments; a sticker's base is already the size tier's own (fixed or computed) price. Calling the label formula with a sticker's numbers would either silently add the label's $0.35 onto every sticker or require awkwardly zeroing it out. Kept the same *shape* deliberately though — `(size base − discount) + material/type/shape adjustments, × quantity`, discount computed from the base only and never allowed to shave an adjustment, bulk tiers resolved against a combined quantity — because those are the pricing *principles* this site already committed to (PROJECT_SPEC §12, the Phase 6 "discount never touches an upcharge" direct request), not something specific to labels. Sticker bulk-discount tiers are a second, independent tier list (`YeffoPrint_Sticker_Pricing::META_TIERS`) evaluated only against combined sticker quantity — a sticker order never counts toward the label discount threshold or vice versa, since the two products sit at completely different price points and mixing thresholds would misprice one or the other.
- **All sticker pricing knobs live as new meta on the same "one active" `yp_pricing_rule` post, not a second Pricing Rule CPT:** one Pricing Rules screen for the whole site (base/fee/label tiers, now also the custom-size rate, sheet/die-cut and shape dollar adjustments, and sticker tiers), matching `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::get_active_rule_id()`'s existing "exactly one active rule" model rather than introducing a second single-record CPT with its own active-record logic to duplicate. The sticker bulk-tier repeater UI is a second, independent copy of `assets/admin/pricing-tiers.js` (`sticker-pricing-tiers.js`) rather than a second load of the same file — the original hardcodes one global config name and one set of DOM ids, so it can't run twice against two different tier lists on the same screen; matches this project's general preference for a small dedicated script per concern over a shared component abstracted before it's needed twice for the same purpose.
- **Materials are shared with a new scope flag, not split into a separate sticker Material CPT:** vinyl and holographic genuinely apply to both product lines; duplicating them as separate records risks the two drifting out of sync the way §4's Phase 4 entry already ruled out for `compatible_products`. `YeffoPrint_Commerce_Record_Meta::SCOPE` (`label` / `sticker` / `both`, defaulting to `label`) reuses the existing Material CPT, admin screen, image/description/price fields wholesale — new sticker-only materials (glitter, kraft, clear) just get created there with a different scope. `get_materials_for( $for )` centralizes the query (an explicit `NOT EXISTS` fallback to `'label'` only, so every pre-existing Material keeps working in the flow it already worked in without a migration, and never silently starts appearing in the sticker flow it was never reviewed for) — `class-custom-order-controller.php`'s existing label-form `options()` endpoint was updated to call it too, closing a real regression risk: without that fix, the first sticker-only Material an admin ever creates would have silently leaked into the label Custom Design form's picker.
- **What's deliberately not built yet:** the customer-facing `/custom-stickers/` form (still the Phase 10 "coming soon" placeholder), its REST submission endpoint, the hidden linked WooCommerce product(s) a sticker order would add to cart (mirroring `YeffoPrint_Custom_Design_Fee_Product`/`YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Labels_Product`), and the cart-display (`YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::display_item_data()`) branch for a sticker line item. None of that is reachable yet — no sticker `CustomOrder` can be created until the submission endpoint exists — so this stage is safe and independently reviewable on its own, matching the phased-validation discipline the V1 and V2 builds both followed rather than shipping the whole feature in one unreviewed pass.

## QR code label field (direct request)

A new `qr_code` Template field type (`YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::TYPES`, alongside `text`/`textarea`/`color`) — a customer types a URL into the field, the configurator renders an actual scannable QR code in the live preview at that field's position, and staff can pull a print-ready PNG or PDF straight from the order screen for whichever URL a customer entered ("so I don't need to use a 3rd party site").

- **The QR encoder is a faithful line-for-line PHP port of Project Nayuki's reference `qrcodegen` library (MIT), not an independent implementation from the ISO/IEC 18004 spec:** `includes/lib/class-qr-code-gen.php`. A QR encoder is exactly the kind of code where a subtle bug (wrong Reed–Solomon generator polynomial, an off-by-one in the module-placement zigzag) produces something that still *looks* like a QR code but doesn't scan — unacceptable on a physical print product — so this stays a port of well-tested reference logic rather than something reasoned out from the spec by hand. Verified by cross-checking the PHP port's module grid, byte-for-byte, against the upstream Python reference (`pip install qrcodegen`, same author/algorithm) across ~70 cases spanning all four error-correction levels, all three relevant encoding modes (numeric/alphanumeric/byte, auto-selected same as upstream), version boundaries, and randomized fuzz inputs up to the byte-mode capacity ceiling — every case matched exactly after one real bug was found and fixed this way (see next entry). Not vendored as a Composer package — this plugin has no build-step/dependency-manager infrastructure anywhere (same reasoning as Custom Stickers' pricing engine staying hand-rolled rather than pulling in a library), and a single well-documented ~900-line file is easy to keep in one place and audit against the upstream original.
- **A real bug the cross-check caught, worth naming since it's a general Python→PHP translation trap:** the module-placement zigzag loop mutates its own loop-control variable inside the body (`if right <= 6: right -= 1`) to compute where to draw. In Python, `for right in range(size-1, 0, -2)` iterates a pre-materialized sequence, so that mutation has zero effect on which value comes next. PHP's C-style `for` re-reads the *live* variable on every step, so reusing it for both the loop control and the body's adjusted value silently corrupted the step size whenever that line fired — a version-2 symbol drew 550 modules instead of the correct 600, with the deficit scattered unpredictably through the data region rather than failing loudly. Fixed by giving the loop a dedicated control variable (`$right_raw`) and computing the adjusted value as a separate local (`$right`) each iteration, mirroring Python's actual semantics instead of its surface syntax. This is the reason the byte-for-byte cross-check existed at all rather than trusting a careful read-through — the ported code looked correct on inspection.
- **PNG rendering uses GD (`class-qr-renderer.php`), always black-on-white with no color options:** a colored or low-contrast QR code is a real scan-reliability risk on a physical product a customer's phone camera has to read under ordinary lighting — not a design choice worth exposing, unlike Material swatches or brand colors elsewhere on the site.
- **PDF rendering is a minimal hand-built single-page PDF, not a pulled-in PDF library — for the same "no dependency infrastructure" reason as the encoder itself, but with much lower correctness risk:** one FlateDecode 1-bit `DeviceGray` image XObject placed on a page sized to exactly the requested physical dimensions (no extra margin/layout — the file is a ready-to-place crop). Unlike QR encoding, a single embedded raster on one page is simple enough to construct correctly by hand from the base PDF spec. Verified two ways: `file` correctly identifies the output as "PDF document, version 1.4, 1 page(s)", and — the real test — PyMuPDF (a production PDF engine, no relation to this project) opened and rendered it, then every rendered pixel was compared against the same ground-truth QR grid the PNG was checked against; zero mismatches. The PNG path got the identical pixel-for-pixel check via Pillow.
- **One public REST endpoint (`GET /yeffoprint-core/v1/qr`, `class-qr-controller.php`) serves both the live preview and the admin download — not two separate endpoints:** QR rendering is a pure function of its input text, tied to no customer/order/auth context, so the configurator's `<img>` tag and the order screen's "Download PNG/PDF" links point at the exact same route (the latter with `download=1` for a `Content-Disposition: attachment` response) rather than needing a second, admin-only code path. Same reasoning as the existing public `/pricing/calculate` endpoint. Rate-limited per IP (10 min / 60 requests) and capped at 500 input characters before it ever reaches the encoder, mirroring the Phase 10 audit's rate limit on the unauthenticated custom-order upload endpoint — real CPU cost on an unauthenticated route is the same abuse shape either way.
- **The configurator debounces the live preview per QR field (400ms, keyed by field id), not per keystroke:** re-fetching a rendered QR image on every keystroke of a URL would be wasteful and would flicker mid-type; a field showing a dashed placeholder box (`.is-qr.is-empty`) until at least 4 characters are typed avoids firing requests for an obviously-incomplete URL. Matches the existing `pricingDebounceTimer` pattern in shape, keyed by field id (not a single shared timer) since a schema could in principle carry more than one QR field.
- **QR field values are validated as real URLs, not free text — `esc_url_raw()` + `wp_http_validate_url()` everywhere a value is read or written (`YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::sanitize()` and `sanitize_variants()`), same pattern as `color`'s `sanitize_hex_color()`:** restricting to the `http`/`https` schemes specifically (not just "well-formed URI") closes off a `javascript:`/`data:` URL ending up encoded into a QR code and then decoded by some downstream scanner/app in a context that trusts it — confirmed rejected by the same test pass that validated the valid-URL and required-field paths.
- **Order-screen download links reuse `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::format_variant_summary()`'s sibling data path, not a new lookup:** `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::add_qr_download_links()` hooks `woocommerce_order_item_get_formatted_meta_data`, reading field types from the order's own frozen `_yp_template_snapshot.field_schema` rather than the Template's current schema — same "what was actually purchased" snapshot principle Phase 7 already established for pricing/size/material, so a QR field type changed or removed after the fact doesn't affect an already-placed order's download links. **Real bug found and fixed:** this was originally gated on plain `is_admin()`, on the assumption that meant "staff looking at the order screen." It doesn't — `is_admin()` is also true for admin-post.php/admin-ajax.php requests, which is exactly how a manual order-status change (the order screen's own "Update" button) or the "Resend order emails" bulk action actually trigger WooCommerce's transactional emails. That meant these staff-only download links leaked straight into the customer's real order email whenever the email happened to fire from inside wp-admin, while staying correctly hidden for the identical status change made from checkout or the Venmo/Zelle payment webhook. Fixed by scoping the check to the actual order-edit screen id (`is_order_edit_screen()`: classic `shop_order`, HPOS `woocommerce_page_wc-orders`) instead of the blanket "are we anywhere in wp-admin" check — the orders *list* screen, where the resend bulk action runs, has a different screen id and no longer matches. (This turned out to be a real bug worth fixing on its own, but not the cause of the "can't send test email" report below — that one took a live stack trace to actually find.)
- **The real cause of "WooCommerce Settings → Emails → 'Send a test email' fails whenever yeffoprint-core is active," found from a live stack trace after the fix above didn't resolve it:** `YeffoPrint_Reorder::render_reorder_link()` (`class-reorder.php`) type-hinted its `$item_id` parameter as `int`. It's hooked to WooCommerce's own `woocommerce_order_item_meta_end` action, fired from `woocommerce/templates/emails/email-order-items.php` — used to render every order-related email, the "Send a test email" preview included. That template passes the item id through as a **string**, not an int, so PHP threw an uncaught `TypeError` ("Argument #1 ($item_id) must be of type int, string given") on every single order email render, real or preview. WooCommerce's email-preview REST controller wraps rendering in a `catch (\Throwable $e)` and reports that as a generic "couldn't send the test email" failure — which is also exactly what a real customer order email would have done, uncaught, since nothing in the normal send path catches a `TypeError` the way the preview endpoint does. `$item_id` is only ever used in string concatenation in this method, so it never needed to be typed `int` in the first place — the type hint was pure liability with no upside. Fixed by dropping it. Root-cause note for next time: a strict scalar type hint on a parameter WordPress/WooCommerce itself supplies via `do_action()`/`apply_filters()` is inherently risky — nothing enforces that a core hook's documented "this is always an int" holds through every one of its own call sites, and there's no reason for our own callback to be stricter than the data actually requires.
- **"Every page shows homepage content" (live-site incident, not a code bug) — root cause was Settings → Permalinks silently reverted to "Plain," almost certainly during a pre-launch content cleanup pass that also deleted every Page:** worth logging since nothing about the symptom pointed at permalinks directly, and it cost a long diagnostic pass (browser/CDN cache, DB template overrides, deleted pages, rogue plugins — all real things to check, all ruled out one at a time) before the actual cause surfaced. The tell, once found: page source showed REST/AJAX URLs in the fallback `index.php?rest_route=...`/`?wc-ajax=...` form instead of the pretty `/wp-json/...` form — the giveaway that WordPress had no rewrite rules mapping *any* pretty path to anything, so every request (including `/wp-json/` itself, which is why it returned homepage HTML instead of JSON) fell through to WordPress's default query, the front page. Recreating the deleted pages didn't help because the pages were never the problem — the URL structure that would have pointed *at* them was gone. Fixed by switching Permalinks back to "Post name" and saving (which is a different action than just re-saving Permalinks with "Plain" still selected — that only reflushes the *current*, still-broken structure, which is why an early "try re-saving Permalinks" suggestion didn't resolve it). No code changed for this one.
- **Gallery card hover-swap removed (direct follow-up to the vial-mockup-leads change already logged above):** once the card's media box became sized/shaped for a standing vial photo rather than the flat label's 15:7 rectangle, the hover-revealed label image read as a badly-cropped strip inside that box — worse than not showing it at all. `blocks/template-card/render.php` now renders a single image (vial mockup, falling back to flat artwork only if no mockup is uploaded) with no hover state; `assets/css/patterns.css` drops the `--primary`/`--hover` opacity-crossfade rule pair accordingly. The flat label artwork itself isn't gone from the site — it's still exactly what the configurator's Label View on the actual template page renders; it's just no longer paired with the vial photo on gallery/homepage cards.

## Branded order emails + order tracking (direct request)

Two follow-ups to the WooCommerce email work above: reskin every stock WooCommerce email with the site's own look, and give a customer a branded page to track their shipment instead of a bare carrier link.

- **Every WooCommerce email now shares one skin, not eleven separately-styled ones:** `yeffoprint/woocommerce/emails/email-header.php`, `email-footer.php`, and `email-styles.php` override WooCommerce's own defaults — CMY brand stripe, near-black header/footer bands with the site's wordmark, a rounded 16px card, and colors hardcoded from the theme's own palette rather than sourced from Settings → Emails' color pickers (a fixed design decision, not a per-store setting). The eleven content templates (`admin-new-order.php` and the rest) are also copied into that same directory per WooCommerce's documented override pattern, wording unchanged from WooCommerce's stock copy — they exist so each email's copy is easy to find and edit later, not because anything in them needed to change. Fonts fall back to Helvetica/Arial rather than the site's own Geist/Inter, since most inboxes strip custom web fonts entirely. The store's mailing address was deliberately dropped from the footer (direct request) — it no longer echoes `woocommerce_email_footer_text` at all.
- **Order Tracking reads the *existing* "WooCommerce Shipping" plugin's own label data (`wc_connect_labels` order meta) rather than adding a second place to enter a tracking number:** `YeffoPrint_Order_Tracking::get_shipments()` (`class-order-tracking.php`) normalizes that plugin's label array into `{carrier_id, carrier_label, tracking_number, carrier_url}`, skipping any label without a tracking number yet (purchased but not yet scanned) or marked refunded/voided. Staff keep buying labels exactly as they do today; nothing new to fill in.
- **The tracking page needs no new guest-access token:** unlike the proof-approval flow's own generated `ACCESS_TOKEN` (that record has no other identity a URL could carry), a WC order already has WooCommerce's own `order_key` — the same secret its native "View order"/"Pay for order" links already trust for guest checkout — so `yeffoprint_core_tracking_url()` reuses it instead of minting a second secret to generate/store/rotate. `class-order-tracking-controller.php`'s `check_access()` is the same shape as `class-proof-approval-controller.php`'s: key match for a guest, nonce + ownership/staff check for a logged-in session.
- **A "Track your order" button, not a clickable order number:** the literal ask was making the order number in the email clickable, but that text is rendered by WooCommerce's own shared `emails/email-order-details.php` partial (used by every email type, and one of the templates WooCommerce revises most often — three separate filters added to it since 10.6.0 alone) — overriding that whole file just to link one string is a real version-drift risk for something a dedicated button already does better. `YeffoPrint_Order_Tracking::render_tracking_button()` hooks the existing `woocommerce_email_after_order_table` action instead (same hook shape `class-manual-payment-gateway.php`'s payment-instructions callout already uses one hook over from) — appears automatically on every customer-facing order email the moment a shipment exists, admin's own copy and the plain-text part handled separately, no per-template edit needed anywhere.
- **UPS/USPS live tracking is stubbed, not wired up — direct decision, credentials don't exist yet:** `tracking-providers/class-usps-tracking-provider.php` and `class-ups-tracking-provider.php` implement the real OAuth2 client-credentials token exchange (that part needs no API-specific knowledge to get right) and a best-effort read of each carrier's current published event-response schema, clearly flagged in both files' docblocks to verify against a real response once credentials exist. Every failure mode — no credentials configured, a bad token, a malformed response, a network error — throws `YeffoPrint_Tracking_Exception`, caught in `class-order-tracking-controller.php` and turned into an empty `events` array rather than a broken page; the carrier's own direct tracking-site link (`carrier_direct_url()`, needs no API key at all) is always present as the fallback the front-end shows instead. This means the tracking page and the email button both ship real value today — a direct link to the right carrier page, on the site's own branded page — with the live in-page timeline plugging in later by filling in four settings fields (Dashboard → YeffoPrint → Settings → Shipment Tracking: UPS Client ID/Secret, USPS Consumer Key/Secret), no further code changes.
- **`/track-order/` is a real WP Page provisioned by `wp yeffoprint setup-pages`, not a REST-only endpoint:** same FSE-template-plus-vanilla-JS pattern as `/proof-approval/` (`templates/track-order.html` + `assets/js/track-order.js`, hydrated from `class-order-tracking-controller.php`'s `GET /orders/{id}/tracking`), so a customer landing here from an email sees the same header/footer/type system as the rest of the site instead of a bare JSON response or an unbranded carrier page.

## Custom Stickers, stage two: the customer-facing form (direct follow-up to the "Custom Stickers" section above, which shipped stage one — data model + admin — and explicitly scoped this part out)

The four pieces that section listed as "deliberately not built yet" — the `/custom-stickers/` form, its REST submission endpoint, the hidden linked WooCommerce product, and the cart-display branch — all land together here, since none of the four is independently useful without the others.

- **No separate flat fee item, unlike Custom Design's own $25 design fee — the single biggest structural decision in this pass:** re-reading the original section's own scoping ("pricing is preset size tiers... not a manual-quote flow") turned up no mention of a separate proofing/design charge for stickers the way Custom Design's is a named, recurring detail throughout that flow. The sticker price itself is the whole charge — one cart line item (`YeffoPrint_Custom_Sticker_Product`, same hidden-anchor-product role as `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Labels_Product`, no `YeffoPrint_Custom_Design_Fee_Product` counterpart), not two. `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Meta::DESIGN_FEE` still gets written for a sticker order (`class-custom-order-payment.php`'s `find_design_fee()` sums the order's own linked item(s) for a sticker order instead of hunting for a separate no-batch-quantity fee item, which doesn't exist here) — its stored meaning is really "what unlocked production," which for stickers is just the order total; the admin screen's "Design Fee" row is relabeled "Amount Paid" for a sticker CustomOrder so it still reads correctly (`class-custom-order-editor.php`).
- **`YeffoPrint_Cart_Item_Keys::STICKER_TYPE`'s mere presence on a cart item is the discriminator every shared hook needs:** a sticker line item carries `CUSTOM_ORDER_ID` and `TOTAL_QTY`, same as a Custom Design labels item — without a marker, `class-cart-pricing.php`'s `apply_price()`/`display_item_data()` and `class-order-item-meta.php`'s `snapshot()` (all three already branching on exactly that combination for the label flow) would price and display a sticker as if it were a mispriced label batch. `STICKER_TYPE` only ever gets set by `class-custom-sticker-controller.php`, so checking it first in each of those three methods is enough to route to this flow's own logic before the label branch ever runs.
- **A second, independent bulk-discount pool, not shared with labels:** `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::combined_sticker_quantity()` mirrors `combined_label_quantity()` exactly, summing only cart items that carry `STICKER_TYPE` — the original section's own "a sticker order never counts toward the label discount threshold or vice versa" decision, now actually wired into cart calculation rather than just the pricing formula it already governed.
- **A dedicated `/pricing/calculate-sticker` endpoint, not an extension of the existing `/pricing/calculate`:** that route's own docblock calls it "deliberately template-agnostic" for the label formula specifically (size/material/quantity only); a sticker preview needs a type and shape too, plus an optional custom-dimensions pair neither label pricing has, so bolting those onto the existing route's args would have muddied a genuinely different formula. Kept in the same `class-pricing-controller.php` file regardless — one authoritative-pricing home, per that file's own stated purpose.
- **Artwork upload is required, not optional — the one real divergence from Custom Design's own form:** Custom Design's "inspiration files" are optional because staff design the label from a brief; `ARTWORK_UPLOADS` is explicitly the *print-ready* file per the original section's own field comment, and there's nothing to print without it. Reuses that flow's existing `/custom-orders/uploads` endpoint as-is rather than a duplicate — `YeffoPrint_Secure_Upload` doesn't care what a file is *for*.
- **What's still not built, on purpose:** reorder support for a Custom Stickers order (Custom Design's own reorder was a documented V2 follow-up after its base flow shipped, not part of that flow's own first pass either) — `class-reorder.php`'s existing hooks already no-op safely for a sticker line item (it has `_yp_custom_order_id` but always also `_yp_batch_quantity`, so the existing "is this the flat fee item" check correctly never matches and simply shows no reorder link, rather than a broken one) — and live UPS/USPS tracking event details specific to stickers, which need nothing extra beyond what class-order-tracking.php already ships for every order type.
- **The existing `/custom-stickers/` page won't pick up the new template on its own:** `wp yeffoprint setup-pages` never overwrites an existing page (Step-1-of-this-repo's-own-idempotent-setup-command convention), and a page at that slug already exists from stage one's "coming soon" placeholder. Whoever deploys this needs to either change that page's own Template setting in wp-admin (Pages → Custom Stickers → Page Attributes → Template → "Custom Stickers Form") or delete it and re-run the WP-CLI command — no code path does this automatically, the same way none of this project's other page-provisioning ever retroactively updates a page that already exists.

## Card surcharge (direct request: "pass on credit card fees I'm charged")

- **The one thing this deliberately can't do — exclude debit cards — isn't an oversight, it's a hard technical ceiling, and it was surfaced before writing any code:** researched live before building (Stripe's own docs, current state-law summaries) rather than built from assumption. Findings: surcharging a debit card is a federal Durbin Amendment violation, not just a card-network guideline; several states ban credit card surcharging outright (Connecticut, Massachusetts, Maine, and — contested in court — California as of a 2024 law); the surcharge is capped by card network (currently 3% Visa / 4% Mastercard, or actual processing cost if lower). Stripe's own sanctioned path for a compliant surcharge is a certified partner app (Yeeld or InterPayments) from Stripe's own App Marketplace — installed and configured directly with Stripe, not something built into WooCommerce — because only Stripe's own card element sees the real card data (credit vs. debit) at the moment it's entered; WooCommerce/PHP never sees it before that point, so nothing at this layer can conditionally skip a debit card. Presented as an explicit choice rather than silently building the noncompliant version: the store owner chose the flat-fee-in-WooCommerce path anyway, with that specific gap understood and documented in both the settings-screen copy and `class-card-surcharge.php`'s own docblock — anyone touching this code later sees the same tradeoff, not just this doc.
- **Opt-in per gateway, not a blanket "surcharge every card" switch — and a different rate per gateway, not one shared rate (direct follow-up: cards and Afterpay/BNPL gateways cost genuinely different percentages to accept):** `YeffoPrint_Admin_Menu::SURCHARGE_GATEWAY_RATES_OPTION` is one option keyed by gateway id (`[ gateway_id => ['rate' => float, 'label' => string] ]`), defaulting to an empty array — the settings screen lists every currently-registered WC payment gateway as a table row with its own rate/label inputs, so an admin sets each gateway's actual cost individually rather than the plugin guessing "not Venmo/Zelle" from gateway metadata that doesn't reliably say "this one is a card." A blank or 0 rate is the same as "not surcharged" — there's no separate on/off checkbox to keep in sync with the rate. `sanitize_surcharge_gateway_rates()` also drops any stored id that no longer matches a real registered gateway (a since-removed/renamed gateway can't leave a stale, silently-inert row behind) and drops any row whose rate comes back at or below 0, so a blanked-out row doesn't linger as a no-op entry. (First shipped as one global rate + a gateway checklist; corrected to per-gateway rates in the very next request, before the single-rate shape ever needed a real migration.)
- **Same fee mechanism `YeffoPrint_Rewards` already uses, just positive instead of negative:** `WC_Cart::add_fee()`, hooked to `woocommerce_cart_calculate_fees` — no new WooCommerce integration surface, and it gets WC's own fee display/order-item/email-totals rendering for free, the same way the Rewards discount already does. Hooked at priority 20, specifically *after* Rewards' own fee (default priority 10), so a customer redeeming points is surcharged on what they'll actually be charged, not the pre-discount amount — confirmed with a direct test (a $110 cart with a $20 Rewards discount already applied surcharges 3% of the net $90, not $110).
- **Non-taxable by design, matching several states' own guidance that a surcharge shouldn't itself be taxed** — same `add_fee(..., false)` taxable flag Rewards' own fee already uses.
- **No fee shown until a gateway is actually chosen:** reads `WC()->session->get('chosen_payment_method')`, which is empty/unset while just viewing the cart (before checkout) or before the customer has picked a payment method on the Checkout block — the fee only appears once a surcharged gateway is actually selected, which is also the point card-network rules require the disclosure to happen by (before the customer commits to paying).

## Quantity presets changed from 25/50/100/250/500/1000 to 10/20/30/50/100/250 (direct request: "customers typically order labels in increments of 10")

- **Still one function, no new admin setting:** `yeffoprint_core_quantity_presets()` (`includes/configurator/quantity-presets.php`) stays a fixed business rule, not promoted to an admin-configurable record — same reasoning as Phase 5's original entry above, just a different fixed list. Every consumer (the Template configurator, Custom Design form, Custom Stickers form) already reads it through the schema-fetch endpoints rather than a hard-coded copy, so this was a one-file change plus each form's own network-failure fallback array (`[ 25 ]` → `[ 10 ]`), not a hunt across the theme.

## Admin gets emailed on a proof decision (direct request, surfaced while answering "does staff get notified when a customer responds to a proof?" — the honest answer at the time was no)

- **`class-proof-approval-controller.php`'s `approve()`/`request_changes()` previously only ever updated post meta** — the only existing "something needs you" signal was the admin-menu bubble count (`class-admin-menu.php`, counts every CustomOrder in `design_in_progress`, direct request from an earlier pass), which a change-request response feeds into automatically (it's the same status a fresh submission lands in) but an *approval* doesn't touch at all, since `approved` was never part of that count. Two new private methods, `notify_admin_of_approval()`/`notify_admin_of_change_request()`, both routed through a `notify_admin( $subject, $body )` helper — same shape and same `get_option( 'admin_email' )` recipient as `class-payment-webhook-controller.php`'s own `notify_admin()`, not the contact form's dedicated, admin-configurable recipient option, since this is an internal alert about an existing order, not customer correspondence. Each email links straight to that CustomOrder's admin edit screen; the change-request email also includes the customer's own notes verbatim (falling back to "(No additional notes provided.)" if left blank, same as the admin editor's own display already does elsewhere). Identified by `get_the_title( $custom_order_id )` rather than `BRAND_NAME` meta specifically, since a Custom Stickers order (also routed through this same proof-approval flow) has no brand name field at all — every CustomOrder's post_title is set at submission regardless of type (`"{brand} — {date}"` for labels, `"Custom Stickers — {date}"` for stickers), so it's the one identifier guaranteed to exist for both.

## Admin panel reskin, phase 1: shared foundation + Templates/Sizes/Materials/Sticker Sizes (direct request: every YeffoPrint admin screen "uses the native WordPress post UI... not easy on the eyes")

Confirmed with the owner before starting: reskin in place, not a rebuild. Every editor's `save_post_{type}`/`add_meta_box()` flow is completely independent of how the screen renders (verified directly in `class-material-size-editor.php`), so this touches only what gets rendered/enqueued — no `save()` method, meta key, or validation rule changes anywhere in this pass. Full phased plan (this is phase 1 of 4) is in the session's own plan file; phases 2–4 (Pricing Rules/Field Presets, Custom Orders/Proofs, Settings/Dashboard) follow the same approach.

- **New `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell` (`includes/admin/class-admin-shell.php`) is the one place that knows "is this a YeffoPrint screen"** — a shared `POST_TYPES` list (all 8 CPTs, not just this phase's 4) drives three screen-agnostic jobs: disabling the block editor (`use_block_editor_for_post_type`) since none of these records need more than a short plain-text description, removing WordPress's own raw "Custom Fields" meta box (redundant with this plugin's purpose-built ones), and adding a `yeffoprint-admin` body class + enqueuing the new shared stylesheet. Registering it against all 8 CPTs now (not just phase 1's 4) means later phases never touch this class — they just get the same chrome-hiding/font-loading for free the moment their own screens are visually reviewed and tuned.
- **Deliberately never touches `#adminmenu`/`#wpadminbar`** — those are shared with every other wp-admin screen, not just this plugin's; restyling them would make the sidebar itself flicker between looks as staff navigate to Posts/Plugins/etc. Only `#wpbody-content` (the part that already changes per-screen) is in scope.
- **The Publish meta box is restyled, not removed** — Draft/Publish is real functional data (a Material/Size's active/inactive state, a Template's storefront visibility), not just chrome, so hiding it would remove a control staff actually need.
- **New `assets/admin/admin-shell.css`, following `assets/css/login.css`'s own established pattern** (the theme's prior reskin of another native-WP screen, wp-login.php) — wp-admin never loads `theme.json`'s generated stylesheet, so every token is the same value `theme.json` declares, hardcoded with a `/* token name */` comment rather than a CSS custom property, and `!important` shows up wherever needed to beat wp-admin's own ID/class-level specificity (postbox headers, button classes, list-table chrome) — same justification `login.css` already documents. Fonts are the identical Google Fonts CSS2 URL/weights already used for the login screen, registered fresh in `class-admin-shell.php` since wp-admin doesn't load the theme's own login-only enqueue.
- **Scoped generically (`.postbox`, `.button`, `input[type=...]`, `.wp-list-table`, `.nav-tab`), not per-screen** — since Templates/Materials/Sizes/Sticker Sizes' own meta boxes are all plain `<p>`/`<label>`/`<input>` markup (verified by reading all three editor classes in full), a single shared ruleset restyles every one of them, plus `class-design-setup-menu.php`'s tab strip and all 4 list-table screens, without needing separate CSS per editor. The one actual PHP change this phase needed was wrapping Template's Compatible Sizes/Materials checklists (`render_gallery_box()`) in a new `.yp-admin-checklist` span — a plain stacked checkbox list was the one spot generic postbox styling alone couldn't fix, since it reads as a wall of text once there are more than a handful of records.
- **Admin.css's own more specific widget rules (the field-schema repeater, pricing-tier rows) still need to win** — every existing `admin.css` enqueue call now declares `deps: ['yeffoprint-core-admin-shell']` so it always loads after the new shared foundation, letting those more specific rules override the generic ones without a specificity fight.
- **Known gap, left for a later pass**: the classic TinyMCE editor box that replaces the block canvas for Templates'/Materials' description field renders in an iframe, which plain admin CSS can't reach — its default WP styling is left as-is for now (already much cleaner than the Gutenberg canvas this phase specifically removed, so not the same complaint).
- **Not verified against a live screenshot** — no way to see the rendered wp-admin from here; same honest caveat this project's other visual work (the homepage redesign pass) already carries.

## Admin panel reskin, phase 2: Pricing Rules + Field Presets

- **`admin.css`'s own widget-specific rules (the bulk-discount-tier row, the field-schema repeater/grid) were still the stock wp-admin grays** (`#dcdcde`/`#f6f7f7`/`#646970`/`#b32d2e`) even after phase 1 shipped — real mismatch, since phase 1's generic `admin-shell.css` rules are deliberately the *less* specific layer here (this file loads after it precisely so its own rules can win, per that file's own docblock). Swapped every hardcoded gray for the same brand tokens `admin-shell.css` already uses, with the same `/* token name */` comments. `.button-link-delete`'s color was already visually correct before this (an `!important` in `admin-shell.css` was winning over `admin.css`'s stale, non-`!important` red regardless of load order — `!important` always beats specificity/order within the same origin) but is cleaned up here too so the file's own colors stop relying on being silently overridden by a different file to look right.
- **Sticker type/shape adjustment fields (`class-pricing-rule-editor.php`'s `render_sticker_box()`) — a run of small label+number-input pairs — get a new `.yp-admin-field-grid` wrapper** (`auto-fill, minmax(160px, 1fr)`) so they sit side by side instead of stacked full-width one per row; no change to each field's own internal markup, purely a wrapping `<div>` around the existing loop. Same reasoning as phase 1's `.yp-admin-checklist` treatment for Template's Compatible Sizes/Materials — a plain vertical list of many small same-shaped fields is the one pattern generic postbox styling alone can't fix.
- **Three number inputs (`Base price per label`, `Custom design fee`, `Custom size rate`) were missing `class="widefat"`** that every sibling field in this screen already had — a pre-existing inconsistency (not introduced by the reskin) that stood out once everything else on the page got wider, more consistent inputs; added for visual consistency, not a functional bug.
- **Field Presets needed no changes at all** — `class-field-preset-editor.php` reuses the exact same `#yp-field-schema-app` markup and `field-schema.js`/`admin.css` styling as the Template editor (already reskinned in phase 1), just with an empty `previewImageUrl` (handled by existing "position isn't part of a preset" copy). One color-token pass in `admin.css` fixed both screens' repeaters at once.

## Admin panel reskin, phase 3: Custom Orders + Proofs

- **`class-custom-order-editor.php`'s Request Details table needed new CSS, not reused list-table rules** — it's a hand-built `<table class="widefat striped">` key/value table (`<th>` row-labels inside `<tbody>`, no `<thead>` at all), which `.wp-list-table`-scoped rules from phase 1 never matched since WP_List_Table output always carries that class and this table never does. New rules distinguish a row-label `<th>` (`tbody > tr > th` — bold, warm-white background, `white-space: nowrap`) from a real column-header `<th>` (`thead th` — the nested per-row Batch table's Size/Material/Quantity/Compound header), since both patterns coexist on this one screen (the Batch table nests inside one of the outer table's own `<td>` cells).
- **The change-request/customer-provided-design `.notice` callouts get a real card treatment** (rounded, shadowed, white) instead of WordPress's default flat yellow box — left-border recolored to magenta-deep, the same "needs your attention" accent already used for `.button-link-delete`, rather than introducing a third semantic color. Their own inline `style="margin:...; padding:..."` attributes are untouched by this (a stylesheet `!important` beats a plain inline style, so this was safe to add without touching the render method itself).
- **Plain `<ul>` lists (the Proofs box's proof list, Request Details' uploaded-file list) get a shared row treatment** — bullets removed, each `<li>` a bordered row — since neither had ever had any class of their own to hang list-table-style CSS off before.
- **A `readonly` input (the Proofs box's "Customer approval link," meant to be clicked-to-copy, not edited) now gets a distinct muted background + pointer cursor** — it looked identical to every editable field otherwise, with no visual cue that clicking it does something different than typing in it.
- **`class-proof-editor.php` needed zero changes** — its own markup (a select, a file-picker button pair, plain `<p>`s) is entirely covered by phase 1's already-generic postbox/button/input/select rules; `yp_proof` was already in `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::POST_TYPES` from phase 1, so the shared stylesheet was already loading here.
- **The admin-menu "needs attention" bubble count (`class-admin-menu.php`) is untouched, by design** — it lives in `#adminmenu`, which this whole reskin has deliberately never touched (see phase 1's own reasoning: the sidebar is shared with every other wp-admin screen, restyling it would make it flicker between looks as staff navigate elsewhere). Confirmed no regression: nothing in this reskin's body-class/enqueue logic touches menu markup at all.

## Admin panel reskin, phase 4 (final): Settings/Card Surcharge/Rewards + a real Dashboard
