- **The real work here was recognition, not styling** — Dashboard, Settings, Card Surcharge, and Rewards are the *first* YeffoPrint admin pages this whole reskin touches that aren't tied to a post type at all (`add_menu_page()`/`add_submenu_page()` pages, not CPT edit screens), so `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::is_yeffoprint_screen()` — built around checking `$screen->post_type` against `POST_TYPES` — had no way to recognize any of them; the shared stylesheet was silently never loading on any of these four pages through phases 1–3. Fixed with a new `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::register_page_hook()` (static, since each page's own `admin_menu` registration runs independently of this class's constructor) — each of the three page-registering classes now passes the exact hook-suffix string `add_menu_page()`/`add_submenu_page()` already returns to it, which is *also* exactly what `get_current_screen()->id` resolves to for that page — no hook-name pattern to guess or keep in sync by hand, just the same return value already being used for each class's own asset-enqueue scoping.
- **New CSS layer needed: `.form-table`** — `do_settings_sections()`'s own auto-rendered layout and Rewards' hand-built manual-adjustment form both use WordPress's `th[scope=row]`/`td` "form table" pattern, a third distinct WP core table shape (alongside `.wp-list-table` from phase 1 and the `table.widefat` key/value pattern from phase 3) that needed its own rules.
- **Two rules from earlier phases had to be *broadened*, not just extended, once these postbox-free pages showed up**: `.notice` (phase 3's rule was scoped `.postbox .notice`, but Rewards' success/error messages after a manual adjustment render directly in `.wrap`, no postbox around them at all) and the `table.widefat` detail-table rules (phase 3's were also `.postbox`-scoped, but Rewards' own adjustment-history table isn't in one either). Both now also match a bare `.wrap > .notice` / `.wrap > table.widefat`, since a Settings-style page has no postbox to hang the original selector off of.
- **`class-rewards-admin.php`'s adjustment-history table had two hardcoded core WP colors** (`#00a32a`/`#d63638` for positive/negative point deltas) that predate this whole reskin — swapped for cyan-deep/magenta-deep, the same positive/attention split `admin-shell.css`'s own notice colors already establish, so the one inline-styled exception in this pass (a per-row color that has to stay inline, since it's genuinely data-dependent) still reads as the same design system as everything else.
- **`render_dashboard()` was one placeholder paragraph** ("Templates, Materials, Sizes... are managed from this menu") — replaced with a real landing page: a clickable "N custom orders need a proof" banner (reusing the existing `count_needing_a_proof()` the sidebar badge already computed — surfaced here too since a badge on a collapsed sidebar item is easy to miss on first landing on this exact page) plus an 8-card quick-link grid covering every section this reskin touched across all four phases (Design Setup, Pricing Rules, Field Presets, Custom Orders, Proofs, Rewards, Card Surcharge, Settings).
- **This closes out the admin panel reskin** — all 8 CPT editors, the Design Setup tab strip, all list-table screens, and all four plain admin pages now share one consistent, branded skin instead of stock wp-admin chrome, while every save/validation/settings-registration code path from before this project started is completely unchanged.

## Order-email customization formatting (direct request: "format the customization options on the labels nicer")

- **The blocker was WooCommerce core's own `wp_kses()`, not this codebase** — `class-order-item-meta.php`'s `format_customization_display()` already reformats a Template-flow batch's dense, `" — "`-joined `"Customization"`/`"Label N (qty M)"` meta row into a real field-by-field display, but only on the admin order-edit screen; WC core's `email-order-items.php` runs every meta row's `display_value` through `wc_display_item_meta()` → `wp_kses()`, whose HTML-email whitelist is only `<br>`/`<span>` (no attributes)/`<a>` — nothing that filter could build would have survived being sent. Overriding that core template to lift the restriction was considered and rejected on the same "real version-drift risk" grounds already documented for `class-order-tracking.php`'s "Track your order" button, just above.
- **So the fix bypasses that pipeline entirely, the same way that same button already does** — a new `render_customization_email_fields()`, hooked to `woocommerce_email_after_order_table` (unconstrained by `wp_kses`, since it isn't going through `get_formatted_meta_data()` at all), prints a fresh `<table class="yp-email-fields">` block per batch line item directly into the email template; `format_customization_display()` now also suppresses the raw joined-string row in this same context (via a new `$rendering_html_email` flag, set true on `woocommerce_email_header` and false on `woocommerce_email_footer` — WC's plain-text templates never call either, so plain-text emails are completely untouched, still showing the original joined string, on purpose). New CSS (`.yp-email-fields`/`.yp-email-fields-heading`/`.yp-email-field-label`/`.yp-email-field-value` in the theme's `email-styles.php`) matches `.yp-email-callout`'s already-established visual language — same uppercase-label/bordered-box pattern, a neutral warm-white box instead of that callout's pink so the two don't compete. `email-styles.php`'s `<style>` block is auto-inlined by WooCommerce's own Emogrifier pipeline at send time, so these classes work like every other component already in that file, no manual inline-style juggling needed.
- **The variants→markup loop was already duplicated (admin `<div>`s vs. the new email `<table>` rows) plus a third near-duplicate `_yp_variants`/`_yp_template_snapshot` decode** already existed in `add_qr_download_links()` — both extracted into small shared private helpers (`variant_field_pairs()`, `get_batch_data()`) rather than left as three copies of the same logic, so `add_qr_download_links()`, `format_customization_display()`, and the new email renderer all read from one place.
- **Scoped to Template-flow batch items only** — Custom Design/Custom Sticker order items already write Size/Material/Quantity/Compound as separate, already-readable named meta rows (not the joined-string problem this fixes), so `get_batch_data()` (keyed on `_yp_variants`/`_yp_template_snapshot`) naturally skips them.

### Follow-up: a color swatch next to a `color`-field's hex value (direct request: "can I see what the actual 'color' is... in a small box next to the hex code?")

`variant_field_pairs()` now also returns each field's `type` (from `field_schema`), so a new shared `color_swatch_html()` can tell a `color`-type field apart from any other text value and prepend a small `<span>` styled `background-color:<hex>` before it — same class-plus-inline-style technique `email-header.php`'s own wordmark dots (`.yp-dot`) already use, since a per-value color can't live in a static stylesheet rule the way the swatch's fixed size/shape can. Re-validates the hex format at render time (`^#([0-9a-f]{3}|[0-9a-f]{6})$`) even though `class-field-schema.php`'s own `sanitize_hex_color()` already guarantees it at save time, since this value is about to go straight into a `style` attribute. Renders on both surfaces this pass already touches — the admin order screen (`.yp-order-field__swatch`, `admin.css`) and HTML order emails (`.yp-email-color-swatch`, `email-styles.php`) — automatically for any Template whose field schema uses a `color` field; nothing to configure per-Template.

## Rewards points visibility for admin + a complete customer history (direct request: "I need a way to see rewards points balances/history for users... user should also see their points history... in their dashboard")

- **Two data sources that had never been merged anywhere** — `class-rewards.php`'s own docblock already documents the balance as a running total, not a ledger: order-based earn/redeem lives as order meta (queried ad hoc), while manual admin adjustments *and* referral bonuses (both route through the same `adjust_balance()`) live in a single, store-wide, 300-entry-capped `ADJUSTMENTS_OPTION`. Nothing anywhere joined those two into one customer's chronological view — confirmed by reading both consumers directly: the customer's own My Account → Rewards history (`class-account-endpoints.php`'s `render_rewards_history()`) only ever queried orders, so a manual adjustment or referral bonus silently changed a customer's balance without ever appearing in their own history; the admin Rewards page had no per-customer view at all, only a flat, unfiltered, store-wide adjustments log.
- **One new shared method, `YeffoPrint_Rewards::get_history_for_user( $user_id, $limit )`, used by both surfaces** — so the admin lookup and the customer's own history are structurally guaranteed to agree on what "this customer's history" means, instead of two independent merge implementations drifting apart later. Pulls the customer's own orders carrying `ORDER_POINTS_EARNED_META` (one row per order, since an order can carry both a nonzero earned *and* redeemed amount — matching the shape the customer tab's existing `<li>` markup already expects) plus a new `get_adjustments_for_user()` (filters the *full* stored option array by `user_id`, deliberately not `get_recent_adjustments()`'s system-wide-recency slice, which would wrongly favor other customers' more-recent entries over this one's own older ones), merges and sorts both by date. Fetching only the newest `$limit` orders is still provably sufficient — an order can only land in the final top-`$limit` merged result if fewer than `$limit` rows total (of any kind) are newer than it, so fewer than `$limit` orders specifically are.
- **Customer-facing fix was a pure data-source swap** — `render_rewards_history()` now calls `get_history_for_user()` instead of querying orders itself; the exact same `<ul class="yp-rewards-history">` markup and CSS classes render both kinds of row (an adjustment row's "order" span shows its reason text instead of an order number), so the theme's `woocommerce.css` needed no changes at all.
- **Admin fix is a new "Look up a customer" section on the *existing* Rewards page**, not a new page — a plain `method="get"` search box (no nonce needed, it's read-only) showing the customer's balance and full history (`table.widefat.striped`, already styled by the phase-4 reskin above) via the same shared method at a higher limit (50 vs. the customer's own 15). The existing email-or-username → `WP_User` resolution logic in `handle_adjust()` (POST, nonce-protected) was extracted into a shared `resolve_user()` so the new GET lookup and the existing adjust handler don't carry two copies of the same reasoning. Small UX nicety: a successful lookup pre-fills the adjust form's Customer field, so staff can search, review, then act on the same customer without retyping.
- **Left alone on purpose**: the 300-entry cap on `ADJUSTMENTS_OPTION` — a pre-existing, documented "keep it simple" tradeoff, store-wide rather than per-customer, so a very busy store's older manual adjustments could in theory age out before a customer's order history would. Not part of either request; the real fix if it ever becomes one is moving manual adjustments to their own table instead of a capped option, a materially bigger change.

## "Rewards Points" meta box on the WooCommerce order screen (direct request: "a little box... showing how many points each order is going to earn... reflect that [they've been awarded]... a button to manually award them")

- **New `YeffoPrint_Rewards_Order_Box` (`includes/admin/class-rewards-order-box.php`)**, registered via `wc_get_page_screen_id( 'shop_order' )` rather than the two hardcoded screen ids `class-order-item-meta.php`'s own `is_order_edit_screen()` uses — WooCommerce's own official resolver for "the order edit screen, whichever storage (classic post type vs. HPOS) is actually active," so one `add_meta_box()` call covers both without this class needing to know which is in use. The callback normalizes its own argument the same way core's HPOS migration guide documents: a `WP_Post` on the classic screen, a `WC_Order` directly on the HPOS one.
- **The earned/redeemed math moved out of `finalize_order()` into a new, read-only, static `YeffoPrint_Rewards::calculate_points( $order )`** — `finalize_order()` itself is unchanged in behavior, just now calls this instead of computing inline. The box calls the exact same method as a live "will earn" estimate on an unprocessed order (never written anywhere, recomputed on every page load from the order's own current fees/line items), guaranteeing the estimate and the eventual real award can never disagree about the formula.
- **Three states, driven by the same `ORDER_PROCESSED_META`/`ORDER_POINTS_EARNED_META`/`ORDER_POINTS_REDEEMED_META` `finalize_order()` already reads and writes** — a guest order (`get_customer_id() === 0`) shows a plain "not eligible" note (guest orders never get an earned/redeemed meta row at all, so a bare "0 points" would have read like a bug); an unprocessed order shows the live estimate plus, for a `manage_options` user, an "Award now" button; a processed order shows the actual stored earned/redeemed amounts, the customer's current balance, and a link into `class-rewards-admin.php`'s "Look up a customer" section (pre-filled via the same `?lookup_user=` query arg that section already reads).
- **"Award now" doesn't reimplement the award logic** — its `admin-post.php` handler re-checks `ORDER_PROCESSED_META` itself (for an accurate error message if two tabs race) and then simply calls `( new YeffoPrint_Rewards() )->finalize_order( $order_id )` directly, the exact same idempotent, hook-triggered method every normal payment event already calls — one code path for "how does an order get its points awarded," whether that happens automatically or by a staff member's click.

## Order numbers formatted as "YP-{id}" (direct request: "change the format of customer order numbers... YP-XXXX")

- **A display filter on the id WooCommerce already assigns, not a new counter** — confirmed by reading WC core directly: `WC_Order::get_order_number()` is `apply_filters( 'woocommerce_order_number', $this->get_id(), $this )`, the *one* place every consumer (order emails, the admin order list/screen, My Account, the REST API's own `number` field, invoices) reads an order's displayed number from. `YeffoPrint_Order_Number_Format` hooks that single filter and returns `"YP-{$order->get_id()}"` — the underlying numeric order id is completely untouched: the database, the order-edit URL, REST ids, and every internal by-id lookup this plugin already does all keep working exactly as before. Reusing the existing id (rather than a new sequential counter starting at 1) was a direct choice, made explicitly to avoid the extra moving part of a separately-persisted, concurrency-safe counter for a purely cosmetic change.
- **Admin order search would otherwise silently break** — confirmed by reading both WC data stores: a search term only resolves straight to an order id when `is_numeric( $term )` (classic `WC_Order_Data_Store_CPT::search_orders()`) — `"YP-1042"` isn't numeric, so pasting an order number straight from an email into the admin search box would find nothing. Fixed by stripping a leading `"YP-"` from `$_GET['s']` on `admin_init`, once, before WooCommerce's own search ever runs — covers both the classic `edit.php?post_type=shop_order` screen and the HPOS `admin.php?page=wc-orders` one from a single hook, rather than filtering each data store's search results separately.
- **The packing-slip PowerShell script (see its own session's file, not part of this git repo) needed a small follow-up fix**: its header row was building `"#{0}" -f $Order.number`, which — since the WooCommerce REST API's own `order.number` field reads from this same filtered `get_order_number()` — would have printed a redundant `#YP-1042`. Updated to print `$Order.number` directly.

## How It Works page (direct request: describe the order timeline and materials offered, "a page template like the other pages... styled according to site guidelines, add in some icons/imagery")

- **A real block-theme template, composed from patterns, not a one-off page of pasted content** — same convention every other real page on the site already follows (`custom-design-form.html`, `custom-stickers-form.html`, `contact-form.html`, …): a `templates/how-it-works.html` template-part shell, `theme.json`'s `customTemplates` entry so it's selectable from Pages → Page Attributes → Template (matching `custom-design-form`/`proof-approval`'s own precedent there), and a new `create_page( 'how-it-works', ..., 'how-it-works.html' )` call in `class-pages-setup-command.php` alongside every other site-structure page — same idempotent, dev-triggered-only provisioning, never auto-created.
- **Three new patterns, each reusing an established component rather than inventing new visual language**: `how-it-works-intro.php` (a plain `.yp-section` eyebrow/H1/intro banner, the same shape every other standalone page's header already takes); `process-steps.php` (the homepage's own `.yp-steps`/`.yp-step` card grid and hand-drawn line-icon style from `how-it-works.php`, just with the full 6-step order-to-shipping walkthrough instead of that pattern's 3-step teaser, including 3 new icons drawn in the exact same 20×20/`stroke="currentColor"`/`stroke-width:1.6` idiom as the existing three); `turnaround-time.php` (a new `.yp-turnaround` stat card — "~1 week" in large magenta-deep type — on a `.yp-section--tint` background, the same alternating-tint rhythm the homepage already uses between sections).
- **`.yp-step`'s per-card accent color was hardcoded to exactly 3 children** (`:nth-child(1/2/3)`) since the homepage's own teaser only ever has 3 steps — with `process-steps.php`'s 6, changed to `:nth-child(3n+1/3n+2/3n)` so the cyan/magenta-deep/yellow cycle repeats every 3 cards instead of running out after the first three. Purely additive: `3n+1` still matches child 1 first, so the homepage's existing 3-card version renders identically to before.
- **The Materials section is the existing `materials.php` pattern, reused as-is, not re-described in new copy** — it already queries real, published `yp_material` records (name, swatch photo, hover/on-vial photo if uploaded, blurb from the record's own description) rather than a hardcoded list, so this page's Materials section can never drift out of sync with whatever's actually live, and automatically gets real product photography wherever an admin has uploaded it (with the existing per-material gradient-chip fallback for any that don't).
- **Closing CTA is the existing `custom-design-cta.php` pattern, reused as-is** — already links to `/custom-design/`, a real, already-provisioned page, so this pass didn't need to guess at or hardcode any URL.
- **Not independently verified rendered** — the WordPress.com content-publishing connector (`content-authoring.execute`) was returning a signing error for every operation tried this session (pages, tags — read-only `describe` calls worked fine), so this couldn't be provisioned/previewed live from here; needs a real `wp yeffoprint setup-pages` run (or the connector retried) and a visual check once it can be.

### Follow-up: a "What Material Should I Select?" guide, from real supplied copy

- **New `material-guide.php` pattern, deliberately hardcoded rather than data-driven like `materials.php` above it** — the site owner supplied real, specific customer-facing copy for each material (thickness in mil, finish, application notes, a holographic-specific shipping-delay caveat), lightly copyedited for grammar only, facts untouched. That's fundamentally different from `materials.php`'s own one-line blurb (pulled from a Material record's own short description field) — this is a longer decision-guide, so it's its own section directly below the swatch grid rather than replacing or stretching that grid's compact format.
- **The holographic 24-hour-shipping-delay note gets its own visually distinct callout** (`.yp-material-guide__note` — a left-bordered, tinted aside inside that card) rather than blending into the paragraph above it — a genuinely actionable detail a customer choosing that finish needs to actually notice, not just technically have been told.
- **Includes a material ("Prism") that may not exist as a published `yp_material` record yet** — flagged directly rather than silently glossed over: `materials.php`'s own swatch grid only ever shows what's actually live, so if there's no Prism record yet, the swatch grid above this guide won't include it even though the guide below describes it. Whoever publishes this should add that Material record (with a swatch photo, ideally) so the two sections agree.
- **The "Contact us" link in this pattern's intro is the one guessed URL in this whole page** (`/contact/`) — safe because `class-pages-setup-command.php` already provisions a real page at that exact slug, unlike every other link on this page which reuses an existing pattern's own already-correct URL.

### Follow-up: a real photo circle next to each material guide entry

- **Went back to being data-driven for the photo, while staying hardcoded for the copy** — direct follow-up request: "pull the picture assigned to the material." Each of `material-guide.php`'s six hardcoded entries now carries its own `slug`, looked up against a published `yp_material` record's `post_name` the same way `materials.php` already resolves its own swatch grid, and rendered via that record's `get_the_post_thumbnail_url()` if one exists.
- **Falls back to the exact same per-material gradient "chip" `materials.php`'s swatch grid already falls back to, not a second definition of what each material looks like** — the existing `.yp-material-swatch__chip--{slug}` rules in `patterns.css` now share their gradient definitions with new `.yp-material-guide__photo--{slug}` selectors via one combined rule per material, so a material's fallback look can't drift between the two sections. Added one new shared fallback (`--prism`) that didn't exist before, styled as narrow repeating diagonal bands — deliberately distinct from holographic's broad 4-stop gradient wash, so the two don't read as the same material at a glance.
- **Circle, not the swatch grid's square chip** — a deliberate visual distinction so this section doesn't look like a re-run of the grid above it. Placed to the left of each entry's text (name reads first left-to-right, but the material sample is the very first thing the eye lands on), stacking centered above the text under 600px where a circle competing with the card's own padding would otherwise feel cramped.

## Dashboard operational widgets (direct request: "one screen: pending orders, pending proofs, proofs waiting on customer approval," plus a configurable overdue flag)

- **Two genuinely separate status systems, queried and shown side by side rather than forced into one** — confirmed by reading the actual data model (`class-custom-order-meta.php`, and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md's own §6): native WooCommerce order status (payment/fulfillment) and `yp_custom_order`'s own `_yp_status` pipeline (`design_in_progress` → `awaiting_approval` → …) don't share a vocabulary, so "pending orders" (WC orders still in `processing` — this store's own established "paid, not yet fulfilled" meaning, per `class-custom-order-payment.php`'s three payment-confirmed hooks) and the two Custom Order buckets are three independent queries, not one unified list. Direct follow-up during scoping: the overdue check covers Custom Orders stuck in the design/proof pipeline too, not just unshipped WC orders — a stalled design needs staff action just as much as an unshipped box does.
- **New `YeffoPrint_Dashboard_Widgets` (`includes/admin/class-dashboard-widgets.php`), composed into `class-admin-menu.php`'s existing `render_dashboard()` rather than folded into that already multi-purpose class** — that class still owns the page itself (menu registration, the pre-existing quick-links grid, now relabeled "Quick Links" and moved below these new widgets); this one only owns the three data tables and their own WC-order/CustomOrder queries. `render_custom_order_section()` is one shared method for both the Pending Proofs and Awaiting Approval tables (identical query shape, just a different `_yp_status` value), not two near-duplicates.
- **The old single "N custom orders need a proof" banner on this page is gone, superseded rather than duplicated** — the new Pending Proofs table below is a strict superset of what that banner told you (every one of those orders, with customer/date/overdue state, not just a count). `count_needing_a_proof()` and the sidebar bubble it drives (`add_needs_attention_badge()`) are untouched — that's an independent, already-working signal, not replaced.
- **New admin-configurable `DASHBOARD_DUE_DATE_DAYS_OPTION` (default 7), registered on the existing general Settings page** (`class-admin-menu.php`, alongside Live Preview/Promo/Splash/Tracking/Contact — the same "general operational setting, no dedicated page" tier those already sit in) — one number applies uniformly across all three tables, per the direct request ("I should be able to adjust that"), rather than a separate due-date window per bucket. Floored at 1 day (`sanitize_due_date_days()`) — a same-day due date would flag every fresh order as overdue the moment it's placed.
- **A visual flag only, deliberately not a new email/cron notification** — confirmed as the intended scope before building: this dashboard is read-only, checked when staff visit it (a highlighted row + an "N days overdue" pill, reusing the same magenta-deep "needs your attention" accent `.yp-dashboard__attention`'s removed banner and the admin reskin's own notice colors already established). A proactive emailed alert would need its own scheduled `wp_schedule_event` job and "already notified" tracking — neither exists anywhere in this plugin yet (confirmed by search) — and was explicitly scoped out, not merely deferred by oversight.
- **`table.widefat`'s existing reskin rules needed broadening again, the same way phase 4 already broadened them once for postbox-free pages** — those rules matched `.postbox table.widefat` or the *direct child* `.wrap > table.widefat`; this widget's tables sit one level deeper (`.wrap > .yp-dashboard__section > table.widefat`), so a third selector variant (`.yp-dashboard__section table.widefat`) was added alongside the existing two in every affected rule, rather than loosening the direct-child selector itself and risking it reaching some unrelated nested table elsewhere in wp-admin.

### Follow-up: the dashboard page itself was hard to find

- **A pre-existing, already-documented WordPress quirk finally had real consequences** — this class's own top-of-file docblock already called it out before any of the above was built: with no submenu explicitly registered at the parent menu's own slug, WordPress quietly inserts one labeled the same as the parent ("YeffoPrint" again), and clicking the *top-level sidebar item itself* just expands/collapses that flyout rather than navigating — only the duplicate-labeled entry inside it actually opens the page. Direct report confirmed exactly this: "clicking the YeffoPrint heading... just toggles the submenu." Tolerable when this page was a placeholder; not once real "what needs attention today" content (the three widgets above) lived there and staff couldn't find it.
- **Fixed by registering that submenu explicitly** — `add_submenu_page( 'yeffoprint', 'Dashboard', 'Dashboard', ..., 'yeffoprint', [ $this, 'render_dashboard' ] )`, at the exact same slug as the parent, first among the submenu registrations so it sits at the top of the flyout. This is the standard, documented way to override WordPress's own auto-inserted duplicate — the sidebar now shows a distinctly-labeled "Dashboard" entry instead of a second "YeffoPrint."

## How It Works page follow-up: theme version bump + a live Thickness field

- **`yeffoprint/style.css`'s `Version` header had never been bumped since the theme's very first commit**, confirmed via `git log` on that one line — despite every pattern this whole How It Works effort added across several PRs. Direct report: the material guide's copy still wasn't showing even after the swatch grid above it (added in the same original template) rendered fine. WordPress caches a theme's scanned block-pattern list keyed partly to this header, so a version that's sat static since day one is a plausible reason a newer pattern file wouldn't get picked up — bumped `0.1.0` → `0.2.0` to force a fresh scan. Flagged honestly rather than as a confirmed root cause: this environment has no way to inspect the live site directly to verify it (network egress to the production domain is blocked, and the available WordPress.com content-authoring connector was unreliable for the list/search calls that would have helped diagnose this further) — it's a safe, independently-justifiable fix regardless of whether it's the actual cause.
- **New `THICKNESS_MIL` meta on the Material CPT** (`class-commerce-record-meta.php`), added alongside the existing `PRICE_ADJUSTMENT`/`HOVER_IMAGE`/`SCOPE` fields on the same editor screen (`class-material-size-editor.php`) — direct follow-up: "I like where you put the thickness in mil for some of them because the content had it. Can we add that field to the material form." `0` doubles as "not set" rather than a separate boolean/empty-string state, since no real material is actually 0mil thick — the admin field itself shows blank rather than `0` to avoid implying a value's been deliberately entered.
- **`material-guide.php`'s hardcoded `spec` pill is now a live override, not a live replacement** — each entry gained a `finish` label (`Glossy`, `Matte`, `Chrome`, …) alongside its existing hardcoded `spec` string; the loop already looks up a matching `yp_material` record for the photo circle, so reading `THICKNESS_MIL` off that same record was free. Where a live thickness is set, the pill becomes `"{thickness}mil · {finish}"`, trailing zeros trimmed (`4mil`, not `4.00mil`); where it isn't, the pill falls back to the entry's original hardcoded `spec` string exactly as before — so this page's current appearance is unchanged until the site owner actually fills the new field in.

### Follow-up: click (or hover) to see a bigger photo

- **Zero new JS** — direct request: "hover or click on the image to see a bigger image of what the material looks like." The site already has a generic accessible drawer primitive (`assets/js/site.js`'s `openDrawer`/`closeDrawer`, driving the header's search/cart panels and the splash screen) with a centered-modal variant, wired purely through `data-yp-drawer-trigger`/`data-yp-drawer-close` attributes — `initDrawers()` scans for these on every page load, so six new per-material `.yp-drawer--center` modals (one per entry, each its own `id="yp-material-zoom-{slug}"`) just work the moment they're in the rendered HTML, no code changes to that file at all.
- **The enlarged photo reuses the Material editor's existing "Hover / on-vial image" field** (`HOVER_IMAGE`, `class-material-size-editor.php`) rather than just blowing up the swatch photo — that field was already built for exactly this ("a photo of this finish actually applied to a vial"), and it's literally what was asked for ("what the material looks like on the vinyl"). Falls back to a larger export of the primary swatch photo if no on-vial photo has been uploaded for that record yet; a material with neither (gradient-fallback only) renders as a plain, non-interactive circle exactly as before — no dead click target promising an enlargement that doesn't exist.
- **"Hover" and "click" do two different, complementary things, not the same thing twice** — clicking opens the actual enlarged photo in the modal; hovering (or keyboard-focusing) only scales the circle up slightly as tactile feedback, since a floating full-size preview near a small circle in a six-item list risked clipping at the section's edges and wouldn't work on touch at all. A small always-visible zoom-icon badge on the circle (not hover-only) gives touch users the same "this is tappable" signal a mouse user gets implicitly from the cursor/scale change.

### Follow-up: removed the now-redundant swatch grid from this page

- **Direct report: "The materials and choosing a material section seem redundant... eliminate the first and just keep the choosing a material section."** True only once the guide grew its own real photo circles (the "Click (or hover)" follow-up just above) — before that, the swatch grid above it was the only place with real material photos, and the guide below was text-only. With both sections independently showing a photo + name per material, the grid's `materials.php`'s own count-of-finishes heading and square-chip grid had become a near-duplicate of what the guide already covers in more depth, directly above it.
- **One-line template change, nothing else** — `templates/how-it-works.html`'s `<!-- wp:pattern {"slug":"yeffoprint/materials"} /-->` reference was removed; `materials.php` itself is untouched, since the homepage (`templates/front-page.html`) still uses it as its own materials section (a shorter, count-driven overview, appropriate there where there's no room for the guide's longer copy). No other page or pattern references `yeffoprint/materials`, so this is the swatch grid's only removal.

## Web Design for Peptide Resellers — new service page (Part A of a two-part build; Part B is the maintenance subscription's billing)

New line of business, direct from the site owner: web design services for peptide resellers, packages from design through execution, plus a separate recurring maintenance subscription (that second part's billing infrastructure is its own follow-up entry below). Planned with the owner first (design-partner planning session, not started from a spec) — two decisions were confirmed before writing any code: packages are sold via a quote/contact conversation, not self-serve checkout, since a real build's scope varies too much per client for a fixed price; and package tiers/pricing are deliberately placeholder for this pass, with the owner filling in real numbers before launch.

- **Same page-building convention as every other page here, no exceptions** — `templates/web-design.html` composed from `<!-- wp:pattern -->` references, a `theme.json` → `customTemplates` entry, and a `create_page( 'web-design', ..., 'web-design.html' )` call in `class-pages-setup-command.php`, identical in shape to the How It Works page's own build earlier this session. A "Web Design" link was added to the header's primary nav (`parts/header.html`) alongside the site's other top-level pages.
- **Five new patterns, each built from an established component rather than a new one**: `web-design-hero.php` reuses `hero.php`'s exact structure (eyebrow-accent, H1, buttons, stats row), swapping in new copy and a new illustration; `web-design-packages.php` is a 3-tier card grid built on the exact same "grid + zero the auto margin-block-start" fix `.yp-steps` already established (a Group block's `layout:"default"` auto-injects a top margin on every child but the first, which fights a grid's own `gap`); `web-design-included.php` is an icon+label grid using the theme's existing hand-drawn icon idiom (20×20 viewBox, `stroke="currentColor"`, `stroke-width 1.6`, see `process-steps.php`); `web-design-maintenance-teaser.php` introduces the subscription (its own button is covered in the Part B entry below); `web-design-cta.php` is a new "dark break" closing section, since `custom-design-cta.php`'s existing copy ("Create a Custom Label") is specific to label orders and doesn't fit this page.
- **The "nice graphics" ask got a new illustration, not a stock image** — `.yp-browser-mock`, a browser-window mockup (chrome bar with traffic-light dots, a gradient hero band, a 3-card product grid, a cart badge) built the same way `hero.php`'s own `.yp-proof__mark` already is: solid brand-colored SVG shapes, not a raster asset, so it stays crisp at any size and needs no image to manage. The gradient band uses a real `<linearGradient>` def with `stop-color: var(--wp--preset--color--...)` — CSS custom properties work directly in an SVG `style` attribute, so this didn't need the `gradient-brand` token reimplemented as a flat color.
- **Every placeholder price is flagged twice, not just left as an editable-looking number** — `web-design-packages.php`'s `$packages` array (same "hardcoded content array" convention `material-guide.php`'s own `$materials` array established) holds `'$X,XXX'` literal strings, and each card renders an explicit "Placeholder — edit before launch" label directly above the price on the live page, not just in a code comment — confirmed by rendering a static mock through headless Chromium before shipping that the flag is genuinely impossible to miss at a glance, not just present in markup.
- **The middle "Growth" tier is visually featured** (`.yp-web-design-package--featured` — accent border, elevated shadow, a "Most Popular" badge) — a deliberate nudge toward the tier most owners would actually want most customers landing on, the same reason pricing pages conventionally do this; purely a visual treatment, no different code path or pricing logic behind it.
- **The maintenance teaser's button is already wired to Part B, not a placeholder `#` link** — it reads `get_option( 'yeffoprint_maintenance_payment_link', '' )` directly and falls back to `/contact/` when that option is empty, so this page ships fully functional today and needs zero follow-up edit once Part B's Stripe setup is done — the option just starts getting a real value at that point.

## Maintenance subscription billing — Part B: a direct Stripe Billing integration (free), not WooCommerce Subscriptions

Confirmed with the owner before writing any code: WooPayments (already installed, v11.0.0) does vendor a full subscriptions engine internally (`vendor/woocommerce/subscriptions-core/`, the same code the official paid WooCommerce Subscriptions extension uses) — but the owner checked their live account and it isn't actually enrolled/visible there, and that official extension itself is a paid product (~$199/yr). Building this on top of either wasn't viable. **Confirmed approach instead: a second, direct Stripe connection**, separate from the one WooPayments manages (which is itself managed/obfuscated and doesn't expose API keys this could reuse) — a free Stripe account, a no-code Stripe Payment Link for the recurring price, and a small webhook here to record who subscribed. Same Stripe processor/fees the store already knows via WooPayments, just not routed through WooCommerce's own product/cart/order system — the honest tradeoff, stated up front: a maintenance-plan purchase won't appear in the store's normal WooCommerce Orders list, so this pass adds its own small admin surface for it instead.

- **New `includes/maintenance/` directory** — a deliberately separate concern from `includes/woocommerce/`, since this bypasses WooCommerce's checkout/order system entirely rather than extending it. `class-maintenance-sub-meta.php` registers a new `yp_maintenance_sub` CPT (`class-post-type-registry.php`, same `args()` helper every other record type there already uses) plus its meta constants (`STRIPE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`/`STRIPE_CUSTOMER_ID`/`CUSTOMER_EMAIL`/`CUSTOMER_USER_ID`/`PLAN_LABEL`/`STATUS`/`CURRENT_PERIOD_END`), following `class-commerce-record-meta.php`'s own `register_post_meta()` pattern. post_title is the customer's email, so the native admin list is readable with zero extra work — extended anyway with `manage_yp_maintenance_sub_posts_columns`/`_custom_column` (same convention `class-custom-order-meta.php` already established) for Plan/Status/Renews columns, since raw meta isn't visible on the list screen by default.
- **`class-stripe-webhook-controller.php` — `POST /yeffoprint-core/v1/stripe/webhook`, the one place a subscriber's real status lives on this site.** Registered with `permission_callback => '__return_true'`, the same necessarily-public shape as the existing Venmo/Zelle payment webhook (`class-payment-webhook-controller.php`) — the caller is Stripe's servers, not a browser session, so there's no WordPress nonce to check. Authenticated instead by verifying Stripe's own `Stripe-Signature` header against a secret pasted into Settings when the webhook endpoint is created in the Stripe Dashboard (`class-stripe-webhook-secret.php`, same option-backed storage shape as `class-payment-webhook-secret.php`, just admin-pasted rather than auto-generated — Stripe controls that value, not this site). Verified with plain `hash_hmac()`/`hash_equals()` (HMAC-SHA256 over `"{timestamp}.{payload}"`, Stripe's own well-documented scheme) rather than pulling in the Stripe PHP SDK as a new Composer dependency just for this one check. Handles three events: `checkout.session.completed` (mode `subscription`) creates or updates the matching record; `customer.subscription.updated` syncs `STATUS`; `customer.subscription.deleted` marks it `canceled`. Resolves a `WP_User` by email as a convenience link, purely optional — a Stripe subscriber has no obligation to also have a WordPress account here.
- **Settings page gets two new fields** (`class-admin-menu.php`, same `register_setting()`/`add_settings_field()` convention as the existing Dashboard due-date-days field): "Payment Link URL" (what the Web Design page's maintenance-teaser button actually links to — see Part A above) and "Stripe webhook signing secret," both admin-pasted from the Stripe Dashboard, with the exact webhook URL to paste into Stripe shown right in the field's own description so there's nothing to look up separately.
- **`yp_maintenance_sub` added to `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::POST_TYPES`**, so its list-table screen picks up the shared admin reskin automatically, same as every other record type — no new CSS.
- **A fourth Dashboard section, "Active Maintenance Subscribers"** (`class-dashboard-widgets.php`) — deliberately *not* built on the existing `render_section()` helper the other three sections share, even though the plan going in assumed it would be: that method's fourth column computes "how overdue," which has no equivalent meaning for a healthy, ongoing subscription (there's no due date a subscriber can be "late" against) — forcing this data through it would show a confusing overdue/days-ago label on every row of an entirely healthy list. Same section/table styling classes for visual consistency, its own Customer/Plan/Renews column set instead.
- **Explicitly not built**: actual automated uptime/update monitoring infrastructure (crawling client sites, checking for plugin/core updates, alerting) — the owner's original message described this as the subscription's value proposition for the marketing copy, not a system to build; it's an operational/manual process on the owner's side for now. Also not built: a custom in-house subscription-management UI for customers — Stripe's own free Customer Portal (enabled once, in the Stripe Dashboard) already covers self-service cancel/update-card, so there was nothing this site needed to build to offer that.

### Follow-up: moved the maintenance mention into a badge on the pricing table

- **Direct report: "I don't like where the monthly maintenance and monitoring is. Maybe move it towards the top on some badge on the pricing table?"** The full standalone "Ongoing Maintenance & Monitoring" section (`web-design-maintenance-teaser.php`) sat well down the page, after the packages and the "What's Included" grid — easy to miss, and disconnected from the pricing decision it's actually most relevant to. Deleted that pattern entirely (and its now-dead `.yp-maintenance-teaser` CSS) rather than leaving it unreferenced; its one piece of real logic — reading the `yeffoprint_maintenance_payment_link` option with a `/contact/` fallback — moved into `web-design-packages.php`, which is now the only place that link lives.
- **A clickable pill badge, not a banner**, sits directly between the packages section's intro paragraph and the pricing grid itself — literally "towards the top" (packages is the very next section after the hero) and visually attached to "the pricing table" the request asked for. Uses the same `gradient-brand-deep` token as `.yp-web-design-package__badge`'s sibling "Most Popular" tag family, but as a full clickable link (wrench icon, one line of copy, arrow) rather than a static label, since this one needs to actually go somewhere. On narrow screens it switches from a fixed-width pill to a full-width wrapping banner (`width:fit-content` can't shrink below its own one-line content, which is wider than a phone screen) — same underlying content and link, just reflowed.

## Theme version bump, round two — the pattern-cache mechanism is now confirmed, not just suspected

Direct report: "the web design page is blank." Root-caused with actual confidence this time, not just a plausible-safe-fix guess: WordPress core's `WP_Theme::get_block_patterns()` caches its scan of a theme's `patterns/*.php` files in a key that includes the theme's own `Version` header string verbatim. The very first version-bump fix (`0.1.0` → `0.2.0`, "How It Works page follow-up," above) was applied once and never touched again — every pattern shipped since then (all four of the Web Design page's patterns: `web-design-hero.php`, `web-design-packages.php`, `web-design-included.php`, `web-design-cta.php`) was added to a theme still reporting the same `0.2.0` it was scanned under, so none of them were ever in the cached list WordPress was actually serving from. Since the Web Design page's entire body is composed from exactly those four `wp:pattern` block references with nothing else, "cached list has zero of this page's patterns in it" renders as a fully blank page — a clean, complete explanation, not a coincidence.

Fixed the same way as before — `0.2.0` → `0.3.0` — but this time with a standing comment added directly in `yeffoprint/style.css`, right below the header block: bump this version any time a `patterns/*.php` file is added or renamed. Editing an existing pattern's content in place doesn't need a bump (its slug/filename didn't change, so the cached list already points at the right, now-updated file) — only a new or renamed pattern silently fails to render anywhere it's referenced until the version moves. This has now broken a real page twice from the same missed step; the comment exists so it doesn't need to be rediscovered a third time.

## Web Design Packages become a real, admin-editable record (direct follow-up: "I'd like to make it future proof and be able to adjust prices from the YeffoPrint admin panel")

The pricing table's three tiers had been a hardcoded PHP array (`web-design-packages.php`'s own `$packages`) since the page shipped — fine for placeholder copy nobody was meant to edit without asking for a code change, wrong once the owner specifically wants to adjust real prices themselves. Rebuilt on the same real-CPT convention every other admin-managed record in this plugin already uses (Materials, Sizes, Pricing Rules), rather than a Settings-page form or a JSON blob — a site owner already spends time in this exact "list of records, click one, edit fields, save" UI daily, so a fourth thing that also worked that way needed the least new explaining.

- **New `yp_web_design_pkg` CPT** (`class-post-type-registry.php`, same `args()` helper every record type here uses) — the tier's name is the post title, sort order is `menu_order` via the native "Page Attributes" box (same drag-orderable convention Material/Size already use), and active/inactive reuses `post_status` (publish/draft) rather than a redundant meta flag — exactly the three "reuse a WordPress-native mechanism instead of building a new one" choices `class-commerce-record-meta.php`'s own docblock already established for Material/Size.
- **`class-web-design-package-meta.php`** registers `PRICE`/`TAGLINE`/`FEATURED` via `register_post_meta()`; the feature bullet list (`FEATURES`, an array) is deliberately *not* registered — nothing outside this record's own editor ever reads or writes it, so there's no REST schema to declare, and plain `get_post_meta()`/`update_post_meta()` already round-trips an array fine without registration.
- **`class-web-design-package-editor.php`** — a plain meta box (Price/Tagline/Featured-checkbox/Features), no JS repeater. The feature list is one bullet per line in a `<textarea>`, not the JS-driven bulk-discount-tier repeater `class-pricing-rule-editor.php` uses for its own multi-field rows — that repeater exists for rows with several fields each; this is a flat list of single strings, and a textarea covers "add/remove/reorder a line" with zero new JS.
- **`wp yeffoprint setup-web-design-packages`** (new WP-CLI command, same idempotent, dev-triggered, never-touches-existing-work shape as `setup-pages`) seeds the exact three placeholder tiers the old hardcoded array held, so a fresh deploy of this change isn't a blank pricing section the moment it ships — the owner then edits real numbers on each record from the admin panel, which is the whole point of this pass.
- **`web-design-packages.php` now queries `YeffoPrint_Web_Design_Package_Meta::get_published()`** instead of reading a literal array. The "Placeholder — edit before launch" flag survives as a runtime check instead of always-on: a tier's price gets the flag only while it still reads exactly `"$X,XXX"` (the seed command's own starting value) — edited to anything else, the flag disappears on its own, no separate "is this real yet" field to keep in sync. A genuinely empty CPT (nothing seeded yet, or every tier moved to draft) falls back to a plain "Packages coming soon — contact us" line instead of rendering an empty grid.
- **`yp_web_design_pkg` added to `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::POST_TYPES`**, so its list screen and editor pick up the shared admin reskin automatically, same as every other record type — no new CSS.

### Follow-up: the post type didn't register at all — a real bug, not a deploy issue

Direct report: "I don't see that Web Design Packages editor in the YeffoPrint submenu," then, after confirming the code had actually deployed: "still getting invalid post type" on `edit.php?post_type=yp_web_design_package` directly. That second detail ruled out every deploy-timing explanation — WordPress's own "Invalid post type." message only shows when `register_post_type()` was never successfully called for that slug on the request in question, and confirming the code was live meant that call *was* running, just failing.

Root cause: `yp_web_design_package` is **21 characters**. WordPress hard-caps a post type's name at 20 (`wp_posts.post_type` is a `varchar(20)`, and `register_post_type()` itself checks the length and returns a `WP_Error` — silently, not a fatal — for anything longer, rather than truncating or erroring loudly). That `WP_Error` was never checked here, so the failure was invisible: no crash, no admin notice, just a CPT that never entered `$wp_post_types` — exactly consistent with everything observed (menu item never appears, direct URL says "Invalid post type," no other functionality broke since nothing else depends on this specific registration succeeding).

Renamed the slug to `yp_web_design_pkg` (17 characters) everywhere it appeared — the `register_post_type()`/`register_post_meta()` calls, the `save_post_{post_type}`/`manage_{post_type}_posts_columns`/`manage_{post_type}_posts_custom_column` hook names (which embed the post type slug directly, so they needed the same rename), `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell::POST_TYPES`, the seed command's `wp_insert_post()` calls, and this doc. The PHP class names (`YeffoPrint_Web_Design_Package_Meta`, `YeffoPrint_Web_Design_Package_Editor`, …) were left as-is — only the raw post type slug string has WordPress's 20-character constraint, class names don't.

### Follow-up: the maintenance badge now opens a modal instead of linking out

Direct report: "The add maintenance and monitoring badge doesn't do anything when clicked. Can we change that to show a modal window styled like the site with information on what's included." The badge always had a real `href` (the Stripe Payment Link, or `/contact/` as a fallback), but a bare link straight out — with no chance to explain what the subscription actually includes first — reads as "does nothing" even when it technically navigates somewhere.

- **The badge is now a real `<button>`** (`data-yp-drawer-trigger="yp-maintenance-modal"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`) instead of an `<a>` — same visual pill, with `border`/`background`/`font-family`/`cursor` resets in `patterns.css` undoing default button chrome so nothing about its look changed. Opens a `.yp-drawer.yp-drawer--center` modal, the exact same accessible primitive already used for the header's search/cart panels, the splash screen, and the material guide's photo lightboxes (`assets/js/site.js`'s `openDrawer`/`closeDrawer`) — no new JS needed, wired entirely through the existing `data-yp-drawer-trigger`/`data-yp-drawer-close` markup convention.
- **Modal content**: the intro paragraph the old, now-deleted `web-design-maintenance-teaser.php` section used to show, a five-item feature list (reusing `.yp-web-design-package__features`' own check-icon bullet styling verbatim — the same list treatment already used inside each pricing card, so this doesn't introduce a second "list of things included" visual language), and a CTA button. That button is what actually carries the Payment-Link-with-`/contact/`-fallback logic now (`$maintenance_url`, unchanged) — its label reads "Subscribe to Maintenance" once a real Payment Link is set, "Ask About Maintenance" while it's still falling back to `/contact/`, so the button never overpromises a live checkout that isn't wired up yet.
- **The feature list itself is illustrative, not confirmed copy** — plausible, uncontroversial maintenance-plan inclusions (updates, uptime monitoring, backups, security monitoring, priority support), the same spirit as the package tiers' own placeholder content, just not visually flagged as a placeholder the way pricing is: this is soft descriptive copy, not a number a reader could mistake for a real commitment. Easy to hand-edit directly in `web-design-packages.php`'s modal markup whenever the owner has final wording.

## Web Design page gated to admins while it's still being finished (direct request: "only admins can see it right now... other users should see a coming soon page instead")

- **A `page_template_hierarchy` filter, not a redirect or a condition baked into the real page's own patterns** — `class-web-design-page-gate.php` (new `includes/access/` directory — a genuinely new concern, not a fit for `woocommerce/`, `admin/`, or any other existing one). This is the standard, documented WordPress mechanism for "resolve this page to a different block template based on a runtime condition": `get_page_template()` runs its list of candidate template filenames through this filter before searching for the first one that exists, so prepending `'web-design-coming-soon.html'` when the visitor lacks `manage_options` (the same capability every other admin-only surface in this plugin already gates on — Dashboard, Settings, …) wins that search. The real `/web-design/` URL keeps resolving normally either way — a redirect would have meant a second URL to keep straight, and an admin previewing their own in-progress page needs to just visit it directly, not get bounced.
- **New `templates/web-design-coming-soon.html` + `patterns/web-design-coming-soon.php`** — same header/footer template-part shell every other page uses, one small centered pattern (eyebrow, "Coming Soon" H1, a line of copy, a Contact Us button) in place of the real page's five. No `theme.json` `customTemplates` entry — that array only makes a template *manually selectable* from Pages → Page Attributes, and this one is never assigned that way; it's only ever reached through the capability filter above.
- **The header's "Web Design" nav link is untouched, deliberately** — a non-admin visitor clicking it now lands on a normal-looking "coming soon" page instead of the real one, which reads as intentional (a common, expected pattern) rather than a broken link. Hiding the nav link entirely wasn't asked for and would have been a second, separate piece of visibility logic to keep in sync with this one.

### Follow-up: gate removed — page is launched

Direct request: "remove the admin gate from the web design page, ready to launch." `class-web-design-page-gate.php` (and its `require_once`/instantiation in `class-yeffoprint-core.php`) is deleted outright rather than left disabled — nothing else depended on it, and an inert gate class sitting in the codebase reads as still-in-progress to the next person who finds it. `templates/web-design-coming-soon.html` and `patterns/web-design-coming-soon.php` are deleted along with it: the capability filter above was their only caller, so once it's gone they're unreachable dead code, not backup content worth keeping around. `/web-design/` now resolves to its real template for every visitor, admin or not.

## Follow-up: the homepage's Featured Designs section wasn't actually filtering by Featured

Direct report: "The featured indicator on the vial templates is not what the homepage uses to decide what to put there. I checked the box that said featured on the [a] template and it doesn't show on the homepage." A code-level audit found no bug to point at — the Template editor's Featured checkbox and the homepage's Query Loop block both agreed on the same meta key (`YeffoPrint_Template_Meta::FEATURED`, `_yp_featured`), and the Shop Labels archive's own "sort by featured" query used it consistently too. Nothing was actually wrong in any of the code that *wrote* or *named* the flag.

Root-caused instead by elimination, over three rounds of owner-confirmed observations: the homepage section was showing designs, but never the owner's newly-starred one; fewer than 4 templates total were starred (ruling out "your item got pushed off a full page by date order"); and, decisively, the designs actually showing on the homepage weren't themselves starred Featured either. That combination only has one explanation — the section wasn't filtering by Featured at all, it was just showing the 4 most recent `yp_template` posts regardless of status. `featured-designs.php`'s Query Loop block relied on WordPress core translating its own `metaKey`/`metaValue` JSON attributes (`"metaKey":"_yp_featured","metaValue":"1"`) into an actual meta filter at render time — a mechanism that, on this site, silently wasn't taking effect.

Fixed by not depending on that translation at all: `featured-designs.php` now resolves the featured post IDs itself in plain PHP (`get_posts()` with `meta_key`/`meta_value`, `fields => 'ids'`, capped at 4, newest first) — the same reliable "read live WordPress data with a direct query" convention `materials.php`/`material-guide.php`/`web-design-packages.php` already use rather than trusting a block attribute to do it — and hands that exact ID list to the Query Loop via its `include` attribute, a plain array of post IDs with no JSON-to-meta_query translation step to fail. Card rendering itself (`wp:post-template` + the `yeffoprint/template-card` block) is untouched. The whole section is now wrapped in a PHP `if ( $featured_ids )` guard, so it disappears entirely rather than rendering an empty grid on the rare day nothing is currently marked Featured. No theme version bump needed — this edits an existing pattern's file in place rather than adding or renaming one.

### Correction: `include` isn't a real Query Loop attribute either — the first fix above didn't actually fix it

Owner follow-up after the fix above shipped: "The code updated... nothing has changed. I'm still seeing the same templates that ARE NOT marked as featured." Checked from a private window, so this wasn't a browser-cache report.

The first fix's `include` attribute turned out to be the same mistake as the `metaKey`/`metaValue` it replaced, just a different unsupported key. WordPress core's `core/query` block only ever translates a fixed, documented set of `query` attribute keys into `WP_Query` args (`postType`, `perPage`, `offset`, `order`, `orderBy`, `author`, `search`, `exclude`, `sticky`, `inherit`, `taxQuery`, `parents`, `format`) — confirmed against Gutenberg's own "Extending the Query Loop block" documentation, which is explicit that a custom `post__in`-style filter isn't part of that set and has to go through the `query_loop_block_query_vars` filter instead. `include` was never among the recognized keys, so it was silently dropped exactly like `metaKey`/`metaValue` before it, and the block kept falling back to "4 most recent `yp_template` posts."

Rather than lean on a WordPress core filter hook a second time (with its own timing questions — this pattern file's PHP runs once, per request, when the pattern's content is resolved, and the Query Loop's own dynamic render happens moments later against whatever block-comment markup that PHP produced; getting a filter's add/remove window to straddle that correctly was more moving parts than the bug was worth), `featured-designs.php` now skips the Query Loop block's query-building entirely for this section: it resolves the featured IDs via `get_posts()` as before, then hand-renders each card by instantiating the `yeffoprint/template-card` block directly (`new WP_Block( [...], [ 'postType' => 'yp_template', 'postId' => $id ] )`) — the same `postId` context the Query Loop's own Post Template block would have set, just supplied directly instead of asking core to derive it from a query. The output markup (`div.wp-block-query` > `ul.wp-block-post-template.yp-card-grid` > `li` per card) matches exactly what core would have rendered, so `patterns.css`'s existing `.yp-card-grid` rules — which already assumed an `<li>` one level above `.yp-template-card`, see that rule's own comment — apply unchanged. Deliberately no `<!-- wp:query -->` block comment left in the output: `core/query` is a dynamic block, and if that comment were still there, `do_blocks()` would call its own render callback on it and rebuild the query from scratch from the same unsupported attributes, discarding the hand-rendered content underneath it.

`popular-designs.php` (a separate, currently-unused-on-any-page pattern) has this exact same `metaKey`/`orderBy":"meta_value_num"` shape and is almost certainly equally broken wherever it eventually gets used — flagged here rather than fixed now, since it isn't wired into any template yet and fixing it wasn't part of what was reported.

## Info modals for the label configurator's Size and Material sections (direct request: "a tooltip or info button near materials and size... a modal with the material information we created earlier... a modal that shows the label sizes side by side with a rectangular drawing showing the dimensions")

Two new "?" buttons next to the Size and Material headings on the configurator (`templates/single-yp_template.html`) — reusing `.yp-field__tooltip-trigger`'s existing small circular button style (previously only used for the per-field admin-description tooltip) so the whole page has one consistent info-icon language, wrapped in a new `.yp-section-heading-row` flex row so the button sits beside the heading without joining its `aria-labelledby` text. Each opens a `.yp-drawer--center` modal via the site's existing drawer primitive — both modals are static server-rendered patterns referenced with `<!-- wp:pattern -->` from the template, not built by configurator.js, so they're present and openable the instant the page loads rather than waiting on the configurator's own REST fetch.

- **Material info modal** (`patterns/material-info-modal.php`) reuses "the material information we created earlier" literally — the same Material Guide copy already built for the How It Works page (`patterns/material-guide.php`), not a second, independently-authored blurb. The per-material array and its live-data resolution (matching each entry's `slug` against a published `yp_material` record for its real photo and thickness) moved out of `material-guide.php` into a new shared `yeffoprint_material_guide_entries()` (`functions.php`), so both surfaces read the exact same data and can't drift apart from editing one and not the other; `material-guide.php`'s own rendering (including its per-material zoom-photo drawers) is otherwise untouched. The modal shows every material the store offers, not just whichever ones happen to be compatible with the design currently open — this is general "how do I choose" reference content, the same list already shown site-wide, not something scoped per-Template.
- **Size info modal** (`patterns/size-info-modal.php`) is new content, since there was no pre-existing size write-up to reuse — draws each of *this* Template's compatible sizes (`Template.compatible_sizes`, the same list `class-template-schema-controller.php` already resolves for the picker itself) as an inline SVG rectangle sized from the Size record's real `PRINT_WIDTH_MM`/`PRINT_HEIGHT_MM` meta, labeled with its dimensions in both mm and inches. Every rectangle shares one scale factor (the largest side across all of this Template's compatible sizes maps to a fixed pixel box) rather than each being scaled independently to fill its own box — independent scaling would make every size's rectangle look the same size regardless of its real dimensions, defeating the entire point of "see how much bigger this one actually is." A size with no width/height set yet in the admin (both still 0, the field's default) shows a dashed placeholder box and "Dimensions not set yet" instead of a zero-size rectangle.
- Both patterns call `get_the_ID()`/`get_post( get_the_ID() )` directly — safe here specifically because `<!-- wp:pattern -->` block templates don't execute PHP themselves, but a referenced pattern file does, and by the time it runs the main query's post is already resolved for this exact singular-`yp_template` request, the same assumption `functions.php`'s own `wp_enqueue_scripts` hook already relies on (`get_the_ID()` there feeds `yeffoprintConfigurator.templateId`, which the whole configurator already depends on working). Both patterns are marked `Inserter: no` — pure page infrastructure, never meant to be inserted standalone into arbitrary page content by an editor.
- Two new pattern files added → theme `Version` bumped `0.4.0` → `0.5.0` per this project's own established rule (see the Version-bump comment in `style.css`) — otherwise WordPress's cached pattern scan wouldn't know either new file exists.

## Fix: gallery card badges (New/Popular/Featured/Customizable) rendered as blank white pills

Direct report: "The badges appear blank on the templates I select a badge for." Not a data/lookup bug — `yeffoprint_core_badge_label()` and the Template editor's Badge dropdown were both already correct; the badge `<span>` was rendering with the right text every time.

The bug was purely CSS: the base `.yp-card__badge` rule (`global.css`) sets a colorful gradient background with white text. `.yp-template-card__badge` (`patterns.css`, gallery/homepage cards specifically) overrides that background to plain white — so the vial photo shows through cleanly behind the pill — but never overrode `color`, so the label text stayed white too: white text on a white pill, invisible on every card, on every badge value, since the rule was first written. Fixed by giving this override its own `color` (brand magenta, reads clearly against the white pill) and a neutral low-elevation shadow in place of the base rule's gradient-tuned pink one.

## Custom admin dashboard, Phase 1: the app shell foundation

Direct request: "start building the custom admin UI for YeffoPrint... I still don't like the way WordPress version is." The existing admin experience (`YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell`, `admin-shell.css`) is a CSS reskin *over* wp-admin's own chrome — the same list tables, postboxes, and page-reload-per-save rhythm, just repainted. The owner wants a genuinely custom, app-style dashboard instead, built in verifiable pieces. A full 8-phase plan was written up front (three research passes over the existing admin surface, REST surface, and design system) and approved along with a clickable mockup — see the plan's own Context section for the full reasoning; this entry covers Phase 1, the foundation everything else builds on.

**Architecture, decided up front and now built**: this stays inside wp-admin's existing auth boundary (same `yeffoprint` top-level menu slug, same `manage_options` gate — no second login system) but the moment that one page loads, WordPress's own sidebar/toolbar/footer are hidden and a custom-branded shell takes over the full viewport. No bundler, no framework — plain JS, matching this project's existing "no unjustified frameworks" stance. A new admin REST API layer (`yeffoprint-core/v1/admin/*`) backs every future screen instead of classic meta-box form POSTs.

- **`YeffoPrint_Admin_App`** (`includes/admin-app/class-admin-app.php`) replaces `YeffoPrint_Admin_Menu::render_dashboard()` as the `yeffoprint` slug's render callback (`class-admin-menu.php` now points both its `add_menu_page()`/`add_submenu_page()` registrations at `YeffoPrint_Admin_App::render()`, which outputs nothing but an empty `<div id="yp-admin-app">` — everything else is JS). `render_dashboard()`/`YeffoPrint_Dashboard_Widgets` are left in place, unused, for a real Dashboard home view once enough of the plan has shipped to summarize (Phase 6) — same reasoning as every other "kept for a later phase, not deleted" note in this doc.
- **Real design tokens, not a mirror** — the whole point of doing this differently from the existing reskin (see that class's own docblock: it hardcodes a literal copy of every theme.json value, "documented, deliberate, but drift-prone"). `YeffoPrint_Admin_Token_Bridge` (`includes/admin-app/class-admin-token-bridge.php`) reads `wp_get_global_settings()` — the theme's actual, live theme.json, already fully merged by WordPress's own global-styles engine — and flattens it into the same `--wp--preset--…`/`--wp--custom--…` custom properties the storefront computes, scoped under `body.yeffoprint-app` and inline-enqueued via `wp_add_inline_style()` on an empty (`false` src) registered handle, a standard core pattern for attaching computed CSS to a dependency chain. The admin app also enqueues the theme's own `global.css` directly (not a copy) — buttons, the `.yp-drawer` primitive, and every token this app uses come from the exact same file the storefront loads, so there's no second copy to let drift.
- **New admin REST layer**: `YeffoPrint_Rest_Security::admin_write()` (added alongside the existing `guest_or_nonced_write()`) is the shared permission callback for every `/admin/*` route — logged-in + `manage_options` + the same `X-WP-Nonce` check the storefront's own nonce-gated endpoints already use, just with no guest pass-through (nothing under `/admin/` should ever be reachable without a session, unlike storefront writes). The first and only route so far, `GET /admin/ping` (`includes/rest/admin/class-admin-ping-controller.php`), does nothing but return the current user's display name — a deliberately trivial first endpoint that proves the *entire* chain (capability gate on the page → nonce baked into the page → this endpoint's own permission check) works end to end before any real screen has to debug auth and a feature at the same time.
- **`assets/admin-app/app.js`** — plain ES-module-free vanilla JS (no bundler): renders the nav (grouped to match the plan's own phase list — Overview/Catalog/Sales/Store), a hash router (`#/materials`, `#/pricing`, …) with zero full-page reloads, and the one real view in Phase 1 (Dashboard) which calls `/admin/ping` on load and shows "Connected as {name}" in the topbar — with the same stale-nonce recovery (`GET /session/nonce`, retry once) the storefront's own REST calls already use. Every other nav item renders a shared placeholder ("this section's screen ships in a later phase") so the whole map is navigable from day one, matching the mockup shown before this shipped.
- **`assets/admin-app/app-shell.css`** hides `#wpadminbar`/`#adminmenumain`/`#wpfooter`/admin notices and zeroes `#wpcontent`/`#wpbody`/`#wpbody-content`'s padding, all scoped under `body.yeffoprint-app` (added server-side via an `admin_body_class` filter, so there's no flash of WP chrome before JS runs) — new admin-only chrome (the nav panel, topbar, placeholder card) is authored fresh, everything else (buttons, cards, the drawer primitive that later phases will need) comes from the theme's own `global.css`.

Verify: load the `YeffoPrint` menu item, confirm the WP sidebar/toolbar are gone and the custom nav/shell render instead, click through every placeholder section, and confirm the topbar shows "Connected as {name}" once the ping round-trip succeeds.

### Follow-up: the mobile nav was stacking above the page content instead of collapsing

Direct report, screenshotted on an iPhone: "Should the sidebar be loading above the content though?" The original `@media (max-width: 860px)` rule only dropped the shell's two-column grid to one column and un-stuck the nav — it never actually hid it, so on a phone the full ~15-item nav rendered in normal document flow *above* the topbar/content, pushing the actual Dashboard view a full screen or more below the fold every time.

Fixed by giving the nav a real off-canvas treatment below that same breakpoint: hidden by default (`transform: translateX(-100%)`), slid in over the content via a new hamburger toggle in the topbar rather than pushing content down, with a backdrop that closes it on click — the same backdrop-plus-slide-panel language the site's own `.yp-drawer` primitive already uses elsewhere, even though this is the admin app's own lightweight equivalent rather than that exact component (this app doesn't share DOM/markup with the storefront's drawer instances). Picking a nav item closes the panel automatically. Above 860px this is all inert — the toggle button is hidden and the nav stays in its normal, always-visible column, unchanged from before.

(The "tap YeffoPrint twice on mobile to reach Dashboard" follow-up the owner also raised was deliberately **not** fixed — it's WordPress's own standard behavior for any top-level admin menu with children on a touch/collapsed viewport (try any other admin menu with submenus; same two-tap pattern), not a regression from this work, and fixing it would mean attaching global click-handling JS to `#adminmenu` on every wp-admin page — the one boundary `YeffoPrint_Admin_Shell`'s own docblock has deliberately never crossed. Not worth the tradeoff for one extra tap.)

## Custom admin dashboard, Phase 2: Materials and Sizes (the first real CRUD screens)

Direct request: "Go ahead and start phase 2." Both screens read and write straight through WordPress core's own `/wp/v2/yp_material`/`/wp/v2/yp_size` REST routes — no new PHP controller needed for either, since both post types and every field they use already had `show_in_rest` on (`class-post-type-registry.php`'s `args()` helper, `class-commerce-record-meta.php`'s `register_post_meta()` calls) from when they were first built. This is exactly the REST-surface research from the plan's Context section paying off: Materials/Sizes/Sticker Sizes/Web Design Packages/Maintenance Subscribers were flagged as screens that could "largely piggyback on WP core's own routes," and Materials — the more complex of the two, with images and a description — confirms that held up in practice.

- **`assets/admin-app/app.js` refactored into a shared `window.YPAdminApp` surface** every future view script builds on: `request()` (the nonce-header-plus-stale-nonce-retry logic Phase 1's `ping()` already had, generalized so every REST call in the app — this plugin's own `/admin/*` routes and WP core's `/wp/v2/*` routes alike — goes through one place), `escapeHtml`/`escapeAttr`, `bindMediaPicker()` (a `wp.media()` wrapper that binds by element reference rather than a fixed DOM id, since this app's forms render fresh into a drawer each time rather than existing once in a static page — `assets/admin/vial-mockup-picker.js`'s fixed-id version doesn't fit), and `openDrawer()`/`closeDrawer()`/`initDrawer()` — this app's own small equivalent of the storefront's `.yp-drawer` behavior (site.js's real `openDrawer`/`closeDrawer` are private to that file's own IIFE and site.js isn't loaded in wp-admin anyway), reusing that primitive's CSS classes from `global.css` unchanged. `renderView()` now dispatches to `YP.views[id]` when a view script has registered one, falling back to Phase 1's placeholder otherwise — each new phase adds its own `assets/admin-app/views/{name}.js`, enqueued with a dependency on `app.js`, without this file needing to know about it in advance.
- Route dispatch itself moved from firing synchronously at the end of `app.js` (Phase 1) to firing on `DOMContentLoaded` — necessary once view scripts are separate deferred files: `defer` guarantees they all run, in order, *before* `DOMContentLoaded`, so waiting for that event is what guarantees every view has had the chance to register into `YP.views` before the first `route()` call might need one.
- **`assets/admin-app/records.css`** — the shared list/table/pill/chip/form-field styles both screens use (and every future catalog CRUD screen will reuse rather than re-authoring). The add/edit drawer itself is the theme's own `.yp-drawer--center` from `global.css`, not a new component.
- Both screens: a searchable list (client-side filter, since these datasets are small), add/edit via the drawer, delete (soft — moves to Trash, recoverable from the classic screen if needed, not `force=true` permanent deletion), and reordering via explicit ↑/↓ buttons rather than drag-and-drop — a deliberate scope trim: full HTML5 drag-and-drop reordering adds real complexity and its own accessibility considerations for comparatively little gain over two buttons that do the same `menu_order` swap.
- Materials' swatch image uses the post's native `featured_media` (core REST exposes it directly, plus `_embed=1` on the list request resolves each row's thumbnail URL in one call); the hover/on-vial image is a plain meta attachment ID (`_yp_hover_image_id`) with no embedded URL, so opening the edit drawer for a record that has one makes one extra `GET /wp/v2/media/{id}` to resolve its preview.
- One small bug caught before shipping: `.yp-icon-button` (`global.css`) is a fixed-size circular button meant for a single icon/glyph, not a text label — row actions ("Edit", "Delete", the reorder arrows) and the media pickers' "Remove" buttons needed their own compact text-button class (`.yp-row-action`, `records.css`) instead.

Verify: open Materials, add one with a swatch image, edit it, reorder it, delete it (confirm it's recoverable from `edit.php?post_type=yp_material&post_status=trash`) — then confirm the live label configurator (`/shop-labels/{template}/`, or the Material info modal) reflects every change. Repeat for Sizes and confirm the Size info modal / configurator's size picker updates too.

### Fix: the whole admin app rendered with no styling at all
