Direct follow-up, prompted by the owner asking to double back on this: the "no migration plugin exists" premise above was wrong. `yeffoprint-migrate` — a standalone plugin at the repo's top level, alongside `yeffoprint`/`yeffoprint-core` rather than inside either — already existed, built earlier in this same working history to selectively transplant WooCommerce settings/users/orders from an old YeffoPrint site into a *different* new one (browser/AJAX-driven, deliberately no products/media/theme content, deliberately no WP-CLI). It was missed because the search that produced the entry above only checked `wp-content/plugins/` and `yeffoprint-core` itself, not the repo's other top-level plugin directories — and `yeffoprint-migrate` has no entry in this file at all, on purpose: like its README says, it's migration tooling, not part of the site's permanent architecture, so it was never meant to be documented here the way `yeffoprint-core`/`yeffoprint` features are.

That existing plugin's own scope (no products, no media, browser-only) genuinely doesn't cover *this* request (a same-site, code-already-handled-by-git, everything-comes-along server move) — so the two tools aren't actually duplicates, they solve different problems. But the new capability plainly belongs in `yeffoprint-migrate`, not permanently bolted onto `yeffoprint-core`, for the same reason the original tool lives there: it's temporary, deactivate-when-done tooling, not part of the site's permanent architecture. Moved `includes/cli/class-migrate-command.php` from `yeffoprint-core` into `yeffoprint-migrate/includes/class-cli-backup-command.php` unchanged apart from the rename, and renamed the WP-CLI command from `wp yeffoprint migrate export|import` to `wp yeffoprint-migrate backup export|import` — a distinct top-level namespace so it can't be confused with `yeffoprint-core`'s own unrelated `wp yeffoprint seed`/etc. commands, or with this plugin's other, browser-based migration feature. See `yeffoprint-migrate/README.md` for the up-to-date usage of both tools this plugin now provides.

## Manual order creation (direct request: "am I able to make a custom order manually that also is with a proof to be approved by the customer?", broadened immediately after to "manually create any orders, not just custom orders")

Investigation found a real gap: staff could already create a WooCommerce order natively in wp-admin, but there was no admin-side way to create a `yp_custom_order` record — the thing a proof actually attaches to. That record (and its `ACCESS_TOKEN`, the credential behind the customer's proof-approval email link) was only ever minted by the customer's own Custom Design submission form. The proof-attach/notify/approval machinery itself (`YeffoPrint_Proof_Meta::attach_file()`) needed nothing new — it just had nothing admin-created to attach to.

**The key seam, found by reading `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Payment::link_paid_custom_orders()`** (hooked to `woocommerce_order_status_processing` among others): it scans *every* order line item for a `_yp_custom_order_id` meta value, generically, regardless of which product the item is on, and publishes/links whichever linked `yp_custom_order` post is still `draft`. A manually-created order just needs to carry that meta on its line item(s) when proof approval is requested, and actually transition to `processing` (`$order->update_status('processing', …)` — this is what *fires* the hook, unlike passing `status` directly into `wc_create_order()`). Do that, and the entire existing linking/publishing/email pipeline runs unmodified — no new linking code anywhere.

**Two small, behavior-preserving extractions made this possible without duplicating logic:**
- `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::snapshot()` (`includes/woocommerce/class-order-item-meta.php`) — the hook that freezes size/material/pricing/custom-order-id onto a line item — never actually read its own `$cart_item_key`/`$order` parameters. Its body is now `public static function apply( $item, $values, ?int $tier_quantity = null )`; `snapshot()` is a 2-line wrapper. The new optional `$tier_quantity` param (left `null` for the checkout hook, unchanged) is needed because `apply()`'s internal bulk-discount-tier lookup otherwise defaults to reading the *live `WC()->cart`* — correct at real checkout, meaningless for a manually-built order that never touches a cart, where it would have silently priced against an empty/irrelevant admin session cart instead of the order's own batch.
- `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Meta::create_shell()` — extracted from `class-custom-order-controller.php::submit()`'s inline `wp_insert_post()` + `ACCESS_TOKEN` block. Mints the post + token + customer identity, nothing mode-specific; `submit()` now calls it too (mechanical, zero behavior change).

**New `YeffoPrint_Manual_Order_Creator`** (`includes/custom-orders/class-manual-order-creator.php`) builds the order directly — `wc_create_order()` → `$order->add_product()` per row, priced with the exact same `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::calculate_for_cart_item()` the live cart uses (so the charged price and the snapshotted price can never diverge — both come from one deterministic call with identical inputs) → `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::apply()` for the snapshot. Never touches `WC()->cart` — there's nothing to "check out," the order needs to exist correctly the moment the call returns. Customer resolution reuses `YeffoPrint_Social_Login::find_or_create_user()`'s exact account-creation call (`wc_create_new_customer()`, matched by email first so nothing duplicates). Rolls back (deletes the order and any shell already created) on any failure partway through, mirroring `submit()`'s own cleanup pattern.

New admin REST controller `class-admin-manual-order-controller.php` (`GET /admin/manual-orders/customer-search`, `POST /admin/manual-orders`) and admin-app screen `views/manual-order.js` (`#/manual-order`, nav: Sales → "Create Order") — customer search-or-create, a batch-row repeater and live pricing preview reusing the *existing public* `/custom-orders/options` and `/custom-orders/pricing-preview` endpoints unchanged (zero new pricing/options surface), and a "Requires proof approval before printing" checkbox (checked by default) that's the only thing deciding whether a `yp_custom_order` shell gets minted at all.

**Phase A shipped Custom Design orders** — the simplest order type, and the one that exercised every piece of new shared plumbing (customer resolution, direct order assembly outside checkout, the proof-approval linkage).

**Phase B adds Custom Sticker orders**, reusing every piece of that plumbing unchanged — same `wc_create_order()` → `add_product()` → `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::apply()` shape, same `create_shell()`/proof-linkage seam, just `order_type: 'sticker'` instead of `'custom_design'`. Two differences from Custom Design, both mirroring how the customer-facing `class-custom-sticker-controller.php::submit()` itself works: no fee item at all (Custom Stickers has none, unlike Custom Design's separate $25 design fee — one line item, not fee-plus-rows), and no batching (one sticker configuration per manual order, so the bulk-discount tier is just that row's own quantity — there's no cart-wide pool to combine against the way a real cart's `combined_sticker_quantity()` would use). Priced/validated with the same authoritative `YeffoPrint_Sticker_Pricing::calculate()` the storefront submission validates against, so an invalid size/material/dimensions produces the exact same error a customer would see. Artwork upload reuses the shared `/custom-orders/uploads` endpoint (already reachable by a logged-in, nonce-carrying staff session via `guest_or_nonced_write`) but is **optional** here, unlike the storefront form's hard requirement — staff keying in a phone/email order often haven't received the file yet and shouldn't be blocked from getting the order into the pipeline over it. This also required fixing a real gap Phase A had left in the sticker snapshot path: `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::apply()`'s sticker branch and its private `snapshot_custom_sticker()` weren't threading the `$tier_quantity` override through to `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::calculate_sticker_for_cart_item()` the way the labels branch already did (fixed in the same Phase A extraction) — a manually-created sticker order would otherwise have snapshotted a price computed against whatever happened to be in the admin session's own (empty/irrelevant) cart.

Custom Stickers has no public pricing-preview endpoint the way Custom Design does — the storefront prices a sticker live via the cart itself, which this admin screen has no equivalent of. New, minimal `POST /admin/manual-orders/sticker-pricing-preview` wraps the same `YeffoPrint_Sticker_Pricing::calculate()` directly, admin-only (`admin_write`), for exactly this screen's own live total.

**Phase C (Template label) is what's left** — the first case where a proof-requiring shell wraps a real templated order rather than a freeform Custom Design/Sticker one, so it'll need its own narrow `ORDER_TYPE`/rendering branch when it ships. Deliberately no `ORDER_TYPE`/admin-screen changes for either Phase A or B: a Phase-A shell (brand name + batch rows + fee item) and a Phase-B shell (`order_type: 'sticker'`, no fee item) are each byte-for-byte the same shape as an existing customer-submitted order of the same type, so the whole existing Custom Orders screen renders both correctly already.

Verify (Custom Design): `#/manual-order` → search or create a customer → add a batch row → leave "Requires proof approval" checked → Create Order. Confirm a `processing` WC order with correct line items/pricing/order note, a published `yp_custom_order` shell, and that "+ Add Proof" from `#/orders/{id}` sends a working, logged-out-accessible approval link exactly like a normal storefront order.

Verify (Custom Sticker): `#/manual-order` → Custom Sticker tab → search or create a customer → choose size/material/type/shape/quantity (and width/height if the size is the custom tier) → confirm the live price preview matches what the storefront form would show for the same inputs → Create Order. Confirm a `processing` WC order with one sticker line item (no fee item), correct snapshot meta, and (if proof was requested) a published `yp_custom_order` shell with `ORDER_TYPE = 'sticker'` that the existing Custom Orders screen renders correctly.

## Web Design page: hosting disclosure, hosting add-on, "What's Included" accuracy, and a dedicated quote-intake form (direct request, four parts)

Packages previously read as if everything was bundled — no note that hosting/domain registration cost extra, and the "What's Included" icon grid (`web-design-included.php`) asserted Custom Design, Domain Setup, and Business Email as blanket truths (they vary by tier) and Ongoing Support as if it were included (it isn't — it's the existing Maintenance & Monitoring add-on). Every "Get a Quote" link also pointed at the generic 4-field Contact form instead of anything web-design-specific.

**Tier-varies wasn't actually a schema gap.** Each `yp_web_design_pkg` tier already has its own freeform `FEATURES` bullet list (`class-web-design-package-meta.php`) — an owner can already list "Domain Setup" only under the tiers that include it. The real problem was the *icon grid* asserting these as universal. Fixed as a copy change only: those four items' descriptions now say "Included in select packages — see plan details above" (Custom Design/Domain Setup/Business Email) or "available as an add-on to any package" (Ongoing Support); WooCommerce Setup and Plugins & Features are untouched since those genuinely are universal.

**Hosting add-on** ($35/mo — includes business email + a 1-year domain registration; a one-time $75 setup fee applies only if the customer has no existing host and doesn't want to use their own) is a new second badge in `web-design-packages.php`, reusing the exact Maintenance badge/modal pattern already there (same `.yp-web-design-maintenance-badge` class — generic despite the name — same centered-`.yp-drawer` modal, `data-yp-drawer-trigger`, no new JS). The two badges now sit in a `.yp-web-design-badge-row` flex column instead of each auto-centering independently with no gap between them. No Stripe payment link exists for hosting the way Maintenance has one, so its modal CTA is a lead-in to the new quote form rather than a subscribe button. A disclaimer paragraph under the pricing grid states plainly that hosting/domain registration aren't included in any package.

**New quote-request page**, `/web-design-quote/` (`class-pages-setup-command.php`), replaces `/contact/` as the target of every "Get a Quote" link on the Web Design page (hero, packages intro, each package card, the hosting modal, the closing CTA) — direct request for questions a real web-design lead needs answered that the generic Contact form doesn't ask (existing site? hosting? domain? which package? timeline?). Built as a near-exact structural copy of the Contact form's whole stack rather than a new pattern:
- `class-web-design-quote-controller.php` mirrors `class-contact-controller.php` almost line for line — same honeypot, same per-IP rate limit (own transient prefix), same "email is the record, no CPT" shape (see that controller's own docblock for why), same Reply-To-the-submitter `wp_mail()`, same `do_action(...)` seam (`yeffoprint_web_design_quote_submitted`) so `class-telegram-admin-alerts.php` can alert on it too, exactly like it already does for `yeffoprint_contact_form_submitted`.
- `templates/web-design-quote.html` mirrors `contact-form.html`'s markup shape (`.yp-custom-order.yp-section` wrapper, `.yp-field` label/input pairs, the same honeypot convention) but with the richer field set: business/contact basics, what they sell, existing-website (conditional URL field), rough product count, package interest, launch timeline, existing hosting/domain (conditional domain field), interest in the hosting add-on and in ongoing maintenance, and an open "anything else" textarea. Only business name, contact name, email, what-they-sell, and package are server-required — everything else is optional so the form doesn't read as a wall of mandatory questions.
- The package `<select>` is populated client-side from `yeffoprintWebDesignQuote.packages` — a plain array of the same published tier titles the pricing table itself reads (`YeffoPrint_Web_Design_Package_Meta::get_published()`), localized directly in `functions.php`'s existing template-slug-gated enqueue block (no new REST call needed, since that list is already known server-side at render time) — plus a trailing "Not sure yet" option appended in JS.
- `assets/js/web-design-quote-form.js` mirrors `contact-form.js`'s fetch/status-swap shape, adding only the package-select population and two conditional-field reveals (has-website → website URL, has-domain → domain name), both patterned on `custom-design-form.js`'s own radio-driven show/hide convention.

## Fix: every admin-app button rendered unstyled (direct report — "the forms on the admin create order pages... look unfinished")

Root cause was global, not specific to that one screen: `global.css`'s `.wp-block-button.is-style-accent`/`.is-style-outline` rules are descendant selectors — `.wp-block-button` (the style-variant class) as an *ancestor*, `.wp-block-button__link` as a *descendant* — matching exactly how the storefront's own Gutenberg-authored markup nests them (`<div class="wp-block-button is-style-accent"><a class="wp-block-button__link">`). Every `assets/admin-app/views/*.js` screen instead builds `<button class="wp-block-button__link is-style-accent">` — both classes on one flat element, no wrapping `.wp-block-button` at all. A descendant selector never matches when the "ancestor" and "descendant" are the same node, so **every button in the admin app** — not just manual-order.js's, all of them — was rendering with none of its intended background, border, color, padding, or radius; only the base `.wp-block-button__link` transition rule (which does nothing visible without a background to transition) ever applied.

Compounded by the same *class* of bug `YeffoPrint_Admin_Token_Bridge` was already written to fix once before (see that class's own docblock: "Direct report: 'looks like no styling at all.'") — that class bridges theme.json's `--wp--…` custom properties into wp-admin, but only the `variables` type, never the `styles` type `wp_get_global_stylesheet()` can also generate. That second type is what would otherwise supply a Gutenberg button block's base box model (padding/radius/color) from `theme.json`'s own `styles.blocks.core/button` settings — automatically present on the front end via WordPress's own global-styles stylesheet, never present in wp-admin. So the admin app's buttons had no base box model from *any* source, on top of never matching the color-variant selectors at all.

Fixed at the source in `global.css`, not per-screen: `.wp-block-button__link` now carries a complete, self-sufficient base box model (padding, radius, color, font, border:0, cursor) restated directly rather than assumed from theme.json's generated output, and every `is-style-accent`/`is-style-outline` rule gained a second, compound-class selector form (`.wp-block-button__link.is-style-accent` alongside the existing `.wp-block-button.is-style-accent .wp-block-button__link`) so both the storefront's nested markup and the admin app's flat markup match. Purely additive on the front end — the new rules duplicate, rather than conflict with, theme.json's own numbers there.

Two smaller, related layout bugs found and fixed alongside it in `manual-order.js`, both instances of `.yp-form__row` (a fixed 2-column grid meant for label/input field pairs) being reused for something it wasn't built for: the Order Type button row (3 buttons in a 2-column grid — switched to `.yp-form__actions`, `records.css`'s flex button-group class, which also gained `flex-wrap` for narrow viewports) and the sticker Type/Shape/Quantity row (3 fields stranding the 3rd alone on its own row — added a new `.yp-form__row--three` modifier rather than force a 3-field row into a 2-column class). Also added `input[type="email"]`/`tel`/`password`/`date` to `records.css`'s `.yp-field input[...]` selector list — `type="email"`/`password` fields (this screen's new-customer email, and several existing ones on the Settings screen) were falling back to unstyled browser defaults for the same reason as the label/select/textarea siblings right next to them weren't: the selector list just never grew to cover every type attribute in use.

## Follow-up: Maintenance pricing disclosed, package card CTA alignment

Direct follow-up, same session as the hosting/quote work above: the Maintenance & Monitoring add-on had copy describing what it includes but never stated a price (unlike the new Hosting add-on, which does). Now **$35/mo**, shown on the Web Design page's own badge/modal (`web-design-packages.php`) and asked about on the new `/web-design-quote/` form (`templates/web-design-quote.html`, next to the existing maintenance question, same "($X/mo)" convention the hosting question already uses).

Direct report: "the get a quote buttons are all different sizes because each package contains a different number of items" — each package's own `FEATURES` bullet list (admin-editable per tier) is a different length, so a card's "Get a Quote" button sat right after wherever that tier's own list happened to end, rather than at a consistent position across the row. `.yp-web-design-package__cta` (`patterns.css`) now carries `margin-top: auto` — the standard flex-column "pin last child to the bottom" technique, claiming whatever vertical space is left over above itself regardless of how tall that card's own features list is. Independent of, and a firmer guarantee than, the `flex-grow` already on `.yp-web-design-package__features`.

## Manual order creation, Phase C: Template Label orders (the final phase — "am I able to make a custom order manually that also is with a proof to be approved by the customer?", broadened to "manually create any orders")

Phase A shipped Custom Design, Phase B added Custom Stickers. Phase C adds Template Label orders — a real, existing `yp_template` (the same kind customers order from directly on the storefront), not a freeform Custom Design/Sticker submission. This is the one phase that genuinely needed new architecture, not just a new order-type branch reusing existing plumbing, for one reason: **the customer-facing Template flow never has a proof-approval step at all.** `class-cart-controller.php::add()` builds a plain WooCommerce cart item straight from a Template + size + material + field_schema variants — it never touches `yp_custom_order`. Proof approval on a Template order is therefore a wholly new capability, staff-only, for the case a customer dictates label text over the phone and staff want it visually confirmed before printing rather than trusting a transcription.

**New `ORDER_TYPE` value, `'template'`** (`class-custom-order-meta.php`), with two new meta constants: `TEMPLATE_ID` (the wrapped `yp_template`) and `TEMPLATE_VARIANTS` (a JSON-encoded array of `{ quantity, values: { field_id: value } }` — the exact same shape a cart item's own `VARIANTS` already uses, validated through the exact same `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::sanitize_variants()` every other entry point into this data shape already goes through). `SIZE_ID`/`MATERIAL_ID`/`INSTRUCTIONS` are reused as-is, same as every other order type.

**A real fix to the shared snapshot function**, not just an addition: `YeffoPrint_Order_Item_Meta::apply()` previously assumed *any* line item carrying both `CUSTOM_ORDER_ID` and `TOTAL_QTY` was a Custom Design labels row (Phase A/B's own shape). A Template order requiring proof approval carries both too — but it's a real Template + field_schema/variants underneath, not a Custom Design batch row. `apply()` now checks for `TEMPLATE_ID` first to tell the two apart; the customer-facing checkout flow never sets both keys at once (a Template add-to-cart never has a `CUSTOM_ORDER_ID`; a Custom Design labels row never has a `TEMPLATE_ID`), so this only ever branches down the new path for the new manual-order case. The Template/variants branch itself now also writes `_yp_custom_order_id` when present — the same generic key `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Payment::link_paid_custom_orders()` already scans every line item for, regardless of product, so no new linking code was needed for proof-approval publishing to work.

**`find_design_fee()` fix** (`class-custom-order-payment.php`): it previously branched only on `'sticker' === order_type`, else assumed a Custom Design fee item existed to find. A Template order has no fee item either (it's priced exactly like the customer-facing flow — proof approval is a staff opt-in, not a paid design service), so it would have fallen through to record a nonexistent nominal $25 fee. Flipped the condition to `'label' !== order_type` — 'label' is the only type with a real separate fee item; every other type (sticker, now template) sums its own linked line item(s)' totals directly, a form that's automatically forward-compatible with any future non-fee order type too.

**`YeffoPrint_Manual_Order_Creator`** gains `validate_template_fields()` (published Template + size/material compatibility check, mirroring `class-cart-controller.php::add()`'s own rule, with one deliberate stricter deviation: an empty compatible-sizes/materials list means "no restriction" there, but this path still requires the chosen size/material to be a real published record, since there's no live cart product data to fall back on gating checkout the way there is on the storefront) and `add_template_row()` (Custom Stickers' single-line-item shape, not Custom Design's fee-plus-batch-rows one — one line item on the Template's own `YeffoPrint_Linked_Product`, for the combined quantity across every variant, priced with the same `YeffoPrint_Cart_Pricing::calculate_for_cart_item()` every other order type here uses, tier_quantity is this order's own combined variant quantity since there's no live cart to pool against).

**New admin endpoint** `POST /admin/manual-orders/template-pricing-preview` (`class-admin-manual-order-controller.php`) — Template Label has no public pricing-preview endpoint any more than Custom Stickers does (the storefront prices a Template batch live via the cart), so this wraps the same authoritative `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()` directly, admin-only.

**`views/manual-order.js`**'s Template Label tab reuses the exact picker-then-fetch pattern the Customer field already established: search-as-you-type against WP core's own `/wp/v2/yp_template?search=` route (the same route `views/templates.js` already uses), then on pick, fetch the *public* `GET /templates/{id}/configurator` endpoint — the exact same data the customer-facing configurator itself loads (field_schema, compatible sizes/materials) — no new data-shape invented for this screen. The variant batch table renders one column per the Template's own field_schema, with the field-type-to-input mapping (`text`→text input, `textarea`→textarea, `color`→color picker, `qr_code`→URL input) matching `assets/js/configurator.js`'s own customer-facing rendering.

**Admin Custom Orders screen** (`class-admin-custom-order-controller.php`'s `detail_payload()`, `views/orders.js`'s `renderDetail()`) gains its own `template` rendering branch — Design/Size/Material/batch-of-variants (each row's customization rendered via `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::format_variant_summary()`, the exact same summary line the cart/checkout page itself shows)/Instructions — alongside the existing sticker/label branches, plus the "Design Fee"/"Amount Paid" row label now reads "Design Fee" only for `'label'` orders (previously anything non-sticker), matching `find_design_fee()`'s own fixed reasoning above. The classic, pre-admin-app `class-custom-order-editor.php` metabox screen (hidden from the wp-admin menu since Phase 8, reachable only by a direct URL) is deliberately **not** updated for the new type — the admin app is the actual product surface now, and updating an already-superseded, hidden legacy screen for a brand-new order type wasn't judged worth the added surface area.

Verify: `#/manual-order` → Template Label tab → search and pick a published design → confirm its own compatible sizes/materials and field_schema-driven columns render → add a second batch row → confirm the live price preview matches what the storefront configurator would show for the same size/material/quantity → leave "Requires proof approval" checked → Create Order. Confirm a `processing` WC order with one Template line item (no fee item), correct `_yp_template_snapshot`/`_yp_variants` (readable by every existing downstream consumer — QR downloads, reorder, the order-screen customization display — unmodified), and a published `yp_custom_order` shell with `ORDER_TYPE = 'template'` that `#/orders` renders correctly, including a working "+ Add Proof" → customer approval link.

## Bulk pricing table on the Template configurator (direct request: "a bulk pricing table on the template order page so customers can see the savings if they order more... dynamic and show whatever tiered pricing is assigned to that template")

The request's own phrasing ("assigned to that template") doesn't match the actual data model: `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule` has exactly one active tier set site-wide (`get_active_rule_id()` — the most recently modified published `yp_pricing_rule` post), the same "one active rule" model `base_unit_price` and the custom design fee already use. There is no per-Template tier assignment anywhere in the schema, and this feature doesn't add one — it surfaces the real, existing site-wide tiers, computed against whichever size/material the customer currently has selected on that Template.

**`class-template-schema-controller.php`**'s public, unauthenticated `GET /templates/{id}/configurator` payload (already the customer-facing configurator's one source of pricing/schema data) gains two fields: `base_unit_price` (previously computed inline as a placeholder constant — now the real `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule` value, same source `/pricing/calculate` itself reads) and `tiers` (`YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::get_tiers()` verbatim — already public data, since every checkout price is computed from it). No new endpoint: this is where `configurator.js` already loads its base price and size/material adjustments from, so the tiers ride along for free instead of costing a second round-trip.

**`configurator.js`**'s `renderBulkPricingTable()` reuses the exact "instant client-side estimate, then authoritative server value" precedent the file's own pricing section already established for `renderEstimatedSummary()`/`fetchAuthoritativePricing()`: it builds one row per tier threshold (plus a baseline "1+" row) directly from `schema.base_unit_price`/`schema.tiers` and the currently selected size/material's `price_adjustment`s — the exact same inputs `YeffoPrint_Pricing_Rule::calculate()` itself takes, mirrored client-side (discount reduces base only, size/material adjustments added back at full price, same as the server formula) — and highlights whichever row the customer's current combined quantity actually qualifies for. It's called from `renderSummary()` alongside the existing estimate, so it updates live on every size/material/quantity change with no extra request. This table is purely informational: Add to Cart still only ever submits the authoritative `/pricing/calculate` result, unchanged.

**Markup**: a new pattern, `patterns/bulk-pricing-info-modal.php`, follows the exact Size/Material info-modal shape already on this page (`.yp-drawer.yp-drawer--center`, `data-yp-drawer-trigger`) but — unlike those two, which render server-side content — is a pure static shell; `configurator.js` fills `[data-yp-bulk-pricing-table]` itself, since the table has to react to client-side state (selected size/material) a PHP render can't know at page-load. The trigger button sits next to the Quantity heading (`templates/single-yp_template.html`) rather than Size/Material, since quantity is the actual axis the discount is measured against, and stays `hidden` whenever the active PricingRule has no tiers configured at all (nothing to show).

Verify: open a Template page with at least one bulk discount tier configured on the active Pricing Rule → confirm the "?" button next to Quantity is visible and opens the table with the correct per-tier price and the current-quantity row highlighted → change size/material → confirm every row's price updates → change quantity across a tier threshold → confirm the highlighted row moves → remove all tiers from the Pricing Rule → confirm the trigger button disappears.

## Fix: Material Guide photos missing for Glossy/Matte White, and a newly-added Prism photo not showing (direct report: "the matte white and glossy white don't show the pictures, I added prism as an option but it still has the placeholder instead of the one I added")

`yeffoprint_material_guide_entries()` (functions.php, §"Material guide" above) matched each of its hardcoded copy entries to a live `yp_material` record with a single `get_posts( [ 'name' => $slug ] )` query — an exact `post_name` match against a slug hardcoded here (`glossy-white`, `matte-white`, `prism`, ...). That's brittle against an easy-to-hit WordPress behavior: trashing and recreating a same-titled Material — e.g. re-uploading its swatch photo by making a fresh post rather than editing the old one — leaves the old slug reserved by the trashed post, so the replacement silently gets `-2` appended (`glossy-white` → `glossy-white-2`) without anyone touching the slug field on purpose. An exact match then finds nothing, `$photo_url` stays empty, and the entry falls back to the generic gradient "chip" placeholder — exactly the symptom reported for Glossy/Matte White, and for a freshly-added Prism record hitting the same slug collision against an earlier, now-trashed attempt.

Fixed by loading every published `yp_material` post once up front instead of querying per entry, and indexing each by both its `post_name` and its `sanitize_title()`'d `post_title`, with any trailing `-<n>` WordPress disambiguation suffix stripped from each before indexing. A hardcoded `glossy-white` entry now matches a record whether its real slug is `glossy-white`, `glossy-white-2`, or its slug has drifted from its title entirely (title still "Glossy White") — the same class of fix covers all three reported materials with one change, not three special cases. No change to `material-guide.php`/`material-info-modal.php` themselves — they already consumed this function's `photo_url`/`zoom_url` output as opaque data.

Verify: on `/how-it-works/`, confirm Glossy White, Matte White, and Prism each show their uploaded photo instead of the gradient placeholder, and click-to-enlarge opens the correct larger image for each.

## Bulk pricing table, V2: dropped the modal (direct follow-up report: "the ? doesn't work on the template page for the tiered pricing. I also always want it visible, need to find somewhere on the page for it to live")

V1 put the table behind a small "?" info-button next to Quantity that opened it in a drawer modal, hidden until `schema.tiers` was non-empty. Two things made that fragile: (1) `.yp-field__tooltip-trigger` sets `display: inline-flex` unconditionally, and an author stylesheet rule that sets `display` always wins over the browser's own `[hidden] { display: none }` UA rule regardless of selector specificity — so the button's `hidden` attribute never actually hid it, it was visible from the start whether or not tiers existed; clicking it when the active Pricing Rule had zero configured tiers opened a drawer whose body was left completely empty (`renderBulkPricingTable()` returned early before writing anything into it), which reads as "the ? doesn't work." (2) Being tucked into a modal at all meant the pricing info was invisible unless a customer thought to click a small "?" — not the "always visible" the direct request now asks for.

Fixed by removing the modal path entirely rather than patching the visibility bug in place: `patterns/bulk-pricing-info-modal.php` is deleted, and `templates/single-yp_template.html` gets a new always-rendered `.yp-configurator__section[data-yp-bulk-pricing-section]` directly below Quantity — the natural place for it to live, since quantity is the axis the discount is actually measured against. `configurator.js`'s `renderBulkPricingTable()` now unhides that section unconditionally on every render (no tiers-based hide/show), and — since "always visible" also has to hold when the active Pricing Rule genuinely has zero tiers configured — writes a plain "Bulk pricing isn't available for this design yet — check back soon." note into the table area in that case, instead of leaving it blank or hiding the section. No more click, no more modal, no more silent failure mode.

Verify: load any Template page → confirm a "Bulk Pricing" section renders directly below Quantity with no interaction needed → with at least one tier configured on the active Pricing Rule, confirm the per-tier table and current-quantity highlight render correctly and update live on size/material/quantity changes → with zero tiers configured, confirm the section still renders with the "not available yet" note rather than disappearing.

## New field_schema type: `corner_style` (direct request: "a radio selector set to specify which type of corners they want on their labels, squared or rounded... nice to use the ? here and have a modal pop up with a graphic example")

Third field type after `color`/`qr_code` (`class-field-schema.php`'s own docblock narrates each addition — this is the next paragraph there). Unlike those two, it's a fixed two-choice radio rather than free text with a format constraint, so it gets its own closed option set, `CORNER_STYLE_OPTIONS = ['squared' => 'Squared', 'rounded' => 'Rounded']` — both `sanitize()` (a field's own `default`) and `sanitize_variants()` (a customer's submitted value) constrain to those two keys, falling back to `'squared'`/blank respectively rather than accepting arbitrary text. A new `display_value( $field, $value )` helper turns a stored key back into its display label ("Squared", not "squared") — shared by `format_variant_summary()` (cart/checkout line-item text) and `class-order-item-meta.php`'s `variant_field_pairs()` (admin order screen + HTML emails), so both surfaces show the same prettified value from one place rather than each re-implementing the same key→label lookup.

**No downstream physical effect exists yet** — like every other field_schema value, a chosen corner style is captured verbatim in the order's frozen `_yp_variants`/`_yp_template_snapshot` meta and shown to staff on the order screen/email exactly like any other customization field; "carrying it through to production" today means staff reading that value off the order the same way they already read a compound name or strength. No new Size-level "corner radius" attribute or separate order meta key was added — nothing in the data model ties corner style to pricing or a physical die-cut variant, and the request didn't ask for that either.

**Rendering, not the drawer-modal pattern**: the request's "have a modal pop up with a graphic example" could have meant the section-level `data-yp-drawer-trigger` mechanism (Size/Material's own info modals) — deliberately didn't use it, for the same reason the bulk-pricing table fix above just moved away from it: `site.js`'s `initDrawers()` only scans the DOM once at `DOMContentLoaded`, but a field's own tooltip trigger is created dynamically inside `renderFieldInputStructure()` *after* the async schema fetch resolves, so a drawer trigger built there would never get wired up. Used the existing per-field tooltip mechanism instead (`.yp-field__tooltip-trigger` → `.yp-field__tooltip`, bound at render time, already working for every other field's `admin_description`) and taught it to optionally hold a graphic: `configurator.js`'s `cornerStyleDiagramHtml()` draws two small SVG rectangles — one with a corner radius, one without — same "draw it, don't describe it" precedent `size-info-modal.php` already established for scaled label-size rectangles, just built client-side since there's no per-Template data to fetch for it. The "?" trigger appears for a `corner_style` field even with no admin-authored tooltip text, unlike every other type, since the diagram is intrinsic to the type rather than admin content.

The control itself renders as two `.yp-option-pill` buttons in a `role="radiogroup"` (`cornerStyleOptionsHtml()`) — the same visual/interaction language as the Size and Material pickers on this same page, not a native `<input type="radio">`, both for consistency and because `custom-order.css`'s own `.yp-radio-option` convention isn't loaded on the Template configurator page at all. Excluded outright from the live Label View stage (`renderStage()`) regardless of the field's own `show_in_preview` setting — a corner-style choice has nowhere sensible to draw as printed text/swatch/QR the way `color`/`qr_code` do, so unlike those two this type doesn't get a stage-rendering branch at all.

**Admin editors**: `YeffoPrint_Field_Schema::TYPES` is the single registration point both the classic (`assets/admin/field-schema.js`) and admin-app (`assets/admin-app/field-schema-editor.js`) repeater widgets already read their Type `<select>` from, so `corner_style` needed no separate registration step in either. Both got one matching change: the "Default value" control swaps from a freeform text input to a `<select>` of Squared/Rounded when a row's type is `corner_style` (same swap pattern already used for qr_code's Max Characters field), sourced from a new `cornerStyleOptions` key localized alongside `types`/`alignments`/etc. in `class-admin-app.php`, `class-template-editor.php`, and `class-field-preset-editor.php`. The manual-order Template Label tab (`views/manual-order.js`'s `templateFieldInputHtml()`) got the same `<select>` treatment for its own batch-row inputs, reading from the same localized `cornerStyleOptions`.

Verify: Template editor → Customization Fields → Add Field → Type "Corner style" → confirm Default value becomes a Squared/Rounded select → set an artwork and save → open that Template on the storefront → confirm two pills render for the field, the admin's default is pre-selected, the "?" opens a tooltip with a squared-vs-rounded diagram even with no tooltip text set → switch pills → confirm the batch card summary and (if it's the first field) the variant summary label update, and the field never appears on the Label View print stage → add to cart → confirm the order screen and confirmation email show "Corner Style: Squared"/"Rounded" (not the raw lowercase key) → repeat via `#/manual-order`'s Template Label tab, confirming the same select renders in the batch table.

## Automated proof-approval reminders (direct request: a nudge for a customer who hasn't responded to a proof yet)

`YeffoPrint_Proof_Reminder_Scheduler` (`includes/custom-orders/class-proof-reminder-scheduler.php`) sends two reminders — 24h and 48h after a Custom Order enters `awaiting_approval` — then stops on its own. WP-Cron, not a per-order `wp_schedule_single_event()`: this plugin has no existing scheduling infrastructure to extend at all (no WP-Cron/Action Scheduler usage anywhere else in it), and a per-order single event would mean scheduling/rescheduling/cancelling one on every status change for every request. An hourly sweep over every Custom Order currently `awaiting_approval` is simpler and self-healing (a missed sweep just catches up on the next run) — the up-to-~1h fuzziness against a 24h/48h threshold doesn't matter in practice. `ensure_scheduled()` runs on every `init`, not just plugin activation, same reasoning this plugin's rewrite-flush flag already uses: a deploy that skips WP's real activation hook (FTP/file-manager upload, git sync, staging→production push) would otherwise never schedule this at all; `wp_schedule_event()` is a no-op once the event's already on the cron table, so the check is cheap. A new `register_deactivation_hook()` (`yeffoprint-core.php`, alongside the existing activation hook) unschedules it — nothing else in this plugin needed one before.

**Two new `YeffoPrint_Custom_Order_Meta` fields** carry the timing state a reminder job needs, neither of which existed before: `AWAITING_APPROVAL_AT` (a real Unix timestamp, since a plain `update_post_meta()` call never touches `post_modified` — there was no "status changed at" signal anywhere in this data model until now) and `PROOF_REMINDER_STAGE` (0/1/2 — which reminder, if any, has already gone out). Both are set in `YeffoPrint_Proof_Meta::advance_status_to_awaiting_approval()` — the exact moment status enters `awaiting_approval` — and both reset to a fresh state every time that happens again (a "Request changes" round trip followed by a new proof upload), so the 24h/48h clock restarts against the *new* proof, not the first one.

**No explicit "cancel a reminder" logic exists anywhere, and none was needed**: the sweep only ever queries Custom Orders whose `STATUS` is still `awaiting_approval` (`class-proof-reminder-scheduler.php`'s `sweep()`), and approving or requesting changes (`class-proof-approval-controller.php`) moves status away from that value. The order simply stops showing up in the next sweep — nothing needed to know a customer had responded.

**Two channels, per direct request** — email always, Telegram if the order already has a linked chat:
- `YeffoPrint_Proof_Meta::send_reminder_email()` — same "best-effort, silently bail on a missing/invalid email" shape as the existing `notify_customer()` proof-ready email right above it in the same file, reusing the same `yeffoprint_core_proof_approval_url()` link, just with reminder-specific subject/copy that gets more urgent at the 48h stage.
- `YeffoPrint_Telegram_Order_Notifications::on_reminder_due()` — a new listener on a new `yeffoprint_proof_reminder_due` action (fired by the scheduler, same "fires regardless of whether the email actually sent" reasoning as `yeffoprint_proof_ready_for_review`), reusing that class's existing `notify_order()`/`chat_ids()` machinery. A no-op for any order nobody's linked a Telegram chat to yet (the existing implicit "look up your order via the bot" opt-in, unchanged) — there's no separate account-linking concept for this to depend on.

**Staff visibility**: the admin-app Custom Orders detail screen (`class-admin-custom-order-controller.php`'s `detail_payload()`, `views/orders.js`'s `renderDetail()`) now shows "No reminder sent yet" / "Reminder sent (24h)" / "Reminder sent (24h + 48h)" plus how long it's been waiting, directly under the Proofs panel — only while status is actually `awaiting_approval`, since the stage number is stale/meaningless once it's moved on.

Verify: upload a proof to push a Custom Order to `awaiting_approval` → confirm `AWAITING_APPROVAL_AT`/`PROOF_REMINDER_STAGE` are set (`0`) → manually back-date `AWAITING_APPROVAL_AT` by 25 hours (or wait) → trigger `do_action('yeffoprint_proof_reminder_sweep')` (or wait for the hourly cron) → confirm a reminder email sends, `PROOF_REMINDER_STAGE` becomes `1`, a linked Telegram chat gets pinged, and the admin-app order screen shows "Reminder sent (24h)" → repeat past 48h for stage 2 → approve the proof → confirm no further reminders send even past 48h from a since-superseded timestamp.

## Telegram account linking + bot-driven proof approve/reject (direct request: "how would a customer link the bot to their account for order updates... can it also be an option to setup through the registration flow... how great would it be if customers can view/approve/reject proofs right from the telegram bot")

Three pieces, building on the proof-reminder work above.

**Account linking is a new, separate concept from the existing per-*order* chat_id list.** `class-telegram-order-notifications.php`'s `_yp_telegram_chat_ids` (order meta) opts a chat in implicitly the first time it proves knowledge of one order number + checkout email — it has never had any relationship to a WP account, and still doesn't. `YeffoPrint_Telegram_Account_Link` (`includes/telegram/class-telegram-account-link.php`) is new: a single `_yp_telegram_chat_id` on the WP user itself, so a customer connects once, ever, rather than re-proving order+email per order in the bot. The link flow mirrors `class-social-login.php`'s own OAuth `state` pattern almost exactly — a short 8-character code, generated server-side (My Account's new "Connect Telegram" tab), stashed in a transient keyed by the code with the WP user_id as payload, 15-minute TTL, consumed once (win or lose) the moment the bot receives it. A customer completes it either by tapping a `t.me/<bot>?start=link_<code>` deep-link button (Telegram delivers `/start link_<code>` as the chat's first message automatically once they hit Telegram's own "Start") or by typing `/link CODE` themselves — both routed through the same `link_account_reply()` in `class-telegram-message-handler.php`. The bot's username for that deep link is resolved via a new `Telegram_Client::get_me()` call, cached 24h (5 min on failure) since it's fixed per bot and not worth a live API call on every tab view.

**Registration-flow opt-in, without a form field that can't actually work yet.** A checkbox on WooCommerce's own (untouched) classic Register form can't pre-generate a working code — there's no user_id to attach one to before the account exists. Instead, `class-account-endpoints.php` hooks `user_register` — which fires for every path that creates a WP user on this site (the classic Register form, `class-social-login.php`'s first-time signup, and the admin's manual-order-creator, since all three call `wc_create_new_customer()` internally) — to flag the fresh account in a transient. `woocommerce_before_account_dashboard` (already a real hook in the theme's `myaccount/dashboard.php` override) renders a one-time "Connect Telegram" banner on that account's very next Dashboard view — the natural landing spot after registration — then clears the flag immediately so it truly only shows once. A permanent "Connect Telegram" quicklink was also added to the Dashboard's existing quicklink grid for ongoing discoverability beyond that first prompt.

**Bot-driven proof approve/reject is gated to account-linked ownership, deliberately stronger than the order-scoped chat list.** `class-proof-approval-controller.php`'s `approve()`/`request_changes()` previously did their guard/meta-write/notify entirely inline — extracted into new `approve_custom_order()`/`reject_custom_order()` static methods so a non-REST caller (the bot) can trigger the identical domain action. A tapped button only ever works for a chat that is BOTH account-linked AND owns that specific Custom Order (`CUSTOMER_ID` meta match) — the exact same ownership rule `check_access()` already applies to a logged-in web session — checked in new `class-telegram-callback-handler.php`'s `chat_owns_order()`. The weaker order-scoped chat list (opts in from a bare order-number + email guess) was judged too weak a trust bar for a *mutating* action, even though it's fine for read-only status pings; a guest customer with no account keeps their original guest-token proof-approval link untouched — this is a new convenience layered on for a connected account, nothing taken away.

Mechanically: `class-telegram-order-notifications.php`'s `notify_order()` now attaches an inline `[✅ Approve] [✏️ Request changes]` keyboard (new `reply_markup` support on `Telegram_Client::send_message()`) to a proof-ready or reminder message, per-chat, only when that chat passes the ownership check above. A tapped "Approve" resolves immediately. A tapped "Request changes" can't resolve on the tap alone — Telegram has no free-text input on an inline keyboard — so it stores a `class-telegram-callback-handler.php` pending-reject transient (10 min TTL, same shape as `class-telegram-escalation.php`'s own pending state, but with the payload arriving on the *next* message rather than before confirmation) and asks the customer to reply with their notes; `class-telegram-message-handler.php` checks for that pending state before any normal command routing, exactly like the existing escalation check, and applies the reject once the notes arrive.

Two new pieces of Telegram plumbing this required: `Telegram_Client::answer_callback_query()` (Telegram requires acknowledging every button tap or the client shows a stuck loading spinner) and `callback_query` added to `setWebhook`'s `allowed_updates` (previously `['message']` only — Telegram silently never delivers a button tap otherwise). That second change needed `class-telegram-webhook-sync.php` to re-register the webhook once after this deploy even without an admin re-saving Settings — a new `WEBHOOK_CONFIG_VERSION` constant + option, checked on every `init`, mirrors `class-yeffoprint-core.php`'s own rewrite-flush version-check pattern exactly (a plain "sync on option save" hook, unlike an activation hook, never fires for a code-only deploy).

Verify: My Account → Connect Telegram → confirm a code + working deep-link button render → complete the link via the bot → confirm the tab now shows "Connected" with a Disconnect action → register a brand-new account → confirm the one-time banner appears on the first Dashboard view and not again after → get a proof to `awaiting_approval` on a Custom Order owned by the linked account → confirm the Telegram message includes Approve/Request changes buttons → tap Approve → confirm the order moves to `approved` and the admin gets notified → repeat and tap Request changes → reply with notes → confirm the order moves back to `design_in_progress` with `CHANGE_REQUEST_NOTES` set → confirm a chat that only ever did a plain order+email lookup (never account-linked) gets the same proof-ready text with no buttons attached.

## Follow-up: the actual proof image now sends through Telegram (direct report: "does a copy of the proof get sent through telegram so they can see it or will they have to login" — it didn't; buttons let a customer approve/reject a proof they'd never actually seen)

`Telegram_Client` gains `send_photo()`/`send_document()` — Telegram fetches the file itself from a URL passed in the `photo`/`document` param (no upload/multipart handling needed on this end), which is exactly what `wp_get_attachment_url()` already returns for a proof file; that URL was already being handed straight to a browser by `class-proof-approval-controller.php`'s own `get_proof()` endpoint with no signing/auth of its own, so sending the same URL to Telegram is the same trust model, not a new exposure. Both methods take the same `caption`/`$inline_keyboard` shape `send_message()` already established, so the Approve/Reject buttons attach to the photo message itself rather than a separate one.

`class-telegram-order-notifications.php`'s `notify_order()` now resolves the Custom Order's most recent proof file (`get_for_custom_order()` already returns newest-first — the current one actually awaiting this round of approval, not a superseded earlier attempt from before a "Request changes" cycle) via new `latest_proof_media()`, and sends it as a photo or, for a non-image proof file (a PDF, say — `wp_attachment_is_image()` decides), a document, to **every** linked chat this notification goes to — not just the button-eligible, account-linked one, since seeing the proof is useful regardless of whether that particular chat can also act on it. Falls back to the original plain text+link message whenever there's no proof file yet, or `send_photo()`/`send_document()` itself reports failure for any reason — a customer is never left with nothing.

Verify: get a proof to `awaiting_approval` → confirm the Telegram message a linked chat receives now shows the actual proof image (or a document attachment for a non-image proof file) with the caption/buttons on the same message, not a separate text message → confirm a chat linked to an order with no proof file yet (shouldn't normally happen, but) still gets the plain text fallback cleanly.

## Telegram: find a design by name/keyword (direct report: asking the bot "help me find the labs label" got escalated to a human instead of just finding it)

New `YeffoPrint_Telegram_Template_Search` (`includes/telegram/class-telegram-template-search.php`) is a plain `get_posts( [ 'post_type' => 'yp_template', 'post_status' => 'publish', 's' => $keywords ] )` — reusing the exact same search WordPress core already runs for the site's own header search box (`assets/js/search.js`) and the admin manual-order Template picker, not a bespoke search implementation. `class-template-search.php`'s existing hooks (title plus `yp_style`/`yp_color`/`yp_material_tag` taxonomy terms flattened into a `_yp_search_index` meta value) widen that query automatically for any `yp_template` search, including this one, with zero new code needed for that part.

**The actual bug wasn't "no search exists" — it's that WordPress's own search requires every word in the query to appear somewhere in the post, and a natural sentence never will.** "Help me find the labs label" fails to match a Template titled "Labs" outright, since "help"/"me"/"find"/"the"/"label" don't appear in that title at all — only "labs" does. `Template_Search::reply()` strips a generic filler/stopword list (articles, pronouns, "help"/"find"/"looking for"/"label"/"design"/etc.) before searching, so the meaningful word(s) — "labs" — are what actually gets searched on. This isn't a fix scoped to that one example; the stopword list is generic wording, not a hardcoded phrase match, so it holds up for however else a customer phrases the same request.

Wired into `class-telegram-message-handler.php` at two points: an explicit `/search`/`/find <query>` command (alongside `/order`/`/reorder`, no ambiguity — command-token branches all return early before any free-text logic runs), and — the one that actually fixes the reported conversation — a last-resort attempt on free text, checked *after* `YeffoPrint_Telegram_Faq::match()` (so a real FAQ question like "what materials do you offer" still gets its FAQ answer, unaffected) but *before* the escalation fallback. It only ever replaces escalation when the search actually turns up a match — anything that isn't design-related (which is most FAQ/support text) naturally returns zero hits after stopword-stripping and falls straight through to escalation exactly as before, so this can't accidentally intercept a genuine "I need a human" conversation.

Verify: message the bot free text like "can you help me find the labs label" (with a Template actually titled/tagged to match "labs" published on the site) → confirm it returns that Template's title + link instead of the escalation offer → try `/search <keyword>` directly → confirm the same → try a query with no matches → confirm `/search` says so with a gallery link, while the same free-text query (not prefixed with `/search`) still falls through to escalation as before → ask a real FAQ question (e.g. "what sizes do you offer") → confirm it still gets the FAQ answer, not swallowed by the new search step.

## "Log in with Telegram" (direct request: "the bot has an option to allow users to login to the site using their telegram account, can we build that?")

New `YeffoPrint_Telegram_Login` (`includes/telegram/class-telegram-login.php`) embeds Telegram's own official Login Widget on the storefront login form. This is *not* built as a fourth provider inside `class-social-login.php` — that class's whole shape (provider_config()/exchange_tokens()/state param/redirect-out-then-callback) is OAuth 2.0 Authorization Code flow, and Telegram's widget is structurally different: there's no client_id/secret and no server-initiated redirect-out. The `<script src="https://telegram.org/js/telegram-widget.js">` tag embedded on the page *is* the button; tapping it opens Telegram itself, and on success Telegram redirects the browser straight to a `data-auth-url` with signed identity (`id, first_name, last_name, username, photo_url, auth_date, hash`) as query params — no separate userinfo endpoint, no token exchange. Given how different the mechanics actually are, this gets its own class (same "one class per concern" precedent already used throughout the Telegram bot integration) rather than force-fitting it into the OAuth abstraction — while still mirroring that class's externally-visible behavior as closely as it can: same `woocommerce_login_form_end`/`login_form` hook points, same `log_in()` shape, same fallback-injection resilience pattern for a login template that doesn't fire those hooks.

**Verification is Telegram's own documented HMAC-SHA256 algorithm**, not anything invented here: every received field except `hash`, sorted by key, joined `key=value\n...`, HMAC'd with `SHA256(bot_token)` as the raw-bytes key, compared to the received `hash` via `hash_equals()`. Computed over `wp_unslash()`-only values, never `sanitize_text_field()`'d ones — sanitizing first would alter characters and silently break a legitimate request's hash match; sanitizing happens only afterward, once verification has already succeeded. Two replay defenses, since the redirect-mode widget's callback URL (unlike its JS-callback mode) can sit in browser history/server logs: an `auth_date` freshness check (rejected past `DAY_IN_SECONDS`) and a one-time-use transient keyed on the hash itself, so even a fresh-enough replayed URL is refused the second time. The one external setup step, called out in the Settings field's own description: the bot's domain has to be authorized for the widget via @BotFather's `/setdomain`, or Telegram refuses to render/authenticate it — same category of one-time step as registering a redirect URI in an OAuth console.

**No new identity concept for a returning customer.** Telegram's numeric Login-Widget `id` is the exact same identifier space as a private chat's `chat_id`, already stored as `_yp_telegram_chat_id` by the account-linking feature above — `handle_callback()` resolves a returning sign-in via that same `get_user_id_for_chat()` reverse lookup, zero new mapping needed.

**Telegram never provides an email — the one genuine gap with no OAuth-provider equivalent.** *(Superseded by the follow-up below — a brand-new sign-in no longer gets an account, placeholder or otherwise, until a real email is actually submitted.)*

**Reliability**: `enqueue_fallback_script()`/`telegram-login-inject.js` replicate the exact fallback-injection mechanism `class-social-login.php`'s own docblock documents as a real, previously-hit production bug (a login template that doesn't fire `woocommerce_login_form_end`) — with one addition specific to this widget: a `<script>` tag inserted via `insertAdjacentHTML`/`innerHTML` never actually executes (deliberate browser behavior), so unlike the plain link/button the other fallback injects, this one recreates the script element via `document.createElement()` (copying its attributes across) and swaps it in, which does execute.

Verify: Settings → Telegram Bot → enable "Log in with Telegram" (bot token already configured, domain authorized via BotFather `/setdomain`) → load the My Account login form and wp-login.php → confirm the widget renders on both → log in with a Telegram account already linked via the My Account "Connect Telegram" tab → confirm it resolves straight to that existing account → replay an already-used callback URL → confirm it's rejected. (Brand-new sign-in verification moved to the follow-up below, which replaced that half of the flow.)

### Follow-up: a brand-new sign-in asks for a real email before an account is created

Direct request: "change the telegram login to make new users enter an email before continuing." The original approach above created the account immediately with a placeholder `.invalid` address and nagged/gated the customer into replacing it afterward; this inverts that — no account, and no `wc_create_new_customer()` call, happens until a real, available email is actually in hand. `PLACEHOLDER_EMAIL_META` and everything that reacted to it are gone entirely (`YeffoPrint_Telegram_Account_Link::placeholder_email()`/`mark_placeholder_email()`/`has_placeholder_email()`/`clear_placeholder_email_flag()`, and `class-account-endpoints.php`'s banner/checkout-gate/`profile_update` auto-clear trio) — with sign-up itself now gated on a real email, nothing downstream ever needs to detect or react to a fake one.

`handle_callback()` still resolves a returning identity exactly as before (the `chat_id`↔`user_id` reverse lookup, no email involved). For a Telegram id with no linked account, it now stashes the verified identity (`telegram_id`/`first_name`/`last_name`/`redirect_to`) in a new short-lived `PENDING_SIGNUP_TRANSIENT_PREFIX` transient — same 15-minute-window, consumed-once shape as `class-telegram-account-link.php`'s own `/link` code transient — and redirects to a new one-field step, `handle_email_step()`, reached at `wp-login.php?action=yp_telegram_email&t=<token>`. That's a real WordPress mechanism, not a bespoke page: `login_form_{action}` is the same hook core's own lostpassword/register actions use to add a custom step to wp-login.php, so this inherits that page's existing branding (`login.css`, the brand lockup, `login_header()`/`login_footer()`) for free rather than needing its own template/page/CPT. A submitted email is `is_email()`-validated and checked against `email_exists()` — refused (with the pending signup left intact so the same link can be retried) if invalid or already registered to someone else, since silently logging into a different existing account on an email guess would be a account-takeover path, not a convenience. Only once a real, available email clears both checks does `wc_create_new_customer()` run — one time, right there — followed by the same `link()` + `log_in()` calls the returning-user path already used.

Verify: sign in with a Telegram account that has never been linked to any account → confirm no account is created yet and the browser lands on the "one more step" email page (styled like the rest of wp-login.php) → submit an invalid address → confirm an inline error and no account created → submit an address already registered to a different account → confirm the "already registered" error and no account created → submit a real, unused address → confirm the account is created with that real email (not a placeholder), Telegram is linked, the customer is logged in, and they land back on the page they started from (or My Account for a plain login-page sign-in, or Checkout for a sign-in that started there) → sign out and repeat "Log in with Telegram" with the same account → confirm it logs straight into the same account with no email step shown a second time → let the pending-signup link sit past 15 minutes before submitting → confirm it shows "that sign-up link has expired" rather than creating an account off a stale identity.

### Follow-up: repositioned below Google/Discord/Apple on wp-login.php, divider label removed

Direct report, with a screenshot: the Telegram widget rendered right after the password field, above Remember Me/Log In, reading as disconnected from the Google/Discord/Apple button group that sits below the submit button. Same root cause `class-social-login.php`'s own docblock already documents for that exact problem: `login_form` fires at a fixed DOM position core controls (right after the password field) — no hook priority moves that, and no later hook exists while still inside `#loginform`. That class's own fix was a CSS `order` rule on `#loginform` (a flex column) pinning `.yp-social-login` to `order: 10`; the Telegram widget, added afterward, never got the same treatment and just sat at its natural DOM position instead.

`login.css` gets a matching `.yp-telegram-login { order: 11; }` — one step past the social buttons, so it now renders directly under them rather than above the form fields. Margin is intentionally smaller than `.yp-social-login`'s own section-opening margin (`0.6rem`, matching `.yp-social-login__buttons`' own button-to-button gap) — direct request: "move the telegram to be under the others," which reads as wanting it treated as one more item in that same list, not a second section needing its own visual break.

The "or log in with Telegram" divider label above the widget (direct request: "remove the text") is gone from `widget_html()` entirely — it's the one shared render both `render_login_widget()` (My Account) and `render_wp_login_widget()` (wp-login.php) call, so this applies to both surfaces, not just the one in the report. Removing it is also what makes "reads as part of the same list" actually true visually on wp-login.php: a widget with no label of its own, sitting directly under three labeled buttons, reads as fourth continuation rather than an unrelated block that happens to be nearby.

Verify: load wp-login.php → confirm the Telegram widget now renders directly beneath the Google/Discord/Apple buttons, not between the password field and Remember Me, with no "or log in with Telegram" text above it → load My Account's login form (a fresh private window, logged out) → confirm the same missing-label change there too, with the widget still positioned where `woocommerce_login_form_end` already put it.

### Follow-up: the button itself now matches Google/Discord/Apple's size and shape

Direct request: "resized and shaped to match the other two social login buttons." Positioning it correctly (the follow-up above) couldn't fix this part — the widget was still Telegram's own auto-rendering `<script data-telegram-login=...>` tag, which draws its own iframe with Telegram's own fixed branding; `data-size`/`data-radius` were the only knobs it ever exposed, and neither gets anywhere near `.yp-social-login__button`'s actual look, since Telegram controls everything drawn inside that iframe.

`widget_html()` switches to Telegram's *low-level* Login API instead — the officially documented alternative for exactly this case, not a workaround: load the *bare* widget script (`https://telegram.org/js/telegram-widget.js?22`, no `data-telegram-login` attribute, so nothing auto-renders), build a fully custom button, and call `Telegram.Login.auth({ bot_id, request_access: 'write' }, callback)` on click. Telegram opens its own popup/app for the actual authentication and calls back with the identical signed fields (`id, first_name, last_name, username, photo_url, auth_date, hash`) the redirect-mode widget used to put directly in the URL — the click handler just puts them there itself (`window.location.href = callback_url() + '?' + serialize(user)`) and navigates, so `verify_and_get_data()` and everything past it needed zero changes: same payload shape, same HMAC-SHA256, same replay protection, just a different, still-Telegram-documented way of arriving at that callback.

The button itself is a real `<button class="yp-social-login__button yp-social-login__button--telegram">` — reusing `class-social-login.php`'s own shared button class directly, the same border/background/padding/font every Google/Discord/Apple button already gets, plus a hand-authored 18×18 Telegram brand-mark icon (circle + paper plane) matching that class's own `render_icon()` convention (fixed glyphs, no external icon font/CDN). Two small resets in `login.css` are the only new CSS needed: `width: 100%` (the `<a>` tags get this for free from their flex container's default `stretch`; this button has no such parent) and `cursor: pointer`.

**`Telegram.Login.auth()` needs the bot's numeric ID**, not its `@username` the way the old `data-telegram-login` attribute did — `bot_id()` derives it from the leading digits of the already-configured bot token (before the `:`), no new setting or API call needed; not a secret, same public-knowledge category as an OAuth `client_id`.

**The fallback-injection path needed a real fix, not just an update**: `widget_html()` now emits *two* `<script>` tags (the bare widget loader, and this class's own click handler) instead of one, and a `<script>` built via `insertAdjacentHTML`/`innerHTML` never executes regardless of which of the two it is. `telegram-login-inject.js` no longer targets one specific script by selector; it builds the injected markup in a detached wrapper first, recreates *every* `<script>` found inside it via `document.createElement()` (copying both attributes and inline text across), and only then moves the wrapper's children into the real form — both scripts execute correctly this way, whichever fallback path (server-rendered vs. injected) actually delivers the button on a given page.

Verify: load wp-login.php and My Account's login form → confirm the Telegram button now matches Google/Discord/Apple's exact width, height, border, and font, with its own paper-plane icon → click it → confirm Telegram's own popup/app opens (not an inline iframe) → authorize → confirm it redirects back through the existing callback flow exactly as before (a returning identity logs straight in; a brand-new one lands on the "one more step" email page) → decline/close the popup without authorizing → confirm nothing happens (no error, no partial state) → on an install where the fallback-injection path is the one actually delivering the button (simulate by testing on the My Account page, its own resilience case), confirm both clicking the button and the resulting Telegram popup still work identically.

## Rebrand: YeffoPrint → YeffoDesign, customer-facing surfaces only

Direct request, with a new logo file: same CMYK three-bar mark (cyan/magenta/yellow — matches this site's existing brand colors exactly, `theme.json`'s `cyan`/`magenta`/`yellow` presets), new wordmark "YeffoDesign" in two weights (heavy "Yeffo", light "Design"), new tagline "Designed to stand out." Scoped to what customers actually see, per the request — internal naming (the `yeffoprint`/`yeffoprint-core` package slugs, class names, CSS `yp-` prefixes, the admin menu's "YeffoPrint" label, `style.css`'s Theme Name, this doc's own title) is untouched.

**The brand mark got a small fidelity pass alongside the text change**: the three bars used to butt together with no gap inside the rounded-square clip path; the new logo shows them as separated bars within that same silhouette, so each bar's width dropped from 7.3 to 6.63 units with a 1-unit gap between (same total span, same clip path — the gaps just reveal the page background rather than needing new markup). Applied identically everywhere the mark appears: `parts/header.html`, `parts/footer.html`, and `functions.php`'s `login_header` lockup (three separate copies, since block template parts are static HTML with no shared include mechanism, and the login page is real PHP reached through a different hook entirely).

**The wordmark's two-weight look**: `.yp-brand-lockup__word` (`global.css`) dropped from a flat `font-weight: 800` to `500`, with a new `.yp-brand-lockup__word strong { font-weight: 800; }` for the "Yeffo" portion — `login.css`'s own copy of that selector (the login page ships separately since WordPress never loads the theme's normal stylesheet there) got the identical treatment. The header/footer instances just hardcode `<strong>Yeffo</strong>Design`, same as they already hardcoded the old plain word. The login lockup is the one genuinely dynamic copy (`get_bloginfo( 'name' )`, both for the visible word and its `aria-label`) — rather than hardcoding it a third time, it now splits whatever the actual site title is into a bold/light pair *when that title starts with "Yeffo"*, and falls back to the plain site title at a single weight otherwise. That fallback matters during the transition: Site Title (Settings → General) is a database option this codebase doesn't manage, so until an admin updates it by hand the login page still shows whatever's currently configured, correctly, just without the two-weight split.

**Everything else was a literal string swap** in every genuinely customer-facing spot that said "YeffoPrint": the footer copyright line, the label configurator's browser-tab title suffix (`configurator.js`), the Rewards section heading and an empty-state message on the public Materials page (`patterns/`), the splash screen's "We've Upgraded" copy and image alt text, and the Telegram bot's own `/start` greeting (the one bot string a customer directly reads, as opposed to the many admin-facing "Dashboard → YeffoPrint → Settings" doc-comments left alone). Two lookalikes were deliberately **not** touched despite matching the search: the Venmo gateway's settings-field label ("e.g. @YeffoPrint") and the contact-form notification email's subject line — both render only in wp-admin/the owner's own inbox, never in front of a customer, so they're outside this pass's stated scope the same way the admin menu label is.

**New tagline placement**: added once, as a small italic line under the footer's brand lockup (`.yp-brand-lockup__tagline`, `global.css`) — the header stays compact (no tagline there, matching how a header logo lockup normally behaves) and the footer already had a one-line brand statement slot, so the new tagline sits alongside it rather than replacing the existing "Premium custom vial labels..." description, which still carries real product information the tagline doesn't.

**What this pass could not do, since it's outside version control**: the actual Site Title/Tagline (Settings → General — everything reading `get_bloginfo()` dynamically, browser tab titles, RSS, the login-page wordmark's live source, WooCommerce order emails' header/footer) and the Site Icon/favicon (Appearance → Site Editor, an uploaded media attachment, not a theme.json value) both need a manual update in wp-admin; this pass only guaranteed the code stops fighting that change once it's made.

Verify: update Settings → General → Site Title to "YeffoDesign" → confirm the login-page wordmark switches to the two-weight "**Yeffo**Design" treatment, browser tab titles and WooCommerce order emails follow automatically → load the homepage, footer, and header → confirm the wordmark, tagline, and copyright line all read correctly and the brand mark shows visibly separated bars → open the label configurator → confirm the browser tab title ends in "— YeffoDesign" → message the Telegram bot `/start` → confirm the greeting says "YeffoDesign" → view the Rewards section and (with Materials temporarily empty) the Materials page's empty state → confirm both read "YeffoDesign".

### Follow-up: a 13th Homepage Promo theme for the Web Design launch (direct request: "design one and add it to the list for me to select announcing the new web design service")

`YeffoPrint_Promo_Themes::all()` gets a `web-design-launch` entry alongside the 12 seasonal ones — same picker (Settings → Homepage Promo, both the classic page and admin-app), same required offer-text/code fields, same press-proof visual device, so it needed zero new admin UI: `render_promo_theme_field()`'s `<select>` and the admin-app's own dropdown both already loop `YeffoPrint_Promo_Themes::all()` generically. Deliberately not seasonal: rather than recoloring away from the brand the way every existing theme does on purpose, `bars` uses the actual brand CMY colors (`#00AEEF`/`#EC008C`/`#FFF200`) — this banner *is* the brand, announcing a new offering, not a seasonal reskin of it. Icon is a simple browser-window mockup (title bar dots + a mock hero block), matching the stroke-based style every other theme's icon already uses.

**One real functional fix alongside the content addition**: `render.php`'s CTA button was hardcoded everywhere — label "Shop the Sale", link `/shop-labels/` — which would have sent a web-design lead straight into the label shop instead of `/web-design/`. Added optional `cta_label`/`cta_url` keys a theme can set (`web-design-launch` sets both, pointing at `/web-design/`); every seasonal theme omits them and falls back to the exact same hardcoded default as before, so this is additive with zero behavior change for the other 12.

Verify: Settings → Homepage Promo → select "Web Design Launch", fill in an offer (e.g. "15% off your first project") and a code, enable, save → confirm the homepage banner renders in the brand's own CMY colors with the browser-window icon → confirm its CTA reads "Explore Web Design" and links to `/web-design/`, not `/shop-labels/` → switch back to any seasonal theme (e.g. "Summer") → confirm its CTA still reads "Shop the Sale" and links to `/shop-labels/`, unchanged.

### Follow-up: multiple active promo banners, rotating (direct request: "select more than one active promo banner and have it slide through the active ones on the site")

The single-banner model (one theme, one offer, one code, all in separate options) is replaced by one option, `PROMO_BANNERS_OPTION` — a map of theme slug → `{offer, code}` — rather than adding a second parallel "which ones are active" list: a theme is active purely by having both fields filled in for it, the exact same gate the single-banner version already used (`'' === $code || '' === $offer` → don't show it), just applied per theme instead of once globally. `PROMO_ENABLED_OPTION` is still the master switch on top of that, unchanged.
